## Product

### Core goal

Let learners who already study Japanese elsewhere (a kanji/vocab SRS such as WaniKani, a grammar SRS such as Bunpro, a textbook, a tutor) use Crunchyroll anime as real immersion practice, with just enough support to confirm what they half-know without breaking the scene. **The extension's job is to keep them watching, not to teach them.**

This is the north star, not a feature list: every scope decision traces back to it through the [litmus test](#litmus-test). `CLAUDE.md` carries a condensed copy of this goal and the full litmus test — if either changes here, update it there too.

### What it is

A Chrome extension that adds clickable, learner-friendly Japanese subtitles to Crunchyroll. Click or hover a word mid-episode → fast dictionary check. Capture a word or sentence → send it to Anki. Everything else is secondary to that loop.

**The premise, verified:** Crunchyroll has no native Japanese subtitle track in any form. Its own support documentation lists official subtitle/CC support only in Arabic, English, French, German, Portuguese and Spanish (verified 2026-06-23). The extension fills a real, confirmed gap rather than duplicating a platform feature.

### Target user

**Not a beginner looking for their first grammar explanation.** Someone who already has a study system — WaniKani or similar for kanji/vocab SRS, Bunpro or similar for grammar SRS, maybe a textbook or tutor — and uses anime as immersion time: the place they meet studied material in the wild, build real reading/listening speed, and confirm or correct what they think they already know.

For this user, immersion time has one job: exposure and confirmation, fast, without breaking the flow of watching. It is **not** tutoring time. They already have a trusted resource for grammar instruction; an AI explanation competing with it is a worse experience than no explanation. A typical session is mostly watching, with a click only when a word stops them — recognized-but-unsure, or an inflection that threw them — then a two-second confirmation and back to the show.

**What they want, in priority order:**
1. Japanese subtitles to exist at all on Crunchyroll, synced well enough not to be annoying
2. A fast way to check a word without leaving the player
3. A way to send anything that caught their attention into the SRS they already use and trust (Anki) — not a second, competing review system

**What they don't want:**
- To be taught grammar mid-episode by an AI (duplicates and competes with their Bunpro-equivalent)
- A built-in flashcard/quiz system duplicating Anki, where they already have months of review history

### Positioning

Three pillars, not independent — the first causes the other two:
1. **Lean by design, not by compromise.** No backend, no AI API calls. Originally a cost/architecture decision, it doubles as the product's philosophy: nothing stands between the learner and the dictionary — the right default for a user who already has a more rigorous resource for grammar instruction.
2. **Structurally cheaper to run.** No per-user AI-API or server cost, which leaves room to compete on price or reinvest in the product, without the margin pressure competitors running AI/server infrastructure carry.
3. **Depth on one platform, not breadth across many.** ManabiDojo spans Crunchyroll, Netflix, YouTube, manga and the general web. We go deep on Crunchyroll-specific problems (forced-subtitle coexistence, fansub drift, anime-specific vocab) instead of spreading thin.

Pricing model: subscription + lifetime purchase (Decisions → Product & scope, 2026-06-30). Price points are an open question.

## Scope

### Litmus test

**Does it help the user capture something quickly and keep watching, or feed something into a system they already use (e.g. Anki)? Or does it ask them to stop and be taught, or duplicate a tool they already trust (e.g. Anki, or their existing grammar/SRS tools)?** First answer → in scope. Second answer → out, regardless of whether a competitor (ManabiDojo or anyone else) has it. This test is the tie-breaker whenever something is ambiguous. It overrides the Scope lists in `project-plan.md`, not the other way around.

This is the canonical copy; `CLAUDE.md` repeats the paragraph above word for word.

### In scope — core loop

- [x] **Crunchyroll only** — no Netflix, no YouTube, no manga.
- [x] **Universal subtitle import from Jimaku**, with automatic show/episode detection from the Crunchyroll page — not a user-facing search box and not a curated list. (Clarified 2026-07-05: the original "search by show" wording only ever meant "not a curated list".) → Show & episode detection; Jimaku entry resolution
- [x] **Manual subtitle upload fallback (.srt/.ass)** for anything not on Jimaku — also the answer to shows with zero Jimaku subtitles. Built 2026-07-06. → Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload
- [x] **A synced Japanese subtitle overlay on the Crunchyroll player** — the core premise and the target user's #1 priority. → Subtitle overlay, sync & offset
- [x] **Offset/sync adjustment (hotkeys + manual nudge)** — a must-have, since community subtitles often drift. → Subtitle overlay, sync & offset
- [x] **Word-level segmentation** (morphological analysis). → Tokenizing & grouping
- [x] **Click/hover a word → popup with definition + reading** (JMdict). → Popup display; Dictionary data & lookup
- [x] **Anki export** — capture a clicked word or sentence and send it to the user's existing Anki through AnkiConnect (decided 2026-07-02 over offline .apkg/CSV). Moved from the differentiation layer into the core loop on 2026-07-02, because it *is* the "keep watching" loop's endpoint. The sentence-translation and audio fields shipped in Phase 5; the screenshot field is out of scope (below). → the three Anki export components
  - Opt-in metadata on the popup and card (POS, frequency tier, JLPT level, show/episode source), all off by default. JLPT here is a narrow 2026-07-02 exception to the original JLPT exclusion — see Out of scope.
- [x] **English subtitle display** below the Japanese line, taken from Crunchyroll's own caption file rather than a second Jimaku fetch (decided 2026-07-23). Moved from the differentiation layer into the core loop on 2026-07-23, together with the Anki `Translation` field it feeds. → English subtitles

### In scope — differentiation (build after the core loop works)

- [ ] **Furigana-only display mode** (no romaji, no translation overlay) — the one display-simplification feature in scope; romaji and kana-only modes are excluded below. → Roadmap, Phase 6
- [ ] **Subtitle appearance controls** (font size, position, background opacity). → Roadmap, Phase 6
- [ ] **Hotkeys** (toggle subtitles, pause-and-reveal, offset adjust) — **partial:** only the offset hotkeys (Alt+←/→/0) exist; the rest is scheduled in Phase 6.
- [ ] **Auto-pause on new subtitle line** (Anki-style reveal). → Roadmap, Phase 6

### Considered, deliberately unscheduled

- **Sentence-only capture (no target word).** The use case is real — a sentence with no confusing vocabulary can still be worth saving — but it needs its own capture paradigm, not a toggle on the existing flow. Its deferral reason is "revisit once there's usage data from the popup-based flow" (2026-07-04), and that data doesn't exist yet, so building it now would be guessing at a capture paradigm rather than answering a known need. Left for Eric to schedule. See Open questions → "Sentence-only capture".

### Out of scope

Each line: what, why, and when it was excluded. Full reasoning is in [Decisions](#decisions) → Product & scope.

- **AI grammar/sentence explanations, in any form** — breaks immersion and competes with a grammar resource the user trusts more; excluded on principle, including an opt-in BYOK version. Revisiting it is a scope conversation with Eric first. (2026-06-30)
- **Built-in wordbook / SRS / quiz** — would duplicate Anki, where the user already has review history. This bars a rival *review* system, not editing a card you just made: the in-page edit panel (2026-07-30) is in scope, and reading this exclusion as banning it is what shipped the wrong design on 2026-07-29. (2026-06-30)
- **Other video platforms (Netflix, YouTube, etc.)** — Crunchyroll-only by design (see Positioning), not a Netflix-specific gap. (2026-06-22)
- **Manga OCR and general web support** — same reason. (2026-06-22)
- **Romaji subtitle-display mode** — a total-beginner feature for learners who can't read kana yet, which this user by definition can; not a priority call. (2026-07-04)
- **Kana-only subtitle-display mode** — works against furigana mode by stripping out the kanji the user is likely studying elsewhere. (2026-07-04)
- **EN-only subtitle mode** — it would suppress the Japanese overlay and the dictionary popup, which are the extension's core purpose. (2026-07-29)
- **Kanji ON/KUN reading display, even opt-in** — kanji-level pedagogy, the job of a kanji SRS the user already trusts. (2026-07-04)
- **Names dictionary (JMnedict)** — a second data source and pipeline for a problem the kanji-name fix already covers. (2026-07-04)
- **Custom dictionary URL buttons / a choice of dictionary source** — cuts against "nothing stands between the learner and the dictionary": one dictionary, not a menu. (2026-07-04)
- **Always-visible JLPT labels, JLPT filtering or sorting** — JLPT stays an opt-in, off-by-default tag only; the 2026-07-02 exception doesn't reopen the AI-explanation or wordbook exclusions, which stand for unrelated reasons. (2026-07-02)
- **Screenshot Anki field** — confirmed DRM-blocked; dropped from the roadmap entirely, not deferred. (2026-07-17)
- **Karaoke-style word highlighting** — no per-word timing data exists anywhere, and an approximation would drift out of sync and mislead. (2026-07-06)

## Competitive landscape

Competitor facts first; our own status is in the *Where we stand* line under each item.

### ManabiDojo — the main competitor

A 2-person team, ~2 years old, ~5,000–8,000 Chrome Web Store users, rated 4.7–4.8. The free tier covers Crunchyroll-catalogue shows with basic lookups; $6/month or $120 lifetime unlocks AI features, flashcards, Netflix support and — notably — parsing of raw *imported* subtitles for dictionary lookup. A user who imports a missing show via Jimaku still has to pay to get word-segmented, clickable text on it, even though import itself is free.

**Root cause of their reliability issues:** their parsing stack (Ichiran/Ichimoe + CaboCha) is confirmed **server-side**, not client-side — their own changelog describes uploaded subtitles being sent to "our server" for parsing, with explicit capacity limits.

### Where ManabiDojo leads

Confirmed directly from their Features page (2026-06-30) and changelog. These are parity items, not gaps we set out to close.

- **Up to four toggleable subtitle layers:** Japanese (with furigana above kanji while paused), Kana, Romaji, and Native (dual subtitles in any officially supported language).
  *Where we stand:* our English-subtitle display (shipped in Phase 5, 2026-07-23) covers the same end-user need as their Native layer. Romaji and kana-only layers are out of scope.
- **Jimaku import with auto-offset-detection** (aligns against the platform's native subtitle track) and **per-season "Jimaku history"** (remembers the preferred uploader/file; viewable and clearable) — materially more polished than a manual-offset-only flow.
  *Where we stand:* per-show-per-season uploader memory with sticky fallback shipped 2026-07-16; the picked Jimaku entry is remembered per season (2026-08-13); offsets carry forward per series + season + release group since 2026-08-15, with the per-episode override kept. Not built: auto-offset-detection, and a view/clear UI for the saved memory (both Roadmap, Phase 6).
- **AI sentence/grammar breakdown** (chunk-by-chunk, refreshable) and **AI in-context translation** (the meaning of the clicked word in this specific sentence).
  *Where we stand:* out of scope, as a stance.
- **Colour-coded frequency-rank badges** (green/yellow/orange/grey) — more granular than a simple common/uncommon flag.
  *Where we stand:* gap closed 2026-07-19 — a real three-tier frequency badge behind an opt-in toggle, replacing our earlier common-word flag. The tiers' colour treatment is still undesigned.
- **POS tags, inflection info, kanji ON/KUN + JLPT level, a Names dictionary tab.**
  *Where we stand:* POS chips and the inflection line shipped (Phase 4); JLPT shipped as an opt-in tag (2026-07-22). Kanji ON/KUN and a Names dictionary were declined 2026-07-04. Kanji-containing names are still clickable as ordinary words — a separate, narrower fix (Tokenizing & grouping).
- **Custom dictionary URL buttons** (up to 3, e.g. Jisho) and a choice of dictionary API source.
  *Where we stand:* declined 2026-07-04.
- **A built-in Wordbook** with mastery levels and Anki-compatible export.
  *Where we stand:* out of scope; we export straight to Anki.
- **Romaji toggle, hotkeys, furigana display** — all shipped and mature.
  *Where we stand:* romaji excluded; offset hotkeys only, the rest in Phase 6; furigana in the popup, with furigana-only mode in Phase 6.

### Known user complaints (our opportunity map)

1. **Paywall trust** — free features moved behind the subscription after launch; specifically, the parsing/lookup step on imported subtitles is gated even though import itself is free.
2. **Reliability** — intermittent bugs, server connectivity, regional subtitle issues (very likely downstream of the server-side parsing stack).
3. **Price** — €6/month is seen as expensive by some users relative to free alternatives (Yomitan + ASBPlayer). Several reviews mention pairing ManabiDojo with Yomitan, suggesting a segment of their own users already prefers a leaner, non-AI lookup tool.

### Our differentiation strategy

Revised 2026-06-30 after a direct review of ManabiDojo's site, and again on 2026-07-02 once Anki export moved into the core loop. Don't re-litigate "Where ManabiDojo leads" or the complaints without new evidence.

- **Anki export that isn't paywalled a second time** — core to the loop from the start, not bundled inside a wordbook the user may not want, and not gated behind premium the way theirs is.
- **No AI middleman — a stance, not a missing feature.** It doesn't compete with a grammar resource the user already trusts (e.g. Bunpro), doesn't break immersion mid-episode, and answers the underlying cause of complaints 1 and 2 (server-side AI dependency) by design.
- **Free, ungated parsing on imported subtitles** — universal Jimaku import with full dictionary lookup from day one; ManabiDojo paywalls exactly this step.
- **Crunchyroll-specific depth over multi-platform breadth** — forced-subtitle coexistence, fansub drift handling, anime-specific vocabulary.
- **A structurally lower cost base** — no backend or AI-API cost per user, giving room to price sustainably even though our pricing *model* (subscription + lifetime) now matches theirs and WaniKani/Bunpro's. A cost-structure advantage, not a pricing-model differentiator.

*Where we stand on the three Jimaku-import UX gaps* (all decided and scheduled by 2026-07-05): the title-search mismatch (Crunchyroll's English titles vs Jimaku's JP/romanized indexing) is solved by plain title matching against the page's own metadata (2026-07-15), which replaced the 2026-07-04 plan to resolve TMDB/AniList IDs; per-season memory is built (above); auto-offset-detection is in Roadmap, Phase 6.

## Architecture

Organized by component, not by build phase: to learn how something works, go to its component. Each one describes **what is built now**. Reasoning and rejected alternatives are in [Decisions](#decisions) under the same heading, and planned-but-unbuilt work is in [Roadmap](#roadmap). Where a feature spans several components (frequency tiers, JLPT, the source field, fullscreen handling), each piece carries a *See also* line pointing at the others.

### Platform & cross-cutting

**What it does:** The Chrome extension shell — which code runs where, and the rules every component shares.

**Key files:** `manifest.json`, `background.js` (service worker and message handlers), `content.js` (isolated-world content script), `caption-url-sniffer.js` (page-world script)

**Invariants:**
- No backend and no AI API calls. Network traffic goes only to Jimaku, Crunchyroll's own caption CDN and the user's local AnkiConnect.
- Read only what the page already hands a logged-in session; never call Crunchyroll's internal APIs ourselves (passive observation — Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-23; reaffirmed for dubs 2026-09-22).
- Every cross-origin request goes through the background service worker.
- Never widen `host_permissions` on a guess: a speculative host permission is a security regression and a store-review liability (Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-26).

**How it works now:**
- **Manifest V3.** Not really a choice: MV2 has been fully disabled in stable Chrome since October 2024 with no path back; the last enterprise-policy workaround closed in 2025, and even the developer-flag workaround was being phased out by mid-2026.
- **Which context does what is forced, not stylistic** (confirmed during the Phase 2/3 build). Chrome is deprecating cross-origin `fetch` from content scripts, so all network I/O — Jimaku, Crunchyroll's caption CDN, AnkiConnect — lives in `background.js`. kuromoji's dictionary loader needs `XMLHttpRequest`, which service workers don't have, so tokenizing runs in the content script. Net effect: network I/O → background; anything needing a real DOM or XHR → content script.
- **Scripts and permissions.** On `https://*.crunchyroll.com/*` the isolated-world content scripts load in order `vendor/kuromoji.js`, `tokenize-utils.js`, `subtitle-parser.js`, `audio-capture.js`, `content.js` (plus `content.css`) at `document_idle`; `caption-url-sniffer.js` runs separately in the page's own MAIN world at `document_start`. `vendor/kuromoji-dict/*` is web-accessible. `host_permissions` cover `*.crunchyroll.com`, `*.crunchyrollcdn.com`, `jimaku.cc` and `http://127.0.0.1:8765/*` (AnkiConnect, added 2026-07-17); the only permission is `storage`. The manifest matches all of crunchyroll.com, not just watch pages — see Show & episode detection for why.
- **Shared modules** (`tokenize-utils.js`, `subtitle-parser.js`) export for Node only behind `typeof process !== "undefined"` (2026-06-30), so the same files run as content scripts and in the offline tests.
- **Orphaned content script (2026-08-15).** After an extension reload the old content script keeps running with every `chrome.*` call throwing. It notices on its own ~4Hz tick (and a 2s timer, so a paused video notices too), removes every element it mounted, and shows one banner with a Reload button.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-06-30 — `typeof module !== "undefined"` is not a safe Node-only check in a content script: `module` can exist in some browser and bundler contexts. Hence the `process` test.
- 2026-07-15 — **Isolated worlds share state, not function overrides.** A content script gets its own `history` object wrapping the shared navigation state, so reassigning `history.pushState` there has no effect on the page's own calls to its unpatched reference. Reading state (`location.pathname`) works across the boundary, and so do genuine browser events (`popstate`), DOM events and `window.postMessage`. This is why episode changes are detected by polling and why the caption sniffer runs in the MAIN world.
- 2026-07-22 — "Uncaught Error: Extension context invalidated" in the console comes from reloading the extension during development: a stale content-script instance still calls `chrome.runtime.*`. A normal single-session install never sees it; since 2026-08-15 the orphaned script shows the reload banner instead.
- 2026-07-23 — `chrome.webRequest` can't read response bodies in Manifest V3, which is why the caption sniffer overrides `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest` inside the page (English subtitles).
- `createMediaElementSource()` can be called only once per `<video>` for the life of the page, so a re-injected content script could never capture audio again — see Anki export: audio capture, and Open questions → "Extension-reload banner".

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Platform & cross-cutting.

### Show & episode detection

**What it does:** Works out which show, season and episode is playing — with zero user input — and keeps that answer correct as the user moves around Crunchyroll without page reloads.

**Key files:** `content.js` (`detectShowEpisode`, `loadSubtitles`, `episodeIdentity`, `beginSubtitleRequest`, `subtitleRequestIsCurrent`, `installCues`, `isWatchPage`, `handleTimeUpdate`)

**Invariants:**
- **Binding:** every subtitle load, entry pick, file pick and upload is a request (`beginSubtitleRequest`). `installCues` installs cues, the offset file and the Anki show/episode label only if that request is still the newest *and* the page still reports its identity.
- **Unique identity:** `episodeIdentity` must never be shared by two different episodes (pinned by `scripts/test-detection-identity.js`).
- **Never read a stale block:** a `TVEpisode` block naming a different `/watch/` id is dropped unread. When the retries run out without the page confirming the episode, nothing is loaded — no fetch, no cues, a message saying why. There is no fallback to a stale block.
- **No guessed fallback:** no parseable block → `null` → the "use manual upload" message, never a Jimaku query with a wrong or empty title.
- Only `/watch/` pages activate.

**How it works now:**
- **Source of truth: the page's schema.org JSON-LD `TVEpisode` block** (SEO markup, not React-internal state that could break on a frontend rebuild). `detectShowEpisode()` reads `partOfSeries.name`, `partOfSeason.name`, `partOfSeason.seasonNumber`, `episodeNumber`, the compound `name` and the block's own `url`/`@id`. This is the only season source the extension reads — not Crunchyroll's v2 CMS API (`/content/v2/cms/seasons/{id}/episodes`, whose equivalents are `season_title` and `season_number`); the two were confirmed to agree on a real Attack on Titan OAD page (JSON-LD `"name": "Attack on Titan OADs"`, `"seasonNumber": 66`, the same values as the CMS), so the OAD fixtures reflect real runtime data (2026-08-01). The page exposes no external ID at all (TMDB, AniList or MAL) — see Jimaku entry resolution for how the show is matched instead.
- **Episode number:** the numeric code inside the compound `name` (`E1156`) is preferred over `episodeNumber`, which is season-relative on some pages and absolute on others; `episodeNumber` is the fallback when the code isn't numeric. A disagreement between the two is logged once per page. A non-integer code (`EEX`, `14.5`, `SP1`) gives the episode no position (Episode & file selection → Invariants). The missing-number guard runs *after* the code is read, so a prologue whose code is `0` isn't discarded with its null number. `episodeNumber: 0` is a real value (films) and every site handles it with `??` or `Number.isInteger`, never `||`.
- **Compound `name` shapes measured on live pages:** a compound, not a title — `"X | E1 - X"` on the first film page, `"<season> | E<code> - <title>"` for episodes (e.g. `"OVAs | EEX - Memory Snow (Director's Cut)"`, `"Season 1 | E14.5 - …"`); and for a standalone film, the title **duplicated across the pipe with no episode code at all**. How these become search titles is in Jimaku entry resolution.
- **Watch-page gate:** the manifest matches all of crunchyroll.com so navigation *into* a watch page is noticed without a reload, and `isWatchPage()` (`/watch/` in the URL) is required alongside a `<video>` before anything runs.
- **Navigation:** Crunchyroll changes episodes in-page, without reloading. `location.pathname` is polled every 1s (the primary mechanism), with `popstate` as a faster path for genuine back/forward navigation; a change dispatches `jp-immersion-locationchange` and re-runs `loadSubtitles()`, the detect-and-fetch function shared with `init()`. On every navigation the module-scope `video` is re-queried and `handleTimeUpdate` is moved from the old `<video>` to the new one.
- **Retries and staleness:** when detection finds nothing, or (on a navigation-triggered reload only) still reports the previously loaded identity, `loadSubtitles` retries within a 5-second budget. The block's own `url`/`@id` carries the episode's watch URL and the whole block is replaced in place on in-app navigation, so `detectShowEpisode` compares only the id after `/watch/` with the page's own — a locale prefix (`/de/watch/…`), a different slug, a query string or a trailing slash on either side is ignored. A block naming this page is used immediately with no wait; where blocks disagree mid-swap, the one naming this page wins. If retries run out, nothing loads and `lastLoadedIdentity` is left alone so the watchdog reloads the moment the block settles. The 5-second wait now only matters for a block with no URL, and the warning `carries no episode URL` says that premise has degraded.
- **Watchdog:** every 2 seconds, for the life of the page, the `<video>` binding and the loaded identity are re-checked — what a manual reload used to do, without the reload. It won't fire on top of a load already in flight.
- **Identity:** `episodeIdentity` = series + season name + season number + episode number + the page's compound title. It collides on none of the three catalogue captures' 9,740 episode records — 6,997 distinct integer-numbered episodes once duplicates across captures are removed, which is the count `scripts/test-detection-identity.js` checks. Disagreeing `TVEpisode` blocks read as not yet detectable.
- **Audio versions:** each audio version of an episode (sub, dub) is its own `/watch/` id, so switching the audio language is an ordinary navigation to a different episode, not a special case. Both versions' blocks carry identical names — KonoSuba 3 E4 has no `(English Dub)` in the season name — so `episodeIdentity` is the same for both and only the `/watch/` id and pathname tell them apart.
- **Verified live:** end to end on Witch Hat Atelier episode 1 (2026-07-15: reloaded extension and page, subtitles appeared); episode-to-episode navigation (2026-07-15); the 2026-07-17 detection fixes; and checklist F8, the audio-language switch (passed 2026-09-22 on KonoSuba 3, Japanese ↔ English — the console showed the stale block ignored and the old version's in-flight load discarded). Unverified parts: Roadmap → Built, not verified live.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-15 — Crunchyroll's watch pages sit behind a Cloudflare bot challenge that blocks both `curl` and WebFetch, so every page fact comes from a DevTools dump or a console collector Eric pastes into a logged-in session (Test & audit tooling). Grepping a full page dump for TMDB, AniList and MAL — meta tags, JSON-LD, embedded script state — found zero hits.
- 2026-07-15 — The first navigation fix wrapped `history.pushState`/`replaceState`; it shipped and then failed live because of isolated worlds (Platform & cross-cutting). The same change moved `OFFSET_STORAGE_KEY` to per-navigation (Subtitle overlay, sync & offset).
- 2026-07-17 — Five detection bugs from one live session, all confirmed fixed live: intermittent "couldn't detect the show/episode" after next-episode navigation (the URL updates slightly before the new `TVEpisode` block is in the DOM); a show-to-show switch leaving subtitles blank until a reload, because `video` was captured once at `init()` (confirmed working Frieren → Witch Hat Atelier → Frieren both ways); Witch Hat Atelier episode 1→2→1→2 landing back on episode 1's default uploader (a stale block); and the "couldn't detect" error on the homepage and search page, whose autoplay promo banner is an unrelated `<video>`. The `/watch/` segment was assumed rather than checked against the live site, and was confirmed in practice when that fix worked.
- 2026-07-27 — `partOfSeason.seasonNumber` is a positional index into Crunchyroll's own season list, which is ordered by release date and counts OVA/special collections as seasons — not the anime's season number. (How resolution copes: Jimaku entry resolution.)
- 2026-08-01 — **Crunchyroll numbers episodes inconsistently between shows** (from the CMS catalogue sweep of 30 series, 134 seasons, 4,829 episodes, `fixtures/crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-01.json`). Absolute: SPY×FAMILY season 2 starts at episode 26, The Apothecary Diaries' at 25, Detective Conan's third season at 754, Naruto: Shippuuden's seventh at 144 (`S7:144`). Restarting at 1: Frieren, Re:Zero, Chainsaw Man, Overlord, Mushoku Tensei, KonoSuba. "MIXED" is the normal shape of a long franchise: main-line arcs absolute, side seasons restarting (One Piece's Log remaster and HEROINES; the OVA/movie seasons of My Hero Academia, Black Clover and Slime). Crunchyroll splits Naruto: Shippuuden into 17 seasons where the Hulu upload uses 25. **Caveat:** this table came from the CMS `episode_number`, not the JSON-LD the extension reads — the two agree on Naruto and disagree on One Piece — so it describes Crunchyroll's catalogue, not the extension's input. No per-catalogue convention is relied on anywhere (Episode & file selection's magnitude rule).
- 2026-08-01 — **JSON-LD vs CMS on the episode number**, measured on two live pages:

  | page | `episodeNumber` | code in `name` | CMS `episode_number` | displayed |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | One Piece Elbaph | **1** | **E1156** | 1156 | 1156 |
  | Naruto S2 opener | 33 | E33 | 33 | 33 |

  One Piece's Elbaph arc resolved episode 1156 against episode 1 live before the code was preferred. Across all 2,385 episodes of the second sweep, the CMS's own code-vs-number split has the same shape: 2,356 agree, 4 disagree (the `"0"`/null prologues), 7 carry non-numeric codes (`SP1`, `P1`, `SP`, and Heaven's Feel's `I`/`II`/`III`). That check now runs as a standing part of the analyzer.
- 2026-08-01 — **Entries with no episode number** (second sweep: 45 series, 75 seasons, 2,385 episodes weighted to Gundam, Fate, Dragon Ball, Detective Conan and Code Geass, `fixtures/crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-01b.json`): 13 had none in the CMS — the Gundam films (Char's Counterattack, Awakening of the Trailblazer, SEED Freedom, Narrative, Cucuruz Doan's Island), prologues carrying the code `"0"` with `episode_number: null`, and PVs. First read as leaving the films undetectable; settled the same day as a CMS artifact, because JSON-LD reports `episodeNumber: 0` for Cucuruz Doan's Island where the CMS says `null`. Its verbatim metadata resolves end to end through the film path (duplicated title, no marker → non-episodic → lone-result match): entry 2034, three files, and no `?episode=` request (pinned by a fixture). A full audit of the episode path found no falsy-zero handling.
- 2026-08-01 — `season_number` is unbounded (0, 29, 82, 100, 102, 122, 133, 143, 153 and 902 all occur) and not unique within a series (Mobile Suit Gundam uses 1 for both "Mobile Suit Gundam (Dub)" and "Char's Counterattack"; Fate/Grand Order Babylonia likewise), so it can never be a key on its own. No pagination gaps: every season's episode list matched its declared count.
- 2026-08-01 — **Series arrangement varies for films.** Infinity Castle has its own Crunchyroll series entity, so `partOfSeries.name` is already the film title (and `seasonNumber: 0`); The Last Attack is folded into the parent show, so it is the bare franchise; Chronicle is filed under a separate series entity named "Attack on Titan Movies". Both film arrangements have their own verbatim fixture.
- 2026-08-01 — Three separate times a fixture built on an *inferred* page shape passed offline while the real page failed (the film `name`, then Memory Snow's `EEX` code, then the duplicated-title films). Hence the rule that Crunchyroll-derived fixture strings are captured verbatim or marked as a guess (CLAUDE.md → Engineering rules).
- 2026-09-18 — The old identity (series + season number + episode number) was shared by 37 different real episodes across the three captures: Shangri-La Frontier's episode 14 and its 14.5 bonus, Fate/UBW's two prologues, and every dub season numbered like its sub.
- 2026-09-22 — **The URL premise, measured live** (two runs of `scripts/collect-jsonld-blocks.js`): across 76 navigations and 35 watch pages, every watch page carried a `TVEpisode` block, no block lacked a usable `url`/`@id`, and the block named its own page within 500ms every time — including after an audio switch, where the dub page's block names the dub's own id. The first run (36 navigations, 17 watch pages by subtraction) was read off the console and never saved; `fixtures/jsonld/jsonld-blocks-2026-09-23.json` (40 navigations, 18 watch pages, settling at 0/100/250/500ms on 1/1/10/6 of them) is the only JSON-LD fixture. Separately, across the three catalogue captures, 210 of 299 sampled episodes carry more than one audio version, all guids distinct, the page's own id always among them, and 208 of 210 span more than one `season_guid`.

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Show & episode detection.

### Jimaku entry resolution

**What it does:** Turns the detected show/season/episode into the right Jimaku *entry* (Jimaku indexes each season, film or OVA collection as its own entry) — and refuses, visibly, when it can't identify one.

**Key files:** `background.js` (`resolveTextFiles`, `searchQueryLadder`, `parseSeasonMarker`, `stripSeasonSuffix`, `entrySeasonNumber`, `nonEpisodicClass`, `looksLikeStandaloneWork`, `contentTitleCandidates`, `matchEntryBySeasonName`, `matchEntryByContentTitle`, `matchEntryByNonEpisodicClass`, `matchEntryByFullTitle`, `looseTitle`, `franchiseIsSplit`)

**Invariants:**
- **Refuse rather than guess.** When nothing positively identifies an entry, nothing loads and the entry picker is offered. Never fall back to "whatever the search returned first" (`entries[0]`), and never to another season's entry.
- An entry is returned if and only if it was identified (asserted by `scripts/test-entry-resolution.js`).
- A season number and a season title are read from the **same** Jimaku field (`name` or `english_name`), never one from each (2026-08-02).
- An episode-title search may only widen the candidate pool with entries from the **same franchise** (2026-08-02).
- A named season is never matched by its Crunchyroll list position alone (2026-09-18).
- A parsed season marker only proposes a match; the season-stripped entry title must still equal Crunchyroll's title before it's accepted.
- A film is identified by its own title — never by being "the only film-class entry" in the results.

**How it works now:**
- **Status:** closed 2026-08-13 — verified live (the entry-resolution regression script in Test & audit tooling passed in full) and offline-clean; the last full audit (2026-08-13) covered 2,526 episodes with 0 COLLISION / 0 DUPLICATE / 0 EMPTY / 0 request failures. Unverified parts: Roadmap → Built, not verified live. How to re-verify: `CLAUDE.md` → Commands & testing.
- **Jimaku API.** A REST API behind a personal account and API key; entries are keyed to TMDB/AniList IDs with subtitle files underneath. Endpoints: `GET /api/entries/search` (`query`/`anilist_id`/`tmdb_id`/`anime`/`after`/`before`), `GET /api/entries/{id}`, `GET /api/entries/{id}/files?episode=N`. Auth is the raw key in an `Authorization` header (no `Bearer` prefix). Every endpoint returns `x-ratelimit-*` headers and `429` at the limit — 25 requests per window per key (`x-ratelimit-limit: 25`; documented as 25 req/min), so search results are cached rather than re-queried on every load. Auth is per user, so onboarding needs the user's own Jimaku account and key (Roadmap, Phase 7). No viable alternative source exists for this niche. Response shape, checked against real responses (2026-07-15): file objects carry only `url`/`name`/`size`/`last_modified`; entry objects add only `flags` (anime/unverified/external/movie/adult), `anilist_id`, `english_name`, `japanese_name`, `creator_id`. There is no structured season field, no structured uploader field, and no view/download/favourite count anywhere.
- **Search.** The query is `partOfSeries.name` with apostrophe characters stripped (from the query and from both sides of every comparison). Jimaku's search is a substring match against its own titles, and it indexes English titles as aliases (`english_name`) as well as JP/romanized names. A zero-result search widens through `searchQueryLadder()`: the segment after the last colon, then trailing words dropped one at a time, capped at six rungs and each reached only when the rung before found nothing. When a search returns exactly one entry that stands for the whole franchise, the ladder looks past that first hit so later seasons are found (the Fruits Basket "(2019)" case).
- **Extra searches for non-episodic content** (films, OVAs, specials, compilations). `contentTitleCandidates` splits the page's compound `name` back into the titles it's built from — the part before the pipe, and the part after the episode code — first parsed as an `E<n> -` marker, now matched as an opaque token (`E` plus a short alphanumeric run) — takes anything after a pipe even when no code matches, and adds `partOfSeason.name` as a third candidate, most specific first. A trailing-qualifier-stripped variant (e.g. without "(Director's Cut)") is added as an *extra* candidate, so the full title gets first go; a candidate that is only a format word, or equals the series title, is dropped. Results are merged into the pool, filtered by a franchise check that runs in both directions (the entry name extends the series title for a film folded into its parent show, and is shorter than it for a film with its own series entity).
- **Season markers.** `parseSeasonMarker()` recognizes the four conventions in live use — `"2nd Season"`, `"Season 2"`, Roman numerals (`"Overlord IV"`), a bare trailing number (`"…Shukufuku wo! 3"`) — plus "Final Season" (no digit). It strips a trailing `"Part N"`/`"Cour N"` first as a cour marker, bounds the bare-number form to 2–20 so `"Mob Psycho 100"` and `"Jujutsu Kaisen 0"` aren't misread, anchors on whitespace and never consumes a leading separator. `stripSeasonSuffix()` and `entrySeasonNumber()` are thin wrappers. A Crunchyroll season marker is read wherever it sits in the season name, not only at the end ("Season 1: Director's Cut").
- **Season names.** The season name comes from `partOfSeason.name` and is tried with and then without an `(English Dub)` qualifier (Jimaku entries never carry it). Name matching tries three title forms — the whole name; series title + season name; series prefix + season suffix — because Crunchyroll and Jimaku disagree on how much of the franchise title a season name repeats. This is the only thing that resolves arc-named seasons (Demon Slayer, Attack on Titan's Final Season, Dr. STONE), none of which carry a season number on either side. A season name identical to the series title is ignored. A missing `partOfSeason.name` is logged as a warning.
- **Non-episodic classification.** `nonEpisodicClass(name)` reads the season *name* into one of five side formats — OVA/OAD/ONA as one class, film, special, picture drama, recap — plus `stage` (a stage play). "Special" followed by a qualifier (edition, version, cut, feature, screening, broadcast) is not a format, via a negative lookahead. `looksLikeStandaloneWork` treats a compound `name` that names the same work on both sides of the pipe as a standalone work, and a non-integer episode code also makes content non-episodic. `isEpisodic` gates the season-number tier (suppressed for side formats), the confidence calculation and the unfiltered-file fallback. The classifier was checked for over- and under-firing against 37 real season names, including "Mugen Train Arc" — a TV season named after a film, whose film entry sits beside it in the same search results.
- **The tiers, in order:** (1) season name (`matchEntryBySeasonName`); (2) the content's own title (`matchEntryByContentTitle`, top priority for non-episodic content, ties broken by format class — "Mugen Train" matches both the film and the TV retelling); (3) format class (`matchEntryByNonEpisodicClass()`: the entry must belong to the franchise and the match must be unique — this connects Crunchyroll's "OAD" to Jimaku's "Attack on Titan OVA"); (4) a lone search hit (`soloHit`, a single-result search from the searches made here, under the two-way franchise check); (5) season number — only when it came from the season name, or when the page publishes no distinct season name (KonoSuba 3's shape); (6) exact title; (7) exact entry name. `matchEntryByFullTitle()` compares punctuation-insensitively (`looseTitle()` collapses everything non-alphanumeric, so `"Demon Slayer: Kimetsu no Yaiba - The Movie: Mugen Train"` equals `"Demon Slayer -Kimetsu no Yaiba- The Movie: Mugen Train"`), tests containment in one direction only (an entry name contained *in* Crunchyroll's title is the franchise, not the film), and trusts a full-query search returning exactly one entry only if every word of the query appears in that entry's name.
- **Where it declines.** The bare-franchise entry counts as season 1, never as a container, so it is refused for a later season when (a) the franchise is visibly split — `franchiseIsSplit`: some entry carries a season marker above 1 — or (b) the season's own *name* says it isn't season 1 and the franchise genuinely has several works (entries flagged `movie` by Jimaku aren't counted). A `stage` season is refused unless something names it specifically. A film nothing names resolves to nothing. When no tier matches, `resolveTextFiles()` returns `{unresolved: true, candidates}` **before any file request**, and `fetchSubtitles` passes that back as a normal response, not an error (the picker UI is in Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload).
- **Logging.** Entry resolution is logged on every load, in the **service worker** console (not the page console); a season fallback also logs a warning. The resolved entry's name is returned to `content.js` and shown in the switcher panel's label.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- **What resolution deliberately can't do** (2026-08-13). All four end in the entry picker or manual upload, never a wrong load. *No lexical bridge:* Crunchyroll's "Root A" and Jimaku's "√A" share no characters, and Detective Conan is indexed as "Case Closed" / "Meitantei Conan" — these decline (Detective Conan accounts for ~580 refusals in a full audit, correctly; a 2026-08-04 run's 876 DECLINED were two-thirds this one show). *Generically named later seasons:* where Crunchyroll says "Season 2" and Jimaku names the season after an arc, nothing on Crunchyroll's page connects them, so the user picks once per season and it is remembered. *Content Jimaku doesn't hold:* Attack on Titan's Chronicle has no entry, and its lone search result is a different film, so the picker says so and points at manual upload. *Two works sharing one entry with Japanese-only filenames:* Haikyu!!'s OVAs can't be told apart from Crunchyroll's English titles, so a short list is offered instead of one file. Closing the last two would need Japanese↔English title mapping, which is its own scope.
- 2026-07-15 — Title matching was verified against the live API: the exact title Crunchyroll exposes ("Witch Hat Atelier") returns exactly one entry — Jimaku id 11793, `anilist_id: 147105`, JP-romanized `name` "Tongari Boushi no Atelier" — matched through `english_name`.
- 2026-07-17 — "No Jimaku entry found for 'Frieren: Beyond Journey's End'": Crunchyroll writes a straight apostrophe and Jimaku a typographic one, and the straight form returns zero results. Jimaku's season entries for Frieren are "Sousou no Frieren" / "Frieren: Beyond Journey's End" and "Sousou no Frieren 2nd Season" / "…Season 2", while Crunchyroll's `partOfSeries.name` stays the plain title for every season. Confirmed live: season 2 then loaded season 2's dialogue.
- 2026-07-26 — KonoSuba season 3 kept loading season 1: Jimaku marks that show's seasons with a bare trailing number. Season-marker parsing was then validated end to end against live search results for 17 show/season combinations across all four conventions; the three-form name matching (2026-07-29) across 12 further multi-season franchises.
- 2026-07-26 — An optional `[:-]?` separator in the season patterns silently broke Re:Zero: `String.match` returns the leftmost match, which started at the title's own trailing dash in "Re:ZERO -Starting Life in Another World- Season 2". Caught only because validation ran against real entry names across many shows.
- 2026-07-27 — Re:Zero loaded the *next* season's subtitles from the OVAs onward, because Crunchyroll's season number counts the OVA collection as a season (Show & episode detection). Crunchyroll's season title and Jimaku's `english_name` are the same string (`"Re:ZERO -Starting Life in Another World- Season 2"` on both).
- 2026-07-31 — `partOfSeason.name` is published: the warning built to report its absence never fired across the whole live pass, and name matching worked on every ordinary TV season tested.
- 2026-07-31 — Films, OVAs, specials and compilations fell through to the franchise's season-1 entry, silently, and four titles Jimaku does hold came back "No Jimaku entry found". The ladder recovered Mugen Train, Infinity Castle, The Last Attack and Re:Zero's OVAs, all of which returned zero results under Crunchyroll's spelling.
- 2026-08-01 — Three failures from treating "no season block" as "not episodic TV": Attack on Titan's OADs took season 2's entry silently; Re:Zero's OVAs found the right entry and then hard-errored "No subtitle file found for episode 1" (four files, none numbered 1); a film logged `matched by season 1`.
- 2026-08-01 — Three films failed three ways, seen once the *service worker* console was read: Mugen Train loaded Infinity Castle's subtitles; Infinity Castle refused correctly but by accident (a trailing "I" made its single correct result read as a near-miss); The Last Attack fell through to the flagship TV entry. "Demon Slayer: Kimetsu no Yaiba" returns six entries without Mugen Train's, while "Mugen Train" finds it first. Mugen Train's `partOfSeason.name` is already "Demon Slayer -Kimetsu no Yaiba- The Movie: Mugen Train", so it now resolves by season name; a log line reading `for "Demon Slayer: Kimetsu no Yaiba"` shows `partOfSeries.name` (the resolver's `query`), not the season. Passing the compound `name` to Jimaku verbatim returns zero results. Memory Snow's `"OVAs | EEX - Memory Snow (Director's Cut)"` broke an `E\d+` marker (leaving only "OVAs") and carries a qualifier the filenames lack. Infinity Castle's and The Last Attack's season names ("Infinity Castle I", "THE LAST ATTACK") carry no format word. Jimaku's entry name puts "the Movie:" in the middle of "Attack on Titan the Movie: The Last Attack", which no containment test reaches. `seasonNumber: 0` (Infinity Castle) is handled — every test is `!== null` or `??`; the one `&&` omits "Season 0" from the Anki show/episode label, which is right for a film.
- 2026-08-01 — Chronicle loaded The Last Attack's subtitles: Crunchyroll files it under a series entity named "Attack on Titan Movies", Jimaku returns exactly one entry for that (The Last Attack's), and the single-result branch predated the franchise check. The word floor admits "Attack on Titan: THE LAST ATTACK" → "Attack on Titan the Movie: The Last Attack" and rejects the "movies" bucket; both are adjacent fixtures.
- 2026-08-01 — "Special Edition" misread as a format: One Piece lists `"East Blue Special Edition HD, Subtitled (1-61)"` beside `"East Blue (1-61)"`, a remastered cut of 61 ordinary episodes, and the plain `/specials?/` rule would have suppressed the season match across three arcs (61, 82 and 63 episodes). Found by the bulk collector's first partial capture (three series, before an auth token expired mid-run) — invisible on any of the six shows the work was built against. Pinned with verbatim season titles, including the true positives ("OVA Season 1", "Attack on Titan OADs", "…THE FINAL CHAPTERS Special 1").
- 2026-08-02 — *Season 1* played *season 2's* subtitles on Slimes 300 Years and Mushoku Tensei: the season-2 entry carries its marker only in the English name, while the Japanese name has none ("…: Sono ni") or a Roman numeral the parser doesn't read ("Mushoku Tensei II"), and the old test took the number from one field and the title from the other. Jimaku returns the season-2 entry first for Mushoku Tensei.
- 2026-08-02 — Tokyo Ghoul's OVA "Jack" pulled in 24 unrelated shows through an episode-title search. With the franchise filter and the split-franchise rule, both OVAs resolve to their own entries ([PINTO] and [JACK], two files each) where episode 1 had listed all 71 files of the main entry. "Root A" had resolved confidently to season 1's entry. One Piece is the counter-case: 27 search results, no split marker, and one "ONE PIECE" entry that genuinely holds all 24 arc seasons.
- 2026-08-04 — `(English Dub)` made the season-name tier miss every dubbed season: 19 of the 90 seasons in the 2026-08-04 capture, 577 episodes; Code Geass failed on all 55 of its own. Not a regression — Code Geass and Detective Conan fail identically against pre-fix `background.js`.
- 2026-08-12 — Dr. STONE seasons 2 and 3 and Fruits Basket's season 2 and Final Season played season 1's subtitles through the exact-title fallback; their arc names (`STONE WARS`, `NEW WORLD`, `The Final`) carry no number. The traps: One Piece's 24 arcs all have `seasonNumber` 2 or above ("East Blue (1-61)" has no marker; Black Clover's "Season 1 Part 1" parses to 1); Naruto: Shippuuden is "Season 17" on Crunchyroll but one 500-episode Jimaku entry, whose other five search results are films; Dr. STONE has eight entries and no films. Fruits Basket's season-1 entry shares "(2019)" with the series title, so the search returned one entry; season 2 now resolves to its own entry and the Final Season still declines. Verified over 1,267 episodes (every affected show plus the six known-bug shows): both COLLISIONs gone, no new proven defects, and exactly the intended 46 new refusals (Dr. STONE 33, Fruits Basket Final 13). JUJUTSU KAISEN Season 3 had been wrongly written up as part of this defect; it already declined correctly.
- 2026-08-12 — Crunchyroll lists "Chainsaw Man The Stage" as season 3 of Chainsaw Man; it fell through to the TV series' episode 1, silently. Jimaku splits that franchise by named work (Reze-hen, Soushuu-hen), which `franchiseIsSplit` can't see. Only one season across all three captures matches `stage`.
- 2026-09-18 — Kaiju No. 8: Mission Recon, a recap special at Crunchyroll list position 2, loaded Season 2's episodes, "matched by season 2" (found by the audit's baseline sweep). "Season 1: Director's Cut" had been resolving only through its list position.

**See also:** Episode & file selection (what happens inside the chosen entry); Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload (the picker and the remembered entry).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Jimaku entry resolution.

### Episode & file selection

**What it does:** Inside the chosen Jimaku entry, finds the file(s) for exactly this episode, ranks them and picks one — or lists them without loading any when it can't be sure which one is right.

**Key files:** `background.js` (`fetchSubtitles`, `filesForEntry`, `filesFromRememberedEntry`, `courSiblingEntries`, `matchFilesByEpisodeNumber`, `rankFiles`, `PREFERRED_UPLOADERS`, `applyFileHint`, `ARCHIVE_RE`, `fetchAndParseFile`), `caption-url-sniffer.js` (sibling-episode capture), `content.js` (sibling-title cache)

**Invariants:**
- **One retrieval path:** `filesForEntry` serves the resolver, the remembered entry and the entry picker alike. For episodic content nothing else ever lists a whole entry, and a pick it can't match lists the files without loading one.
- Candidates are always filtered to the exact show and exact episode, never a broader per-show list.
- Listing an entry's whole contents requires knowing *which work* it is (a positive tier in Jimaku entry resolution). Reaching the entry through the series-title fallback means the show was found, not this season, so the unfiltered listing is refused there (2026-08-04).
- Narrowing never discards valid alternates for the same work — on a film every file in the entry *is* the film (2026-08-02).
- An episode code that isn't a plain integer gives the episode no position, so its number is never used to pick a file; it is treated as non-episodic (2026-08-02).
- An integer episode never takes a file naming a fractional position (第13.5話, "- 14.5", E14.5); a list of nothing but such files fails loudly.
- An offset retry drops the uploader population the offset was read from.

**How it works now:**
- **Episodic content — Jimaku's filter first, then four retries in order.** The entry is asked for `?episode=N` (Jimaku's own server-side filter, which reads whatever numbering is baked into a filename). Then:
  1. **Local resolution from filenames** when Jimaku's answer can't be trusted. Three triggers, each from a measured failure: the answer disagrees with itself, it contains only archives, or it consistently names a different episode. `第N話` is the discriminator. The two numbering readings are separated by magnitude — an episode number larger than its own season's length can't be season-relative — so no per-show convention is needed. When filenames carry neither pattern, `matchFilesByEpisodeNumber` returns null and Jimaku's own filter stands.
  2. **A cour sibling** (`courSiblingEntries`), retried only when the chosen entry returns nothing. A sibling is a same-season `Part N`/`Cour N` entry, or the chosen entry's name plus a *bare* trailing number whose episodes start above everything the chosen entry holds. The sibling's own filenames must state the requested episode (where it states numbers at all), and within the cour retry only, files that positively state a *different* episode are dropped while files stating nothing are kept. Bracket numbering like `[13]` counts as a position only when it's 1–3 digits, so `[1080p]`, `[BDRip]`, CRCs like `[E395C71D]` and years can't be read as one.
  3. **An offset from Jimaku's own index**, only for a later-season entry that came back empty: `?episode=1` is requested and the highest episode position stated in those filenames is read as that episode's absolute number. Positions are anchored on `" - "` and a following delimiter, so a hyphenated title, a year in parentheses or a resolution isn't read as one. One extra request, only on an entry that already failed, and the retry has to return something before it is believed.
  4. **An offset from a second numbering inside the entry**, last and cheapest-to-trust: only when the entry states episode 1, the higher run is contiguous and at least 3 files long, the requested episode is absent from the lower run, and the derived episode actually returns files.

  The offset retries drop files whose name prefix matches the population that stated offset+1, leaving the entry's own numbering or nothing. Files stating a fractional position are dropped from an integer episode's list.
- **Non-episodic content** (films, OVAs, specials, compilations, and any episode without a plain-integer code) skips the `?episode=` filter entirely. The entry's files are listed and then: narrowed by the episode's own title (on the same punctuation-insensitive key as entry titles; only a match narrows — no match falls through to the full list, never to an empty one), except for single works like a film; **ruled out** when they match any *other* episode title in the season (with a trailing-qualifier-stripped and a before-the-colon variant of each title, and skipping a sibling variant that overlaps the current episode's own title); reduced to the numbered part for a multi-part work, fenced by three conditions (only among files already narrowed to one work by title, every file stating exactly one position, and positions running 1..N with no gaps); and served whole when a positively identified entry holds exactly one usable file that states no episode of its own. If exclusion leaves nothing, that is its own error pointing to manual upload.
- **Where sibling episode titles come from.** `caption-url-sniffer.js` passively watches the adjacent-episode lookups Crunchyroll's frontend already fires on every episode load (`/content/v2/discover/previous_episode/{id}` and a `next_episode` counterpart; title at `data[].panel.title`, identifiers under `data[].panel.episode_metadata`). `content.js` accumulates them in `chrome.storage.local` under `siblings:<seasonMemoryKey>` with a read-merge-write against storage, and trusts a title only when both series and season agree with the page's JSON-LD (an adjacent episode can belong to the previous season). A 2-episode OVA set is covered by one load; an 8-episode OAD set fills in as episodes are watched — partial coverage still helps, because every title learned excludes one more file. No extra request, no auth token; with no match it fails closed to the plain listing. `__jpImmersionSnifferStats().siblingEpisodesSeen` (page console) shows whether it fired.
- **Archives:** files matching `ARCHIVE_RE` (`.7z`/`.zip`/`.rar` etc., which can't be parsed as subtitle text) are dropped; if every file is an archive, a clear error points to manual upload.
- **Ranking:** `rankFiles(files, preferredUploader)` puts a saved per-season uploader preference first (prepended to the list, de-duplicated), then `PREFERRED_UPLOADERS = ["SubsPlease", "Haruhana", "VCB-Studio"]` in that order, matched by the filename's bracket tag (e.g. `[Haruhana]`) — the only place uploader identity exists — and keeps everything else in Jimaku's own order. `rankFiles(files)[0]` is the automatic pick; there has never been a manual step, so auto-select on load falls out for free. (`rankFiles` replaced a single-winner `selectPreferredFile()` on 2026-07-15, once the switcher needed the whole ranked list.)
- **Language-track hint:** `applyFileHint` takes an optional case-insensitive filename substring (e.g. `"[JPN]"`) and matches it only among files sharing the top pick's own bracket tag. It chooses between one uploader's dual CHS+JPN cut and its JPN-only cut of the same episode — which uploader ranking can't — and can't fire on unbracketed direct-source files (no tag). Pinned by four cases.
- **Download:** `fetchAndParseFile()` downloads and parses the chosen file for both the automatic load and a manual pick; dual-language stripping happens there (Subtitle parsing & display filters).
- **Status:** closed with entry resolution on 2026-08-13. (The cour retry is what makes Re:Zero S2 episodes 14–25 resolvable at all: its "2nd Season" entry holds none of them, confirmed live 2026-07-29.) Unverified parts: Roadmap → Built, not verified live.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-06 — Naruto episode 1's Jimaku list has a `.zip` as its first file, which the old blind `files[0]` pick would have fetched and failed to parse. `scripts/batch-test.js` had the `ARCHIVE_RE` filter since Phase 2, but it had never been ported to the extension — a latent production bug.
- 2026-07-06 — Naruto: Shippuuden's entry holds a whole run of Hulu-sourced releases numbered per season (S07E01 = 第144話, S15E01 = 第296話), and Jimaku's own per-file tagging calls every one of them "episode 1" — a first-file pick would have served the wrong episode with no error at all.
- 2026-07-15 — `rankFiles` was unit-tested against the real 8-file candidate list for Witch Hat Atelier episode 1 (prefers SubsPlease over an unmatched uploader, falls back to the first file when nothing matches, keeps non-preferred files in relative order). That episode's Haruhana release has both a CHS+JPN dual-track file and a JPN-only file.
- 2026-07-17 — The Witch Hat Atelier uploader preference looked completely broken (always Haruhana, whatever was picked, after an episode change or a reload): `FILE_HINT` `"[JPN]"` searched *every* candidate (an unscoped `textFiles.find(...)`), and Haruhana is the only uploader whose filename contains that substring, so it overrode both the default ranking and the saved preference. Latent since the hint was added (2026-07-05/06); Frieren's testing never showed it because none of its files contain "[JPN]". Fixed and confirmed live (together with the sticky-fallback retests).
- 2026-08-01 — **Naruto: Shippuuden (entry 2142) breaks Jimaku's filter:** `?episode=1` returns 28 files (every season's opener) and `?episode=5` likewise; `?episode=144` — the episode those tags describe — returns only archives; `?episode=33` returns exactly one usable file, `S12E33 第275話`, a confident-looking answer for the wrong episode. 500 of its 506 files carry both a season-relative and an absolute tag (`S07E01.第144話`), and the absolute numbers cover 1–500 with no gaps and no duplicates; the local resolution was swept over all 500 under both numbering conventions, 0 failures either way, and verified quiet on Frieren, KonoSuba and the AoT OVA entry. Residual risk: the season-relative branch assumes Crunchyroll's season grouping matches the uploader's `Sxx`, and they do disagree for Naruto (17 vs 25 seasons) — but Crunchyroll numbers Naruto absolutely (`S7:144`), so that branch never fires there.
- 2026-08-01 — Frieren S2 episode 1 legitimately returns both `…S02E01…` (Amazon's per-season numbering) and `…S02E29…` (Netflix's absolute) for the same episode, and Frieren's season 2 answers episode 9 with files numbered both 09 and 37 — which is why "the results disagree" is not a safe trigger. Jimaku's index generally carries both conventions because uploaders differ: SPY×FAMILY season 2's entry holds `S02E01` and `S02E26` for the same episode.
- 2026-08-01 — One Piece is the opposite extreme: its main arcs are numbered absolutely (season 24, `"Elbaph (1156-current)"`, first `episode_number` 1156, which far exceeds the season's length), and its Jimaku files carry neither pattern (`第N話` absent; the Netflix rips use `S40E1156`, an absolute number in the `Eyy` position that the season-relative pattern deliberately declines), so Jimaku's filter answers `?episode=1156` accurately. Naruto and One Piece go through opposite branches of the same rule.
- 2026-08-01 — Five consecutive Attack on Titan OADs loaded a file whose uploader release tag matched Crunchyroll's episode number (episode 2 got "[Kamigami] …#3.25 OAD2", episode 3 "[ReinForce] …OAD3"). That numeric matching is Jimaku's server-side filter: `/entries/1597/files?episode=2` returns the OAD2 file, `?episode=3` OAD3, `?episode=1/4/5` nothing. Pinned with Jimaku's real per-episode answers. The same per-file numbering problem first appeared on Naruto: Shippuuden (2026-07-06).
- 2026-08-01 — Memory Snow and The Frozen Bond were offered the identical four-file list, and The Frozen Bond's list included Memory Snow's file. "The Frozen Bond" and the uploader's "Hyouketsu no Kizuna" share nothing, which stays a limitation (a wholly different rendering can't be matched); a partial re-translation can: OADs 4–8 kept listing episode 1's file because Crunchyroll's "Ilse's Notebook: Memoirs of a Scout Regiment Member" is the file's "…Memoirs of a Recon Corps Member" — hence the before-the-colon variant. "No Regrets: Part 1" and "Part 2" never rule each other out (fixture). Sibling titles also need the qualifier-stripped variant, or Crunchyroll's "(Director's Cut)" stops every sibling from matching a filename.
- 2026-08-01 — The sibling cache clobbered itself: it merged against `seasonEpisodeTitles`, which navigation resets and `loadSubtitles` repopulates asynchronously, and Crunchyroll's adjacent-episode lookup fires before that read resolves — so the cache never grew past its last two neighbours (episode 1's title still wasn't excluded by episode 8). Now a read-merge-write against storage.
- 2026-08-01 — The sibling list was not "already available": `detectShowEpisode()` parses only the current episode's block, nothing fetches `/content/v2/cms/seasons/{id}/episodes`, and Jimaku's file objects carry no episode field. The first implementation watched for a batched "episodes in this season" response that doesn't exist (a real page's network log has nothing like it). After three wrong guesses at Crunchyroll response shapes, the sniffer is pinned by `scripts/test-sibling-sniffer.js`, which runs the real script against the verbatim captured JSON.
- 2026-08-02 — Mugen Train showed 1 of its 4 files, because only Fujitv's release spells the title as Crunchyroll does.
- 2026-08-02 — Shangri-La Frontier 43–50 and My Dress-Up Darling past episode 16 loaded nothing: Crunchyroll numbers those seasons absolutely while Jimaku's season-2 entries number from 1 (`?episode=43` empty, `?episode=18` three files — the same episode). Neither entry has a single dual-numbered file (0/103 and 0/78). My Dress-Up Darling's `[Haruhana]` numbers 13–15 absolutely while `[shincaps]` numbers 01–12 per season, both bare; Shangri-La only appeared to work for 26–42 because its absolute-numbering uploader stopped at 42. Jimaku's index files Haruhana's "‑ 13" under episode 1, which is what the index offset reads: 26 for Shangri-La Frontier, 13 for My Dress-Up Darling.
- 2026-08-02 — Shangri-La Frontier's bonus `"Season 1 | E14.5 - …"` (`episodeNumber: 15`) played episode 15's subtitles, and Dr. STONE's `"… | EEX - …"` extra (`episodeNumber: 1`) played episode 1's — the source of the no-position invariant (the episode-level twin of the season-level OAD work).
- 2026-08-04 — Demon Slayer's "TV Specials" season reached the main entry through the series title alone and handed over its whole catalogue — 131 files spanning 26 episodes, identically for all five of its episodes; Dr. STONE's SCIENCE FUTURE did the same. Two wrong turns on the fix, both caught by fixtures: gating on "the entry's files name several episodes" broke every legitimate OVA collection, and the first attempt passed file *objects* to a function expecting names, so the guard silently never fired while the audit kept reporting the bug.
- 2026-08-11 — Haikyu!!'s "TO THE TOP" is one 25-episode Crunchyroll season and two Jimaku entries, the back half named with a bare trailing number; episodes 14–25 (12 of 25) failed. The first fix accepted "Mushoku Tensei: Jobless Reincarnation Season 2 Part 2" as a continuation of season 1's entry — caught by the known-bug regression run and confirmed new with `--background` (COLLISION 0 → 1). KonoSuba's "… 2" and "… 3" are separate *seasons* written the same way (rejected by numbering: 1 ≤ 10, where Haikyu's is 14 > 13). Fixtures: Haikyu accepted, KonoSuba rejected by numbering, Jujutsu Kaisen rejected by name shape; the one constructed value, an empty `?episode=24` on entry 712, is labelled as such.
- 2026-08-12 — Dr. STONE's SCIENCE FUTURE (one 37-episode Crunchyroll season, three Jimaku cours): episode 13 found nothing in Cour 1, and the cour retry asked Cour 3 (episodes 25–37) for "episode 13" and got its 13th file, `S04E37` — episodes 13 and 37 both played 37. It now reaches Cour 2. SPY x FAMILY's Cour 2 answered episode 13 with `S01E13` and `S01E25`, seven of thirteen files a different episode. Frieren still returns all twelve after the fix.
- 2026-08-12 — My Hero Academia's "I am a Hero too" is a TV special listed as an ordinary "episode 1"; its entry holds one file naming no episode, so asking for episode 1 failed on a special Jimaku has.
- 2026-08-12 — MHA: Vigilantes season 2 lost 6 of its 13 episodes: entry 11309 holds one uploader's files numbered 01–08 and another's numbered S02E14–S02E26 for the same episodes. It was wrongly recorded as blocked on Crunchyroll's season episode range (which `resolveTextFiles` never receives) — the entry's own numbers separate the cases:

  | Entry | Stated numbers | States 1? | Outcome |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Vigilantes S2 (11309) | 1–8 **and** 14–26 | yes | offset 13 |
  | Haikyu TO THE TOP 2 (2886) | 14–25 | no | no offset derivable |
  | My Dress-Up Darling S2 (9834) | 1–15 contiguous | yes | no second numbering |

  Confirmed externally: episode 6 resolves to `S02E19.雨と雲`, and Crunchyroll titles episode 6 "Rain and Clouds".
- 2026-08-12 — "Chainsaw Man - The Compilation" is two recap episodes in one Jimaku entry, and both narrowed to the same four files. The numbered-part fences rule out the old failure: Attack on Titan's "- OAD3" and "- #3.25 OAD2" can't be parsed to one position each, and Re:Zero's OVA entry states 26 and 27 and stops. The Compilation had been wrongly written up as resolving to the anime entry; it never did.
- 2026-09-18 — The remembered-entry path and the entry picker, on an empty `?episode=N`, listed the *whole* entry and auto-loaded its top file, marked confident — the shortcut `resolveTextFiles` refuses — and skipped every other episode rule (filter disagreement, cour/offset retries, the single-work special).
- 2026-09-18 — Shangri-La's entry 7707 holds one uploader numbering season 2 absolutely (26–35) and two numbering from 1. The offset probe reads 26 out of `?episode=1`, so episode N is asked as local N−25, and for a local number the entry doesn't hold, Jimaku answered with the absolute uploader's "- 26" (season 2's first episode), loaded as confident. Not reachable on the real episode list (Crunchyroll's season ends where the entry does), but general.
- 2026-09-18 — My Hero Academia season 1 episode 1's list carried Amazon's `S02E01.第13.5話 ヒーローノート`, a recap special, beside the real episode 1 — offered in the switcher, one ranking change from auto-loading. Providers also label the same episode's season differently (Netflix files Haikyu!! S2 as its own show, S01E01), so differing `SxxEyy` labels prove nothing.

**See also:** Jimaku entry resolution (choosing the entry); Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload (the uploader preference and the remembered entry fed into this).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Episode & file selection.

### Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload

**What it does:** The user's escape hatches: a panel showing which Jimaku entry and file are loaded and letting the user change them, per-season memory of those choices, and a manual upload for anything Jimaku doesn't have.

**Key files:** `content.js` (`buildSwitcherPanel`, `renderSwitcherOptions`, `extractUploaderTag`, `uploaderPrefKey`, `entryPrefKey`, `seasonMemoryKey`, `buildUploadControl`), `background.js` (`FETCH_SUBTITLES`, `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE`, `FETCH_ENTRY_FILES` handlers; `filesFromRememberedEntry`)

**Invariants:**
- **Sticky preference:** a single episode's gap never clears or overwrites a saved uploader preference — nothing is ever written back to storage from the ranking.
- **A remembered entry never overrides a working automatic answer:** it exists only after a manual pick; any failure to use it falls back to ordinary resolution instead of throwing; and the picker stays on screen while it's in use, so a wrong pick stays correctable.
- Only an uploader's bracket tag is ever persisted — never a file URL or a raw filename.
- Per-season memory is keyed on `seasonMemoryKey` (series + season number + season *name*, or the work's own title on a season-less page), never on Crunchyroll's list position alone.
- An option the resolver has already ruled out is never presented as a choice.

**How it works now:**
- **Switcher panel.** `buildSwitcherPanel()` creates `#jp-immersion-switcher`, positioned and styled like the offset and upload controls; `renderSwitcherOptions(panel, files, selectedUrl)` fills one labelled native `<select>` with every ranked candidate, pre-selecting the one playing. The label names the resolved Jimaku entry. `FETCH_SUBTITLES`'s response carries the full ranked `files` list and `selectedUrl` alongside `cues`, so one round trip serves both the automatic load and the panel. Picking another file sends `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE` (`url`/`name`), which downloads and parses just that file through `fetchAndParseFile()`; a failed pick reverts the dropdown to the last good selection. `renderSwitcherOptions()` runs on every `loadSubtitles()` (first load and every navigation), and hides the panel (`display: none`) when there's nothing to list — unless there's an entry picker to show. Rough and functional by design (native `<select>`, no styling); polish is in Phase 6. Confirmed working live 2026-07-16.
- **Entry picker** (`FETCH_ENTRY_FILES`). In the unresolved state (Jimaku entry resolution) the panel shows every candidate under a distinct banner — "no subtitles were loaded — pick one" — rather than "check these are the right subtitles", which is the wording over files the user has just loaded by picking an entry (`renderSwitcherOptions` is also called from the picker's own change handler). Picking clears the unresolved wording. A single candidate is shown too; a lone candidate the resolver already ruled out (Attack on Titan's Chronicle) says what it is and points at manual upload, with the dropdown kept for anyone who knows better. A picked entry with no file matching the episode lists its files and says "none loaded", and nothing loads until a file is chosen. Playback is never blocked.
- **Uploader preference.** `extractUploaderTag(filename)` reads the release group's leading bracket tag (e.g. `[Haruhana]`) and returns `null` for unbracketed direct-source files (Netflix/Amazon rips), whose picks persist nothing and apply to that episode only. A successful switch saves the tag under `uploaderPref:<seasonMemoryKey>`; `loadSubtitles()` reads it before every `FETCH_SUBTITLES` call and passes it as `preferredUploader`, which `rankFiles` ranks first in the same pass (Episode & file selection). The sticky fallback needs no code of its own: with no matching file this episode, every file ranks exactly where the default list would put it.
- **Remembered entry.** An entry picked in the switcher is saved under `entryPref:<seasonMemoryKey>`, and `loadSubtitles()` passes it as `preferredEntryId`; `filesFromRememberedEntry()` then gets that entry's files through `filesForEntry` — the same episode rules automatic resolution uses — instead of resolving the entry again.
- **The key.** `seasonMemoryKey` is shared by the uploader preference, the remembered entry and the sibling-title cache (`siblings:`). Picks saved under the older series + season-number keys were deliberately not migrated. Offset memory uses its own keys (Subtitle overlay, sync & offset).
- **Manual upload.** `buildUploadControl()` is a file input for `.srt`/`.ass`/`.ssa`, always visible (overriding one episode is a real case even when Jimaku succeeds). It reads the file with `FileReader.readAsText` and parses it with `parseSrt`/`parseAss` directly — `subtitle-parser.js` is loaded as a content script — then goes through the same request binding and parse cleanup as a Jimaku file. It reassigns the shared `cues` (the one `timeupdate` listener reads from them) and dispatches a synthetic `timeupdate`, so a paused video shows the new file at once. It is also the answer for shows with zero Jimaku subtitles: a zero-results state becomes an upload prompt rather than a dead end.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- No UI exists to view or clear saved memory — part of the 2026-07-04 design, not built (Roadmap, Phase 6).
- 2026-07-15 — The switcher was unit-tested with a DOM stub against the real Witch Hat Atelier episode 1 list: it builds, pre-selects correctly, sends the right message on a pick, updates `cues` on success and reverts on failure. The uploader preference was unit-tested against 4 scenarios (overriding the default ranking; sticky fallback when the preferred uploader is absent; unchanged behaviour with no preference; the preference coinciding with a default-list entry), then confirmed live 2026-07-17 both ways: a SubsPlease preference falling back to Haruhana on an episode without it and returning; a Haruhana preference falling back to NanakoRaws on one gap episode and returning everywhere else.
- 2026-07-17 — Unbracketed Amazon/Netflix files not carrying a preference across episodes was confirmed live as expected behaviour (no bracket tag to extract); Witch Hat Atelier's own episode 1 list contains such files.
- 2026-07-26 — Why the entry name is in the label: Jimaku filenames carry no season a viewer can use — `[shincaps] Kono Subarashii Sekai ni Shukufuku wo! - 05.ass` looks equally right from season 1's entry or season 3's — so a wrong-season load had been undetectable from the switcher and noticed only partway into an episode.
- 2026-08-13 — Until the entry pick was remembered, a season the resolver refuses to guess cost one pick per *episode*; the uploader preference beside it *was* persisted, which made the gap easy to miss and easy to mis-state. The picker also used to require more than one candidate, which hid the panel entirely on Chronicle and left manual upload as the only visible option.
- 2026-09-18 — Crunchyroll's season number isn't unique: in the live captures six season slots are shared by different works, three of them a TV season sharing its slot with a film (Gundam / Char's Counterattack, KonoSuba 3 dub / Legend of Crimson dub, FGO Babylonia dub / Solomon) — so a pick for the film would have applied to every episode of the TV season. Until then the keys were built by `uploaderPrefKey(seriesTitle, seasonNumber)` and `entryPrefKey(seriesTitle, seasonNumber)` — `(seriesTitle, seasonNumber)` alone.

**See also:** Episode & file selection (`rankFiles`, `filesForEntry`); Jimaku entry resolution (the unresolved state); Storage & settings (the key inventory).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload.

### Subtitle parsing & display filters

**What it does:** Turns a raw `.srt`/`.ass` file into clean, timed Japanese dialogue cues, and defines exactly what each cue looks like on screen — which is also exactly what the Anki sentence contains.

**Key files:** `subtitle-parser.js` (`parseSrt`, `parseAss`, `srtTimeToSeconds`, `cleanParsedCues`, `stripDualLanguageCues`), `content.js` (`cueDisplayText`, `lineDisplayText`, `japaneseDisplayAt`, `STAGE_RE`, `INLINE_FURIGANA_RE`, `ASS_OVERRIDE_RE`, `SENTENCE_PERIOD_RE`), `background.js` (`fetchAndParseFile`)

**Invariants:**
- **No silent cue loss:** timestamps are read as base-60 fields of any count, and any that still can't be read are reported with a count rather than vanishing.
- **The track is dialogue:** `cleanParsedCues` — shared by Jimaku files and manual upload — drops karaoke glyph layers and runs the dual-language strip; `.srt` tag markup and gaiji placeholders are stripped at parse time.
- **One definition of what's on screen:** `cueDisplayText` is called both per cue per tick by `updateJapaneseCue` and by the Anki sentence merge, so the screen and the card can't disagree.
- The dual-language strip **fails open:** a file with no majority-Japanese style is returned untouched — a stray line is far better than stripping a user's subtitles to nothing.
- Markup is removed **by class**, not by listing characters; the one explicit list is labelled as deliberate.
- Real Japanese punctuation is never stripped: 「」, 、, ？, ！, ‼, ⁉.

**How it works now:**
