- **Parsing.** `parseSrt`/`parseAss` produce timed cues. `parseAss` keeps each Dialogue line's `Style` and records its alignment override (`assAlignment`, used by English subtitles). `srtTimeToSeconds` reads fields positionally from the right (seconds, minutes, hours, any leading field) with `,` or `.` before the fraction. `parseSrt` strips anything tag-shaped (`<name …>`/`</name>` — .srt dialogue has no other use for ASCII angle brackets, and Japanese writes 〈〉), strips `{\anN}`-style ASS override tags (Jimaku is full of .srt files converted from .ass with the tags left in), and replaces ARIB gaiji placeholders (`[外:…]`) with 〓, the conventional mark for an unrepresentable character.
- **`cleanParsedCues`** drops karaoke effect layers — a single-glyph event with three or more other single-glyph events on screen at the same time — and runs `stripDualLanguageCues()`, which classifies every ASS style by the majority script of its own lines and drops non-Japanese styles wholesale. Kana is the discriminator, since kanji is shared with Chinese and CHS+JPN releases are common. Inside a dropped style a line survives when hiragana is at least a quarter of its Japanese characters (katakana isn't counted, since credits use it for names like `ヨーク`). `.srt` has no styles, so it falls back to a conservative per-line rule gated on a high proportion (0.25) of Latin-only lines. This runs in `background.js`'s `fetchAndParseFile()` and on manual upload — never on `fetchEnglishSubtitles()` — so the on-screen box, the Anki `Sentence` field and the audio-capture cue boundaries are all cleaned in one place.
- **Simultaneous events.** Long lines are often split into several `Dialogue:` events at the same timestamp (a SubsPlease convention, not a bug — verified 2026-06-30 on Bocchi), so every cue on screen at a moment is joined into one displayed line. `japaneseDisplayAt(fileTime)` answers "which Japanese cues are on screen at this moment, and what do they render to" for *any* moment, not just now (the English gap-bridging uses it), and shows identical text from simultaneous events once.
- **The display chain (`cueDisplayText()`)**, in order: invisible format characters (Unicode `Cf`, e.g. U+200E/U+202A) removed; ASS tags (`ASS_OVERRIDE_RE`, a safety net behind the parser) and fansub markup stripped; a stage direction spanning the whole cue dropped (`STAGE_RE`, e.g. （ドアの開く音）). Then `lineDisplayText`, per line: dialogue dash; inline readings, hiragana or katakana, spaces allowed (`INLINE_FURIGANA_RE`, handled before labels); a whole-line stage direction; every leading speaker label — `（Name）` or `Name:` — while dialogue follows, but never at a colon between digits (`５：５`, `1:23:34`); doubled brackets (`((`/`))`) at either end. Finally 。 is replaced with a space and runs of spaces collapsed (`SENTENCE_PERIOD_RE` — only 。, never 、 or ？／！), and half-width katakana becomes full-width. Markup is stripped *before* the stage-direction test, because that test matches only a parenthetical that is the whole line.
- **The markup class:** `\p{Extended_Pictographic}` plus the contiguous U+2600–27BF span (Miscellaneous Symbols + Dingbats — which catches all four music notes, both continuation arrows and the off-screen-speech marker), plus one deliberately curated bracket list, `《》⸨⸩`. The list is forced, not lazy: Unicode classes those brackets exactly like `「」『』（）`, so no property can separate a monologue marker from a real quote. Exempt by design: `‼`/`⁉` (punctuation, though Unicode calls them pictographic), geometric shapes (Japanese censors a character with `○`) and unit signs (`°℃№`); `♪` and `〜` aren't in the class at all, so music-line and vowel-elongation handling is unaffected. Measured over 49,592 real corpus cues from 48 files.
- **Live verification:** unverified parts are listed in Roadmap → Built, not verified live.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-26 — **Dual-language files are real and common.** SubsPlease's `..._ja-en.ass` (the reported `[SubsPlease] … _ja-en.ass`) bundles Japanese (style `JPN TOP`) and English (style `Default`), each independently timed within ~100–200ms; KitaujiSub's Frieren episode 1 carries `Text - CN(1160)` and `Text - JP(1151)`, every Chinese line overlapping its Japanese counterpart; Witch Hat Atelier episode 1's `[Haruhana] … [CHS, JPN].ass` outranked the same uploader's `[Haruhana] … [JPN].ass` purely on Jimaku's ordering. Before the strip, both languages reached the on-screen box and the Anki `Sentence` field (which is on the card front). Real English styles are named `Default`, `Flashback`, `Signs`, `Songs_OP`, `DefaultItalics`. Nekomoe kissaten's Frieren release has genuinely English lyric lines ("And you alright") inside its Japanese-styled ED, and Japanese-only `.srt` files carry a few incidental Latin lines (`erai-raws` and the Netflix `ja[cc]` rip of episode 7 have exactly 4 each — about 1%, against ~50% for a real parallel track). Validated against 27 real Jimaku files across three shows with zero Japanese lines lost: the `_ja-en.ass` goes from 317 English cues to 0 while keeping all 317 Japanese; the Chinese releases (Nekomoe kissaten, Haruhana) lose their Chinese track and keep their credits; the Japanese-only files are untouched.
- 2026-07-27 — A `《` bracket and emoji-prefixed stage directions (a loudspeaker emoji on KonoSuba) survived the filters; the markup-before-stage-direction ordering came from this.
- 2026-07-29 — Defining the markup rule by class fixed a further 514 subtitle lines across 49,592 real corpus cues (`♬` music lines chief among them) with nothing correct broken.
- 2026-09-18 — **Corpus sweep** (386 real Jimaku files, 233,963 lines): five Bandai film releases write every time past one hour with four fields (`00:01:00:15,367` for 1:00:15.367), so 574–728 cues per file after the hour became NaN and the second half of each film silently had no subtitles. `<i>`, `<font color=…>` and `<rb>` rendered literally in 7 of 248 .srt files (984 displayed lines); a TV rip carried ARIB gaiji placeholders (`[外:46123D2913F84CFAB6F8F782E9F5B2F8]`). Karaoke typeset one glyph per event in 7 of 386 files (1.8%), 2,296 moments; 1,067 displayed lines in 25 files showed doubled, one per layer. 7,729 displayed lines (3.3% of 233,963) kept a parenthesised speaker label on their *second* line; Netflix-derived files start lines with U+200E/U+202A (780 cues, two files), which `.trim()` doesn't remove; `（壬氏(ジンシ)）` was cut at its inner parenthesis; 18 lines were losing their text before a ratio or time. 351 kana-bearing lines sat in dropped styles (K-ON!!'s speaker-labelled dialogue in an English-majority `Default`, Natsume's dialogue in a style literally named `JP`, a character's name card) — 338 are now rescued and every staff credit (Chinese credits contain at most a の inside a name, `有明の月`) is still dropped. Leftover parentheticals fell from 8,411 to 614 lines, the rest being real content.
- 2026-09-18 — **Accepted residuals:** Amazon-style ruby writes `<rb>今日</rb>こんにちの` with the reading's closing tag lost, so where the reading ends is unknowable (`ガンドフォーマット` could be reading + word) — tags stripped, every character kept (18 occurrences, 3 files). Parenthesised speech (`（頑張れ！）`, 14 corpus lines) is kept, since shape alone can't tell it from a sound effect. A katakana-only exclamation inside a dropped style (`ナイス`) is lost — 2 lines.
- 2026-09-18 — `\pos` can't mark typesetting: 582 of 965 events in one ordinary dialogue file carry it.
- The NanakoRaws line-break shape and old-form kanji / Chinese lines in lyric files are open questions.

**See also:** English subtitles (which uses `assAlignment` and `japaneseDisplayAt`); Tokenizing & grouping (what happens to the displayed text next); Anki export: cards & capture (the merged sentence).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Subtitle parsing & display filters.

### Subtitle overlay, sync & offset

**What it does:** Draws the Japanese (and English) subtitle boxes over Crunchyroll's player in step with the video, keeps them visible in fullscreen, and lets the user nudge the timing — remembering the correction per episode and per release.

**Key files:** `content.js` (`init`, `renderCue`, `handleTimeUpdate`, `updateJapaneseCue`, `getContainer`, `buildOffsetControl`, `setOffset`, `providerOffsetKey`, `OFFSET_STORAGE_KEY`), `content.css` (`#jp-immersion-subtitle-stack`, `#jp-immersion-subtitle`, `#jp-immersion-subtitle-en`)

**Invariants:**
- Japanese cues are timed against the **offset-adjusted** `video.currentTime`; English cues against the **raw** one (Crunchyroll's own captions are synced by construction).
- Every element the extension shows over the player (subtitle stack, popup, chip) is re-parented into the current fullscreen container on every `fullscreenchange`, not only positioned once.
- The subtitle box is hidden (`display: none`) whenever it has no line to show.
- After any change of cues (automatic load, switcher pick, upload) a synthetic `timeupdate` is dispatched, so a paused video shows the new file immediately.

**How it works now:**
- **Layout:** one bottom-anchored `#jp-immersion-subtitle-stack` (created in `init()`) with two flex children — the clickable Japanese box `#jp-immersion-subtitle` first, the plain-text English box `#jp-immersion-subtitle-en` second — so their order can't vary at any caption length. The stack sits at the Japanese box's original 60px, so a Japanese-only screen looks exactly as it did before English existed. The boxes have `pointer-events: none` except for the clickable words, so they never block clicking the video. The stack is what gets re-parented on `fullscreenchange` and hidden on navigation away from a watch page.
- **Rendering:** `handleTimeUpdate` (a named listener, moved to each new `<video>` on navigation) calls `updateJapaneseCue()` and `updateEnglishCue()` unconditionally on every tick (~4Hz in Chrome); `renderCue` draws the Japanese line and reports completion, which the English line waits on (English subtitles). An empty line sets `display: none`; every path that shows real content — `renderCue`'s non-empty branch and `loadSubtitles`'s status and error messages — clears it.
- **Fullscreen:** the Fullscreen API renders only elements inside the fullscreen element, so `getContainer()` (`document.fullscreenElement ?? document.body`) is consulted again on every toggle: `init()`'s `fullscreenchange` listener re-parents the subtitle stack and the open popup (`activePopup`), and the chip's own `chipRepositionHandler` re-parents the chip.
- **Offset tools:** ±0.1s buttons and a reset in the offset control, plus hotkeys Alt+← / Alt+→ (±0.1s) and Alt+0 (reset, matched by `event.code === "Digit0"`).
- **Offset memory:** an adjustment writes two keys in `chrome.storage.local` — `offset:<pathname>` (this episode) and `offsetProvider:<series>:<season>:<releaseTag>` (this release). Reads prefer the episode's own value and fall back to the release's, so a one-off correction stays a one-off while a systematic one carries forward by itself. The release tag comes from `extractUploaderTag`, the same bracket tag as the uploader preference, so an untagged direct-source rip gets no release key and behaves as before; `offsetTouchedThisEpisode` stops the fallback overwriting a correction the user made before the file list arrived. `OFFSET_STORAGE_KEY` is recomputed on every navigation.
- **Forced subtitles:** Crunchyroll's forced-subtitle layer (on some titles) is a player-rendered text/caption layer, not burned into the video pixels (confirmed 2026-06-23), so coexisting with it is a z-index/positioning question, not a video-corruption workaround. Untested hands-on — no title with such a layer has turned up (Open questions → "Forced-subtitle overlay behaviour").
- Automatic offset detection against Crunchyroll's own subtitle timing is planned, not built (Roadmap, Phase 6).
- **Live verification:** unverified parts are listed in Roadmap → Built, not verified live.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-06-30 — Elements appended to `document.body` don't render while an element is fullscreen; the subtitle box got the `fullscreenchange` re-parent then.
- 2026-07-05 — Alt+0 on a Mac produces "º" (a macOS Alt-key remap), so an `event.key === "0"` check never matched; the arrow-key hotkeys are unaffected by Alt on Mac.
- 2026-07-15 — `OFFSET_STORAGE_KEY` used to be computed once at module load, so a saved offset would have leaked from one episode's key into the next once in-page navigation existed.
- 2026-07-17 — "Sometimes stuck on *Loading subtitles*, sometimes switches fine": the automatic load and the switcher pick set `cues` without the `video.dispatchEvent(new Event("timeupdate"))` the upload path already had, so the display only updated if the video happened to be playing. Confirmed fixed live (mid-episode switching applies immediately, playing or paused).
- 2026-07-17 — **A stray dark oval** appeared wherever the subtitle box sat during gaps between lines — once near the subtitle position, once over Crunchyroll's own episode-list text — because `renderCue` emptied the box (`subtitleBox.textContent = ""; if (!text) return;`) without hiding it, and its CSS `padding`/`border-radius`/`background` still draw on an empty div. It was fixed to the viewport (bottom 60px, centred), not the video, hence appearing over unrelated page content. It couldn't be selected in Inspect Element because of `pointer-events: none`, which first pointed away from our own code; it disappearing with the extension disabled pointed back. Confirmed gone live the same day.
- 2026-07-22 — The word popup and the chip vanished on entering fullscreen if created beforehand (and reappeared on exit), because only the subtitle box had the re-parent fix; confirmed fixed live 2026-07-23.
- 2026-07-26 — Two independently `position: fixed` boxes let a multi-line English caption grow up into the Japanese box and paint over it; and only the Japanese box used to be hidden on leaving a watch page, stranding the English one on non-watch pages.
- 2026-07-26 — The layout was verified in headless Chrome against the real `content.css` with a local harness page (no DRM involved) — layout and CSS bugs here can be tested without a Crunchyroll page.

**See also:** English subtitles (the English box's timing and pairing); Popup display and Anki export: capture chip & edit panel (both use the same fullscreen re-parenting); Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload (the uploader tag the release key reuses).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Subtitle overlay, sync & offset.

### Tokenizing & grouping

**What it does:** Splits each displayed Japanese line into clickable words — kuromoji segments it, then grouping passes merge inflections, set phrases, names and numbers into the units a learner would actually look up.

**Key files:** `vendor/kuromoji.js` (+ `vendor/kuromoji-dict/`), `tokenize-utils.js` (`tokenizeLossless`, `groupTokens`, `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX`, `JAPANESE_WORD_RE`, `NUMERAL_ONLY_RE`, `findPhraseMatchCandidates`, `classifyAndSelectPhraseMatches`, `applyPhraseMatches`, `findKanaMergeCandidates`, `applyKanaMerges`, `suppressTrailingSokuon`, `findKatakanaUnsuppressCandidates`, `applyKatakanaUnsuppress`, `extendsNameRun`, `findNameRunEnd`, `normalizeHalfwidthKatakana`), `content.js` (`buildGroupsForText`, `renderGroups`, `onWordClick`), `background.js` (`CHECK_KANA_MERGES`)

**Invariants:**
- **Lossless tokens:** kuromoji is only ever called through `tokenizeLossless`, which verifies that the tokens rebuild the input; if they ever don't, the line falls back to per-character tokens — unclickable, but complete.
- **Lossless groups:** `buildGroupsForText` renders a line as plain text if its groups ever fail to rebuild it, because `renderGroups` and the Anki bold offset assume they do.
- **No unsupported lemma:** a hiragana-only token in a conjugated form that exists only to take a suffix, with nothing attached, is looked up as itself under a strict interjection filter (`__utterance`), and stutter fragments are unclickable.
- **One pipeline:** the subtitle line and the edit panel's "Change word" both tokenize through `buildGroupsForText`, never a copy.
- Any tightening or loosening of phrase-matching or kana-merge is validated against the corpus (`scripts/batch-test.js`, Patterns 5/6/7), not a single test sentence.
- Mechanisms generalize: no hardcoded lists of broken words, phrases, grunts or heteronym contexts. A fixed list is allowed only where no general rule can draw the line, and then it is labelled in place as deliberate (the dual-view carve-outs below are one).

**How it works now:**
- **kuromoji.js** — client-side, pure JS, bundled locally, run in the content script (its dictionary loader needs `XMLHttpRequest`). Each token carries `basic_form`, `pos`/`pos_detail_1`, `conjugated_type`/`conjugated_form` (Japanese grammatical labels), a katakana `reading` and `word_type`.
- **`tokenizeLossless`** turns every astral-plane character into its own symbol token and sends only the text between them to kuromoji, then checks the round trip.
- Raw subtitle text is half-width → full-width normalized (`normalizeHalfwidthKatakana`, shared with `background.js`) before tokenizing, so half-width releases (e.g. VCB-Studio) also *display* full-width.
- **`groupTokens(tokens, fuseSpans)`** is the core grouping pass. Its rules:
  - **Rule 0 — honorific prefix** (お弁当 → looks up 弁当), with the inflection note "お (honorific prefix)" / "ご (honorific prefix)". It doesn't fire for お母さん/お客様-style words, which are their own lexicalized headwords (お is already in kuromoji's `basic_form`).
  - **Honorific suffixes** (さん/さま/くん/ちゃん/様/氏) are not merged — the preceding word keeps its own group — but kuromoji's uniform 名詞/接尾/人名 tag flags the suffix so its lookup is filtered to `suf` entries (Dictionary data & lookup). A title noun that can stand alone (先生, tagged 名詞/一般) isn't affected.
  - **Rule 0.5 — pronoun + pluralizing suffix** (私たち, 僕たち), merged deterministically with no JMdict check, since the pattern is always grammatical.
  - **Rule 0.6 — ん + copula family** (んだ/んです/んだろう/んでしょう), deterministic: looks up のだ and keeps the copula chain as the inflection label.
  - **Rule 1 — te-form:** [動詞]+[て/で] always becomes one group (the main verb), and the following auxiliary gets its own group, so both can be looked up. **Rule 2** (contracted te-form auxiliary, 見てる → 見ている) records the contraction itself as the inflection.
  - **Rule 3 — allowlisted absorption:** only `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` (た/ない/たい/せる/させる/れる/られる/う) and the plain copula (だ/です, `基本形`) absorb into the preceding word; everything else tagged 助動詞 (らしい/べき/まい) stays separate and is usually picked up by the phrase-matcher. Details: the conjecture stem (でしょ/だろ, `conjugated_form === "未然形"`, sharing です/だ's `basic_form`) looks up the *reconstructed surface*, with or without a following う, so でしょう/だろう and bare でしょ/だろ reach their own headword ("probably; I guess") instead of "be; is"; せる/させる/れる/られる are also accepted when kuromoji tags them 動詞 with `pos_detail_1 === "接尾"`; 助詞 たり/だり absorbs through a surface-form check (`isTari`), because kuromoji tags it 助詞, not 助動詞, so the allowlist alone can't reach it — no conjugation gate; 助詞 ば follows that precedent but absorbs only into a verb or adjective already in `仮定形` (見れば, 聞けば — the gate keeps ば's unrelated "place" noun out); verb-past だら/たら absorbs (読んだら, 組んだら) — it had been missing because only `"た"`, not `"だ"`, is in `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` — told apart from the copula's なら by `conjugated_type` (`特殊・タ` vs `特殊・ダ`); な tagged 終助詞 but directly followed by a noun is treated as だ's attributive form (ひとりぼっちな子 — kuromoji tags that な `助詞/終助詞` instead of `助動詞/体言接続` when the preceding word wasn't recognized as its own token); の tagged 名詞/非自立 is treated as the nominalizer/explanatory particle (なれないのだ), scoped to `basic_form === "の"` so real formal nouns (こと, もの, とき, わけ, はず) are untouched.
  - **Katakana names:** a katakana-only run becomes one inert span (`word: null`: no click, no underline) if any token is tagged 固有名詞, or if a standalone katakana word has no JMdict entry (an async existence check). A later async pass un-suppresses a run that turns out to be a real word. Kanji-containing names are ordinary clickable words and show the normal "no dictionary entry" message on a miss.
  - **Numerals:** `NUMERAL_ONLY_RE` (Arabic digits, half- or full-width) is checked before the `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` non-match branch — deliberately not by widening that regex, which phrase-matching windows, kana-merge and katakana-run detection also key on. Adjacent numeral tokens merge into one surface; the lookup is in Dictionary data & lookup.
  - **Name runs:** anchored on a 固有名詞-tagged kanji token and extended by `extendsNameRun`/`findNameRunEnd` — another 固有名詞 token at any length, but a `一般` token only when it's a single kanji (the sign that kuromoji fell back to character-by-character). A detected run is always fused into one lookup unit; existence only decides what's displayed (a coincidental real entry like 胡桃 shows normally; a miss shows one honest "no dictionary entry").
- **Trailing emphatic っ** (やめろっ, 行けっ): IPADIC emits a bogus standalone 動詞/非自立 token (`basic_form: "く"`) for it, so `suppressTrailingSokuon` removes it — as its own pass *after* `applyKanaMerges`, so a real merge (ん+っ → んっ, "hm?; huh?") gets first claim.
- **Utterances and stutters:** a hiragana-only token in an attach-only form (連用タ接続, 連用ゴザイ接続) followed by nothing that could attach (punctuation, space, end of line) is looked up as itself, showing "no entry" when JMdict lacks it; kanji keeps the verb reading (`言っ…`, `待っ…` are genuinely cut-off verbs). A short kana group followed by a pause and a word beginning with the same sound (`ほ… ほかには`) is a stutter and left unclickable.
- **Phrase-matching** (`findPhraseMatchCandidates` → `classifyAndSelectPhraseMatches` → `applyPhraseMatches`) runs on the **raw** kuromoji tokens, because some inflections arrive pre-combined (帰ったら is one 帰っ+たら token, which Rule 3 would absorb before any scan of grouped output). Fuse spans are passed into `groupTokens` via `fuseSpans` (finalized before Rules 0–3 touch those positions), with per-group `tokenStart`/`tokenEnd`. Every accepted match gets one of two outcomes in one overlap pass, longest span winning:
  - **fuse** — one clickable unit replaces the tokens (からといって, じゃない, んだ, にもかかわらず, ひとりぼっち). Gated on JMdict existence plus one of: 5+ characters; every source token tagged 名詞; a function-POS match; or two adjacent ≤2-character 動詞/形容詞 tokens (the signature of a segmentation error like ぼっ+ち). A candidate is rejected if baseline grouping already produces it, or if either boundary falls *inside* a baseline group rather than on its edge. Fused groups carry the sentinel `pos: "__phraseFuse"`.
  - **dual-view** — the tokens stay clickable and the phrase attaches as an "Also, as a set phrase" note, when both boundary tokens are genuine content words (動詞/名詞/形容詞/副詞/連体詞, e.g. もの…なる). A particle/auxiliary/copula-contraction boundary fuses instead. Carve-outs forced to dual-view — **a deliberate list, not a general rule:** each word keeps a common, unrelated meaning far more often than the idiom that would win the fuse gate (Decisions → Tokenizing & grouping): と (助詞), even beside a negative verb (探さないと); 時 (遠足の時に); 外 (外に, whose idiom sense ほかに wins the fuse gate by coincidence because the compact index isn't filtered by the `rK` tag — only the displayed `k` field is). A pure noun+noun span (王都 = 王+都) always fuses.
- **Kana-merge** (`findKanaMergeCandidates`/`applyKanaMerges`) rejoins a word kuromoji fragmented (ただいま！ → た+だ+いま), triggered by trailing hard punctuation or an embedded elongation mark (〜) against a hiragana run. The elongation branch needs only JMdict existence; the punctuation branch also needs a function-word POS (prt/exp/conj/int/aux-v).
- Phrase-matching and kana-merge make an async background round trip (`CHECK_KANA_MERGES`) only when candidates exist; the common case stays synchronous.
- **Rendering:** `buildGroupsForText` is the one awaited four-stage pipeline (extracted from `renderCue` 2026-07-30); a superseded line finishes building before being discarded, and a generation check guards the DOM write. `renderGroups` records each clickable span's exact character offset in the line, which the Anki bolding uses.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- **kuromoji's own limits.** `word_type` is `"KNOWN"` for both a segmentation mistake and a correct parse, so length/adjacency heuristics are the only signal for that class of bug (2026-07-01). It has no working runtime user dictionary — the bundled `"USER"` node-type branch is dead code (`// TODO User dictionary`, never implemented by its author), so "teaching it words" would need an offline recompile or an application-level merge like kana-merge (2026-07-01). Per-token `reading` is not reliable for sense disambiguation: tested on kanji heteronyms (一行 = いっこう "party" vs いちぎょう "one line"), 勇者一行 triggers a Viterbi-cost split with no usable reading at all, and in general it is right for 大分 but wrong for 十分/人気, which lack their alternate readings entirely (2026-07-03). It doesn't recognize every godan potential form — 輝ける is one opaque unknown token (`連体詞`, `conjugated_type`/`conjugated_form` both `*`) with no link to 輝く (2026-07-05; handled by a lookup fallback in Dictionary data & lookup). Benchmark lindera-wasm/Vibrato-wasm only if real corpus testing shows a quality gap — none has yet (Open questions).
- 2026-09-18 — **kuromoji silently deletes characters:** when two or more astral-plane characters (emoji; rare kanji such as 𩸽 or the 2010 常用 form 𠮟) form one unknown-word token, the same number of characters after them vanish — `😊😊abc` tokenizes to `😊😊` + `c`, and `𠮟𠮟ただいま！` loses ただ. Found by the audit's property test.
- 2026-07-01 — A small っ (sokuon) is 2 UTF-16 code units, not 1 — don't assume "1 mora = 1 character" for small kana (っ/ゃ/ゅ/ょ). This is why the suspicious-fragment signal is "≤2 characters".
- 2026-07-01 — だ/です and their conjecture forms (でしょ/だろ) share `basic_form`; `conjugated_form` (`基本形` vs `未然形`) is the only distinguishing field.
- 2026-07-01 — Root-causing ただいま！ and か〜くれんぼ showed a Viterbi cost / punctuation-adjacency issue, not missing vocabulary. Unfiltered kana-merge produced false positives at 1257-line corpus scale (はいい→"dethronement", あったか→"warm"), all matching only a content-word POS; かくれんぼ isn't flagged `common` though ordinary.
- 2026-07-01 — kuromoji's 固有名詞 tag is inconsistent: レン/ハイター are tagged correctly, equally fictional ヒンメル/アイゼン as ordinary nouns (一般).
- 2026-07-01 — Phrase-matcher false positives met while hardening it: にし→"west"; the function-POS gate then wrongly rejected 文化祭 and ひとりぼっち (mis-tagged by the ぼっち segmentation bug); い+た colliding with 板 "board" appears in nearly every past-progressive scene.
- 2026-06-30 — Te-form grouping was verified against 食べてしまった, 話しかけてくれる, 持っていった, 見てる and 読んでいる — 89 occurrences across 1236 lines (~7%) in the first batch run.
- 2026-07-03 — The first でしょう/だろう fix's gate (`isConjectureCopula`: `conjugated_form !== "基本形"`), which also caught でした/だった and turned a silent wrong entry into a lookup miss; caught by the corpus run (Pattern 2 lookup misses 43 → 38 once narrowed) — hand-picked sentences never included a past-tense copula.
- 2026-07-03 — せる/させる/れる/られる were listed in `PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` but had never been reachable (the check required `pos === "助動詞"`, and kuromoji tags them 動詞/接尾) — a pre-existing gap since the allowlist was written, not a regression; each had been separately clickable.
- 2026-07-05 — A QA report that してい (交換していた) and いいん (いいんだろう) were being fused turned out to be dual-view attachments cutting across baseline groups (して|いた; ん+だろ+う). The boundary-alignment rule also removed 60 other coincidental fuse matches across the corpus (397 → 337).
- 2026-07-05 — **チヤホヤ diagnosis corrected** (QA item D21): the report's "チヤ…ホヤ split by a mid-word ellipsis" was first read as a literal ellipsis, and a katakana-plus-ellipsis merge was built, corpus-validated to zero hits, and traced to the real line fetched fresh from Jimaku — チヤホヤされるんだ！, no ellipsis at all. The real cause was the katakana-name rule silencing the whole run because kuromoji tags チヤ alone as 固有名詞. The ellipsis mechanism was deleted, not kept as dead code; the un-suppression pass replaced it. A standing example of why a fix is validated against the real corpus before it's trusted.
- 2026-07-05 — Numerals: kuromoji tokenizes a half-width run as ONE token (`23` → one `"23"` token) but a full-width run digit by digit (`２３` → `"２"`+`"３"`), confirmed before building. 一人-style kanji counters needed nothing new — 一人 → ひとり already resolved through ordinary kanji lookup and the phrase-matcher's noun+noun fuse. `scripts/batch-test.js` Pattern 10: 36 real occurrences across the corpus, all resolved through existing JMdict entries; the reading synthesis was validated offline on 23/123/2024/9999/10001/0.
- 2026-07-05 — Name runs: kuromoji often recognizes only the surname half of a full name, the given-name half falling through as unrelated common kanji (伊地知虹夏 → 伊地知(固有名詞) + 虹 "rainbow" + 夏 "summer"); 後藤 itself always tokenizes as one token ("後藤-style" was a descriptive label). Pattern 11 (called on raw tokens, since the phrase-matcher's noun+noun fuse can produce the same group shape): exactly 2 firings in the corpus — 伊地知虹夏 (no entry, the intended fix) and 王都 (has an entry, a harmless overlap). The single-kanji gate kept 下北沢高校 split into its two real parts. Accepted risk: a place name + noun compound with no entry of its own could fuse; not observed in the corpus.
- 2026-07-05 — Katakana un-suppression, Pattern 8: 7/7 real corpus resolutions correct (アンデッド, エルフ ×2, ドワーフ, フフッ, フン, ディー).
- 2026-07-05 — **Colloquial contractions are a permanent limitation.** どした tokenizes as どし (kuromoji's IPADIC entry for どす, a rare Kyoto-ben copula, `連用形`) + た, so どす's only JMdict entry, "dagger", surfaces — a genuine homograph collision. どした → どうした needs a whole mora *inserted* with no anchor for where (unlike the potential-form fallback's fixed-position substitution); the contracted どやって tokenizes differently again (ど as its own 接頭詞 token); and そした/こした/ああした have their own unrelated real headwords, which a general reversal rule would override.
- 2026-07-06 — それもそうか itself has no JMdict headword, but its close variants do (それもそうだ, そういえば, そうだね, そうだよね, そっか) and already fuse correctly through the general phrase-matcher; それもそうか falls through to a two-part fuse (それも + そうか), both real, correctly defined entries.
- 2026-07-06 — Confirmed correct as designed: じゃない shows no inflection note (it resolves to its own fused headword, and notes are only generated for grouped constructions — same as んだ/からといって); だろ likewise resolves to its own standalone headword with no note.
- 2026-07-12 — の after an inflected form (なれないのだ) is tagged 名詞/非自立, not 助詞/終助詞 as after a plain verb (かけるの), confirmed by direct tokenizer testing; before the fix it skipped particle filtering and showed 野/幅・布/篦.
- 2026-07-13 — Ordering lesson: suppressing the trailing っ inside `groupTokens` pre-empted kana-merge's ん+っ → んっ, because kana-merge needs two adjacent un-suppressed hiragana groups; caught by the batch-test regression, fixed by running the suppression after kana-merge.
- 2026-07-13 — Confirmed correct as-is in the second Witch Hat Atelier round: お inside lexicalized words, て staying separate in the te-form, and 他にも's chunking.
- 2026-07-30 — `buildGroupsForText` was verified equivalent to the old callback chain by `scripts/test-render-pipeline.js`: the new function and a frozen copy of the old one over 794 real subtitle lines from two providers, compared field by field.
- 2026-08-15 — The first subtitle after a page refresh rendered as plain, unclickable text for its whole time on screen, because kuromoji was still loading; fixed (live status: Roadmap → Built, not verified live).
- 2026-09-18 — **Grunts looked up as verbs:** kuromoji reads a short kana grunt as a verb stem in 連用タ接続 (the form that exists only to take た/て), so `うっ` looked up 得る/打つ, `くっ` 来る/食う, `ふっ` 降る, `うわっ` a non-word "うわる", and `うう` (連用ゴザイ接続) the adjective 憂い — several thousand occurrences. Found by running a second analyzer (UniDic, via lindera) over the 233,963 corpus lines and comparing lemmas. Stutters (`ほ… ほかには`, `い… いや`, `ひっ… ひい～っ`) were resolving to ほる, いる, ひる. Cost accepted (Eric, 2026-09-20): roughly 100 corpus occurrences of a cut-off kana verb (`あたっ…`) now show "no entry" instead of the verb.
- **Accepted residual edge cases** (not scheduled): があん (a rare interjection collision), the たの-vs-のか tie-break, occasional harmless dual-view "noise" (Open questions), and この先's dual-view primacy (accepted 2026-07-04 — see Decisions).
- Open: ひとりも absorbing a character's name (Open questions).

**See also:** Dictionary data & lookup (what each group is looked up as); Popup display (how groups and notes are shown); Test & audit tooling (`batch-test.js` patterns, `test-parse-invariants.js`).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Tokenizing & grouping.

### Dictionary data & lookup

**What it does:** The offline-built dictionary the extension ships (`jmdict-compact.json`) and the lookup that turns a clicked group into the right entries, in the right order, with frequency and JLPT data attached.

**Key files:** `jmdict-compact.json`; `background.js` (`lookupWord`, `POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS`, the `LOOKUP_WORD` handler); `tokenize-utils.js` (`normalizeHalfwidthKatakana`, `collapseVowelElongation`, `derivePotentialFormBase`, `selectPotentialFormMatches`, `numberToReading`); build scripts `scripts/generate-jmdict-compact.js`, `scripts/fix-jmdict-priority.js`, `scripts/build-orphaned-tier-overrides.js` (→ `scripts/orphaned-tier-overrides.json`), `scripts/apply-tubelex-frequency.js`, `scripts/apply-jlpt-level.js` (with `scripts/jlpt-data/`)

**Invariants:**
- **Regeneration runs the whole pipeline, in order** — the sequence is in `CLAUDE.md` → Commands & testing. `apply-tubelex-frequency.js` must run after every base regeneration (and last among the first four scripts); skipping it shipped a real regression (2026-07-13).
- Every regeneration is validated field by field against the previous file before it's trusted. Ordering changes also need a live or manual spot-check, because `scripts/batch-test.js` doesn't check ordering.
- A lookup filter never narrows the results to zero (the "never narrow to zero" safety net).
- **Frequency tiers:** three text tiers (Common / Uncommon / Rare), no raw numbers, and no tier at all when there's no signal — absence of data isn't evidence of rarity. The thresholds are locked (2026-07-19) — don't re-derive or re-ask about them without a real reason to revisit.
- JLPT levels are data for an opt-in tag only — never an always-visible label, a filter or a sort (Scope).
- Normalizations that exist only to find an entry (small-vowel elongation) never change the displayed text.

**How it works now:**
- **Source:** JMdict via jmdict-simplified's JSON build (not raw EDRDG XML) — actively maintained (daily generation since mid-2006, 200k+ entries), CC BY-SA, commercial use explicitly permitted, attribution required, no open-source requirement on consuming software. The raw `jmdict-eng` release (`jmdict-eng-3.6.2`, 117MB) is too large to `JSON.parse` synchronously without stalling either extension context, so a trimmed `jmdict-compact.json` (~33.1MB) is built offline. The background worker loads and caches it; the content script sends a word and gets back entry objects plus `posTags`.
- **Format:** an index from every kanji and kana surface form to an entry index, an 83-code `posTags` map, and per entry:
  - `r` — the first kana reading; `rs` — all kana readings, only when there's more than one;
  - `g` — glosses **grouped by sense** (an array of arrays), up to 3 glosses per sense taken by position and de-duplicated against earlier senses without backfilling; a sense that loses every gloss to dedup is dropped;
  - `si` — sense-info notes (JMdict `s_inf`), a parallel array to `g`, one string per visible sense, `""` when absent, for any entry regardless of POS;
  - `p` — POS codes (jmdict-simplified's `partOfSpeech`, as a whole-entry deduped union); `c` — the `common` flag; `kp` — kana-primary tag (from `fix-jmdict-priority.js`);
  - `m` — the first included sense's own misc tags (arch/obs/dated…); `ki` — the primary reading's (`w.kana[0]`) own tags (ok/ik…);
  - `k` — the entry's kanji spellings (`w.kanji.map(k => k.text)`), excluding forms tagged rK/sK/oK/iK, omitted when empty;
  - `fr` — frequency tier (`"common"`/`"uncommon"`/`"rare"`), omitted when there's no signal; `id` — the JMdict `ent_seq`; `jlpt` — `"N5"`..`"N1"`.
- **Sense budget:** up to 3 *visible* senses for content words, and 8 for entries whose first glossed sense is tagged `prt`/`int` (`isFunctionWordPrimary()`). The budget (`generate-jmdict-compact.js`'s `selectSensesView`) is spent only on senses that actually render.
- **Per-reading views:** when an entry's senses are restricted to particular kana readings (`appliesToKana`) and a reading's view differs from the primary's, that reading gets its own filtered compact entry; readings with an identical view share the primary entry (no bloat for the ~99.6% unaffected case). 802 of 217,856 raw entries are affected. The symmetric `appliesToKanji` restriction (463 entries) is a known, unfixed gap — no live case has shown it producing a wrong card.
- **Homograph ordering** (the order of entries under a multi-entry index key):
  - `fix-jmdict-priority.js` — an entry whose kanji forms are all rK/sK-tagged (or which has none) is conventionally written in kana, so it ranks first for a kana-only key (居る "to be" above 射る/炒る/入る/要る/鋳る). Matched back to the compact file by a (reading, first gloss) fingerprint (`g[0]?.[0]`; 217,601 of 217,625 entries matched on 2026-07-01). This is now only the fallback tiebreak.
  - `apply-tubelex-frequency.js` re-ranks every multi-entry key by real usage frequency from TUBELEX-JA (BSD-3-Clause, derived from YouTube subtitles, 337,757 lemmas), with no raw-release re-download: the compact index already links every kanji spelling to the same entry object as its kana reading (`index["こと"]`, `index["事"]` and `index["琴"]` point to shared entries), so per-entry frequency comes from inverting it — the max TUBELEX count across the surface forms pointing at each entry — and no new field is needed. Ahead of raw frequency it checks: (1) a kana-only key must match the entry's own displayed reading (kanji keys are exempt, since TUBELEX lemmas are often kanji); (2) JMdict's own `common` flag; and a TUBELEX lemma only counts toward an entry if its UniDic POS class maps to the same coarse category as one of the entry's JMdict POS codes — leniently, so an unmapped category on either side is allowed through.
- **Frequency tiers (`fr`):** absolute TUBELEX counts — score ≥ 500 → common, ≥ 20 → uncommon, ≥ 1 → rare. Tiers are assigned after the index re-sort, and a kanji key's score counts for an entry only if the re-sort ranked that entry first for that exact spelling. **Orphaned entries** — 7,737 that JMdict marks `common:true` but TUBELEX has no data for, because TUBELEX's tokenizer doesn't lemmatize multi-morpheme expressions (でしょう/かもしれない/おかね/おにいさん) as units — are tiered by `build-orphaned-tier-overrides.js` into `scripts/orphaned-tier-overrides.json` (checked in, ~180KB), consumed by `apply-tubelex-frequency.js`: first by real occurrence counts in the batch-test corpus (Bocchi ep1, Frieren ep1, One Piece ep894), ranked within the orphaned set; otherwise by the raw EDRDG `JMdict_e.xml`'s news/ichi/spec/gai/nf priority tags, pulled with a scoped regex over only `<ent_seq>`/`<ke_pri>`/`<re_pri>` (not a switch to XML). A bounded keyword check (kanji substrings like 党/省/議会/選挙, gloss keywords like "ministry"/"election") demotes a political/institutional entry one tier. Popup badge: Popup display; Anki field: Anki export: cards & capture; toggle: Storage & settings.
- **JLPT (`jlpt`):** `apply-jlpt-level.js` joins `stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab`'s `original_data/*.csv` (in this repo as `scripts/jlpt-data/`, CC BY-SA 4.0) on `id` — the JMdict `ent_seq` every row already carries. That data is a conversion of Jonathan Waller's JLPT Resources vocabulary list, the one Jisho.org uses. It tags 7,909 of 218,859 entries; the ~450 of ~8,300 source rows that share an id with a row on a different level resolve to the easier level. No ordering dependency on the other scripts; run last by convention. The source's own caveat carries over: no official JLPT vocabulary list has existed since 2010, so this is a community "educated guess". Attribution is not yet shown anywhere user-facing (Open questions). Popup badge: Popup display; Anki field: Anki export: cards & capture; toggle: Storage & settings.
- **`lookupWord` (`background.js`)** — every lookup passes through one point, which normalizes half-width katakana (`normalizeHalfwidthKatakana`, U+FF66–FF9F) and ASCII digits to full width, then filters the index hits:
  - a particle (`isParticle`) keeps only `prt` entries, preferring an entry reached through its own *primary* reading over one reached through an alternate (bare て, not quotative って, which lists て as a contraction);
  - an honorific suffix (`isHonorificSuffix`) keeps only `suf` entries;
  - otherwise the kuromoji POS category is mapped to JMdict POS codes through `POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS` — including the sentinels `__phraseFuse` (`POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS["__phraseFuse"]` → `exp`/`int`, for phrase-fused groups) and `__utterance` (the strict interjection filter for grunts);
  - none of these may narrow the result to zero;
  - when the looked-up word matches one of the entry's `rs` readings, the returned `r` is swapped to it without mutating the shared cached entry (くる showing くる, not 刳る's primary えぐる).
- **Fallbacks, only after a direct miss:** `collapseVowelElongation` (a small vowel after its own large counterpart, おぉ → おお; lookup only); godan potential-form reverse conjugation (`derivePotentialFormBase`/`selectPotentialFormMatches`: final e-row kana → u-row, 輝ける → 輝く, filtered to `v5*` entries, preferring an irregular `vk` match when one shares the derived key); and numerals — full-width index keys first (JMdict has 0–9, most round tens/hundreds/thousands and 10000), then `numberToReading`, a closed algorithm: base-10000 grouping via 万/億/兆/京, the real sound changes for 百/千 (さんびゃく/ろっぴゃく/はっぴゃく/さんぜん/はっせん), and 万/億/兆 keeping an explicit いち where 十/百 drop it.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- **Permanent ordering limitations** (see Decisions): お's "oh!" interjection sense (zero TUBELEX data; `kp`-tagged but not `common`, so it loses to お's common-flagged tail/cord/honorific-prefix senses); 入る (TUBELEX collapses いる and はいる into one kanji lemma with one count); まま (four genuine homograph entries, none with TUBELEX data, leaving only array position); kanji heteronyms such as 一行 (いっこう vs いちぎょう). Also: ふ has no `int`-tagged entry in the raw release at all — an upstream JMdict gap, nothing to fix locally. Whether あ's interjection ordering is right has never been re-checked (Open questions).
- **File size history:** 27MB → ~29MB when POS codes, the common flag and `posTags` were added (2026-06-24); 30,772,667 bytes after the first TUBELEX pass (2026-07-03, unchanged by it); ~30.8MB → ~33.1MB with the 2026-07-05 rebuild (new fields on ~1 in 6 entries); the `id` field added ~1.7MB (2026-07-19).
- 2026-07-01 — jmdict-simplified's JSON exposes only a collapsed `common: boolean`, not the priority tier (ichi1/ichi2, newsN, nfXX); its `tags` carry only reading-form annotations (ateji, gikun, rK/rk/sK/sk, oK/ok, iK/ik). Confirmed against the raw `jmdict-eng-3.6.2.json`, re-confirmed 2026-07-19. The raw XML does carry priority tags.
- 2026-07-01 — Particles have JMdict entries tagged `"prt"` (the filter is `r.p?.includes("prt")`), while their homophone nouns (に→荷, は→歯, な→菜) don't — which is what the particle filter keys on (built 2026-06-30).
- 2026-07-01 — Half-width katakana (U+FF66–FF9F, from releases like VCB-Studio) had been clickable since the 2026-06-30 `ｦ-ﾟ` range was added to `JAPANESE_WORD_RE`, but not *resolvable*: the index is full-width only, so every half-width click had silently shown "no entry" since Phase 3.
- 2026-07-01 — The kuromoji-POS → JMdict-POS category filter (built that day at Eric's direction, reusing the particle-filter pattern) only helps cross-category collisions (こと's particle sense vs its noun senses), not same-category ones.
- 2026-07-03 — **TUBELEX's first pass** scored 59,703 of 217,625 entries and reordered 6,624 keys (こと: 事 "thing/matter" 1,105,160 vs 琴 "zither" 372), and fixed 大事なことさ's な showing だ+た as equal cards. A same-day follow-up fixed cross-reading contamination — taking the max across every key let a rare sense borrow an unrelated common word's frequency through a shared reading (そうか's "Buddhist temple" sense borrowing 宗家; だ outranking だ-as-copula). Excluding kanji keys outright had collapsed coverage from 59,703 to 9,450 entries. Re-validated at 59,011 scored and 2,999 reordered, with the 6 batch-test patterns unchanged (89/38/53/24/397/125). An automated MeCab-based frequency tool can fail to lemmatize a casual multi-morpheme interjection like そうか at all, so "no data" isn't the same as "rare".
- 2026-07-05 — The POS gate was needed because TUBELEX's one "ね" lemma row aggregates essentially all ね usage under the sentence-final particle, which 子's ね-reading entry (the zodiac sign, `n`) was inheriting; after the gate, 57,596 of 217,625 entries were scored and 519 keys reordered.
- 2026-07-03 — The multi-reading "index corruption" hypothesis (~2,500–10,500 entries) was wrong: every sampled case was a real multi-reading word; ~11.2% involve a common entry. String similarity doesn't separate the classes (でけえ/でかい scored *lower* than the bug case くる/えぐる).
- 2026-07-05 — **The compact file was rebuilt from scratch.** The original 2026-06-24 generation script was never saved, so `scripts/generate-jmdict-compact.js` was reverse-engineered against the existing file and a fresh `jmdict-eng-3.6.2` download, and validated statistically before being trusted: 99.96%+ exact match on `r`/`g`/`p` and 99.98%+ on `c` across 217k+ entries, the residual traced to version drift between release snapshots (genuinely different gloss text). The full pipeline then changed the top entry for 82 of 17,512 multi-entry keys (0.47%), every sampled change a legitimate frequency-driven correction (軍: いくさ → ぐん), none a regression. 子's ね/こ order came out right (raw JMdict lists こ first).
- 2026-07-05 — `rs` was verified as a general fix, not case-specific, directly against the raw release (the first check had been circular): 37,514 unambiguous multi-reading words (84,111 readings) cross-checked, 0 missing and 0 wrong; ~46,600 (index key, entry) pairs needed it dictionary-wide (above the ~37,000 first estimated, likely from an earlier snapshot), all now correct. Spot checks: ふなばし/せんきょう (3-way), たまう/たもう, わたし/ワタシ, わい/ワイ (a genuine unrelated second entry correctly left alone), こう (14-way).
- 2026-07-05 — The potential-form fallback exists because kuromoji gives no conjugation signal for 輝ける. The first version picked the wrong sense for 来れる (ら抜き of 来る/くる), which reverse-derives to the same kanji key as the unrelated godan verb きたる ("next, forthcoming"); Pattern 9 went from 7/8 to 8/8 correct once `vk` was preferred. The "oh!" interjection entry lists only おお/おう/おー/オー as readings, never おぉ.
- 2026-07-06 — The third regeneration (adding `k`, grouping `g` by sense and raising the per-sense gloss cap to 3) was validated across all 462,753 index keys: 100% match on `r`/`p`/`c`/`m`/`ki`/`rs`; every gloss difference (20.8% of keys) came from the cap bump, except 0.07% where a gloss the old 2-cap had hidden now attributes to its true earlier sense (benign). `fix-jmdict-priority.js`'s fingerprint moved from `g[0]` to `g[0]?.[0]` and lost 0 of 217,674 matches; batch-test's patterns stayed at 89/38/53/24/336/105/7/8/36/2.
- 2026-07-06 — `k` filtering, real counts in the raw release: sK 14831, rK 5732, iK 604, oK 538; `k` shrank (never grew) on 52,729 of 462,753 keys. 来る's `k` is now just `["来る"]` (來る is sK); じゃない's `k` is omitted (じゃ無い and ぢゃ無い are both sK); いる's "to be" sense (居る, rK) loses its kanji badge too. `rs` isn't displayed anywhere (a full `content.js` grep), so it was left unfiltered.
- 2026-07-06 — Making `m`/`ki` primary-only narrowed them on 8,414 and 16,587 index keys respectively, never adding a tag. Residual, unbuilt: if a user clicks an alternate reading (via the `rs` swap) that is itself archaic while the primary isn't, the badge won't reflect it — no real case has turned up.
- 2026-07-06 — The `__phraseFuse` sentinel narrowed exactly 11 of 181 distinct real fuse-outcome words in the 3-show corpus, all correctly (そうか → just "is that so?", おい → "hey!", よし → "alright"; every dropped entry an unrelated homograph noun); 84 have no `exp`/`int` entry and are left alone by the safety net. It discriminates by context: なんで is one natural 副詞 token in "why?" and keeps its `adv` filtering, while the fuse-requiring 恩人なんで (like だから) resolves to "because". The underlying gap — phrase-fused groups never had a `pos`, so the category filter never ran on any of them — was probably older than the kanji-spelling display that exposed it.
- 2026-07-12 — Live bugs from the first Witch Hat Atelier round, each verified against a downloaded raw release: よ (JMdict id 2029090, 4 senses) wasted a sense slot on a fully deduplicated "hey" and never reached its distinct 4th (`int`) sense; な/なあ/なー/なぁ share entry 2029110, whose "don't"/"do" senses are tagged `appliesToKana: ["な"]` but showed on なあ; bare て lost to quotative って. Confirmed correct as-is: ただ's kanji spellings; 編み's absence is a genuine JMdict gap.
- 2026-07-13 — **The sense cap, measured:** content words have an effectively unbounded tail of real senses (落ちる 27, する 17; 273 *common* entries exceed 6), while only 806 entries are particle/interjection-primary, and a cap of 8 fully covers 802 of them (99.5%) — including the two live cases (の's sentence-final question sense at position 6; ただ今's "right away" at 4). The four outliers accepted: か 15 senses, が 10, に 9, quotative って 9.
- 2026-07-13 — **Regression caught before shipping:** regenerating the base file and re-running only `fix-jmdict-priority.js` reverted こと/僕/くる to pre-TUBELEX ordering (each ranking a rare or untagged entry above a far more common one). Caught by live-test feedback, not by `batch-test.js`, whose pattern counts don't check ordering.
- 2026-07-13 — さん/さま/くん/ちゃん/様/氏 share their bare kana with unrelated nouns (さん → 酸 "acid", 三 "three", 讃 "praise"), which the noun-category filter can't exclude — hence the `suf` filter.
- 2026-07-15 — `s_inf` sampled across the raw release before building: notes are far more common on `prt` (62.8%) and `int` (23.3%) senses than on nouns (1.5%), verbs (3–6%) or adjectives (2–7%), but content-word notes are typically substantive — らしい's "after the plain form of a verb or adjective…", あう's "逢う is often used for close friends… 遭う may have an undesirable nuance" — not register/dialect information the archaic badge already covers. Only 6,668 of 252,198 senses in the whole release carry one (~160KB of text).
- 2026-07-19 — Orphaned-entry detection: the first proxy (words whose reading fragments into 2+ kuromoji tokens) was false — after fixing a check that compared only kana while TUBELEX lemmas are often kanji, 61.3% of *all* content-word entries fragment regardless of TUBELEX coverage (お客様 → お+客+様). Every one of the 7,737 orphaned entries carries a raw priority tag (jmdict-simplified's `common` is itself derived from tag presence), content words being the larger share. nf priority is 1990s newspaper frequency — the formal bias TUBELEX was chosen to avoid — and the spot-check proved it: nf01-ranked orphans included 委員会/外務省/共産党/総選挙/自民党; the keyword check demoted 118 of 7,737.
- 2026-07-19 — **A tier-label bug caught in testing:** the raw per-entry score trusts any kanji-key match by design, so 僕's archaic readings やつがれ/やつこ inherited ぼく's huge score and were nearly tagged "Common". Index *order* was already immune (the common flag wins that tiebreak), the new tier *label* wasn't — hence tiering after the re-sort. Threshold boundary words: the chosen scheme's common/uncommon boundary (悉く, ちょくちょく, ぶら下がる) against looser schemes' (カッと, ぎょっと). Confirmed live the same day: real words in all three tiers, in both the popup badge and the Anki Frequency field.
- 2026-07-22 — JLPT: Waller's list tags different *spellings* of one JMdict entry at different levels (会う/あう N5, the same entry's 遭う N2), which a per-entry join can't keep. Spot checks 食べる→N5, 経済→N4, 憂鬱→N1; the batch-test corpus showed zero regressions (all patterns unchanged). Confirmed working live 2026-07-22.

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Dictionary data & lookup.

### Popup display

**What it does:** What a click on a word shows — headword and reading, numbered senses with notes, part-of-speech and inflection lines, archaic and metadata badges — and the "last looked-up word" chip the popup turns into when the line moves on.

**Key files:** `content.js` (`onWordClick`, `renderEntries`, `buildHeadword`, `POS_LABELS`, `posLabel`, `formatPosChips`, `IRREGULAR_VERB_SELF_CODES`, `INFLECTION_LABELS`, `describeInflection`, `archaicTagLabel`, `isArchaicVerbType`, `NO_KANJI_RE`, `chipifyPopup`, `positionChip`, `removeChip`), `content.css` (`.jp-immersion-popup-*`)

**Invariants:**
- **Show what was actually on screen as primary:** the clicked surface is the big headword; a kana click gets a card's kanji spelling only as a small annotation, and a kanji click gets furigana.
- The POS chip line, the frequency badge and the JLPT badge appear only when their opt-in toggle is on, and all are off by default. JLPT is never an always-visible label, a filter or a sort.
- No frequency badge when there's no frequency data.
- Every label is sourced: an archaic/dated/obsolete badge comes from JMdict's own tags (or a verb class JMdict itself labels archaic). A duplicate with no such tag is demoted, never given an invented label, and nothing project-authored explains grammar or idioms.

**How it works now:**
- `onWordClick` captures the sentence context synchronously (Anki export: cards & capture) and sends `LOOKUP_WORD`; `renderEntries` draws up to three entries. A miss shows "no dictionary entry" — kanji names included.
- **Headword:** `buildHeadword` shows ruby furigana whenever `word !== reading` (not just when the word contains kanji, which was only ever a proxy), so a numeral shows its reading (23 → にじゅうさん). The `<ruby>` headword (`.jp-immersion-popup-reading`) uses `ruby-position: over` and still works as a flex item. Furigana is group/jukugo ruby — the whole reading over the whole compound; per-kanji mono-ruby isn't built (Roadmap, Phase 6).
- **Kanji spellings:** when the clicked word has no kanji of its own (`NO_KANJI_RE`) and resolves to several entries (いる → 居る/入る/要る), each card shows its `k` spelling(s), joined with "・", next to its header — otherwise the cards would be identical kana headers.
- **Senses:** numbered (1., 2., 3.) from `g`'s sense groups; a sense may show fewer than three glosses because some JMdict senses have fewer. A sense note (`si`) renders as a muted parenthetical (`.jp-immersion-popup-gloss-note`, 12px, #777, like the POS line) appended *inside* the gloss span, so long notes (p90 ~47 characters, max 181) wrap as ordinary text within the popup's 320px max-width, which has no max-height or overflow clipping.
- **POS chips:** `POS_LABELS`/`posLabel()`/`formatPosChips()` turn JMdict codes into short labels at every render site (`n` → "Noun", `vs` → "する-verb", every `v5*` → "Godan verb"), with no kokugo-grammar parentheticals ((futsuumeishi), (keiyoushi)), no redundant "(common)" on the default noun, at most 3 chips, and one line per entry rather than per sense (`p` is a whole-entry union). `n-pr` (proper noun) has a label but is unreachable, since proper nouns live in JMnedict, which isn't ingested. `IRREGULAR_VERB_SELF_CODES` hides "Kuru verb"/"する-verb" only when くる/する (or 来る/為る) *is* the headword, where it would just restate it; it stays for a compound (持ってくる) or noun + する (凱旋, tagged `vs` rather than `vs-i`/`vs-s`/`vs-c`, so unaffected either way). The toggle gates only the visible chip line; `p` is still used internally (chip formatting, the archaic check).
- **Inflection line** (`INFLECTION_LABELS`/`describeInflection()`): native-morpheme labels, each with a short plain-English parenthetical (て-form "(connective)", ない-form "(negative)"), and no line at all when there's nothing to show. It covers -ば; られる/れる (passive); せる/させる (causative); -たら/-だら (conditional); contracted てる/てた/てく/てき; なく (ない's connective form); the copula by what precedes it — noun + だ "(copula)", noun + です "(polite copula)", い-adjective + です "(polite)" only; noun + plain copula groupings as "+ です (copula)" (which relies on the `conjugatedForm` carried on every Rule-3 group, also needed to detect the imperative 命令形, which never appears in `inflections`); な "(attributive)"; んだ "(explanatory)" / んです "(explanatory, polite)"; んだろう "(explanatory, conjecture)" / んでしょう "(explanatory, conjecture, polite)"; the い-adjective く-form "(adverbial)" (うまく in うまくなる); the honorific-prefix and contraction notes from grouping; and one combined chain — れる/られる/せる/させる + ない/なく (信じられない → `["られ", "ない"]` → "られる/れる (passive) + ない-form (negative)"), scoped to that family, not a general chain composer.
- **Duplicates and archaic badges:** any entry after the first whose first gloss (`g[0]`) exactly matches one already shown is a duplicate. `archaicTagLabel()` runs on *every* entry, checking the entry's `m` (arch/obs/dated, in that priority), then `ki` (ok/ik), then — as a fallback — `isArchaicVerbType()` (any `v4*`/`v2*` code; JMdict labels every one "(archaic)"), and shows one small badge from whichever fires first. A duplicate with no such signal is visually demoted (`.jp-immersion-popup-entry-demoted`, reduced opacity and size) instead; a non-duplicate with no signal is an ordinary card.
- **Dual-view** phrase matches appear as an "Also, as a set phrase" note beneath the individual words' definitions.
- **Badges:** the frequency tier (`fr`) behind `metaShowFreq`, still styled as the plain pill of the old always-on common-word badge, which it replaced entirely — tier colours are undesigned; the JLPT level (`jlpt`) behind `metaShowJlpt`. Show/episode source has no popup badge (it's redundant while watching that episode).
- Each card has its own "+ Anki" button, so for a homograph the user picks exactly which reading and sense to save (Anki export: cards & capture).
- **Lookup chip:** the popup still dismisses when the next cue arrives, but if it had finished loading real content, `chipifyPopup()` restyles it into a small chip anchored to the `<video>`'s live bounding rect (recomputed on resize and `fullscreenchange`), showing word + reading. Hovering reveals the same body content — "+ Anki" included — through a CSS-only toggle on `.jp-immersion-popup-body`. One chip at a time (`removeChip()` on the next lookup), living 15 seconds after the popup would have closed. (The chip shown after a card is *sent* is a different one: Anki export: capture chip & edit panel.)
- Visual polish is deliberately minimal; the popup redesign is Phase 6.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-03 — The furigana headword had `display: block`, which overrides the browser's native ruby layout that centres `<rt>` over the base; `ruby-position: over` fixed it. Flex items are blockified regardless, so it still sits correctly in its row.
- 2026-07-05 — Duplicate-gloss handling was validated on real cases: 酒 and 君 share an identical first gloss with another card (so exact matching suffices); 酒's ささ reading carries "dated"; あたし carries only register tags (`fem`/`uk`) and is demoted, not falsely tagged; わらわ (also "I; me") carries `arch` and shows "archaic".
- 2026-07-06 — 呉る (a くる homograph, `v2r-s`) and こかす (a 倒す homograph, `v4s`) still showed no badge after the verb-class fix, because the tag check ran only for duplicates and neither shares a first gloss with its siblings (来る/繰る; 倒す). 捧ぐ (ささぐ, `v2g-s`) has no `m` tag and had been silently demoted despite its own POS chip reading "archaic". Every `v4*`/`v2*` code was checked in the full `posTags` map, not a sample.
- 2026-07-06 — Confirmed working as designed in the live rounds: numbered senses with fewer than three glosses (こと's sense 1 shows 2); the kanji-spelling placement; and archaic/dated terms showing no frequency badge (such terms essentially never have TUBELEX data).
- 2026-07-06 — The raw release has kanji for いる's first three senses (射る/炒る・煎る・熬る/入る), which the compact file used to drop; 考える's old `g` was one 6-item flat array across 3 senses with no marker between them.
- 2026-07-12 — られない fell through to the raw, untranslated join "られない" because no branch handled a two-part chain.
- 2026-07-15 — よ's first sense showed a bare "hey; you", with no sign it's the sentence-final particle of certainty/emphasis — the note existed in JMdict (`s_inf`) but was never carried into the compact file (checked by reading `generate-jmdict-compact.js`).
- 2026-07-19 — 待つ's badge looked unchanged after the frequency tiers shipped — expected: same green pill, and 待つ is "common", the tier the old binary flag would have shown.
- なら (だ's conditional 仮定形) still has the labelling gap な had — flagged, not fixed, not asked for.

**See also:** Dictionary data & lookup (the fields shown here); Storage & settings (the toggles); Subtitle overlay, sync & offset (fullscreen re-parenting of the popup and chip).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Popup display.

### English subtitles

**What it does:** Shows Crunchyroll's own English translation under the Japanese line, paired to the Japanese cue on screen, and supplies the Anki `Translation` field.

**Key files:** `caption-url-sniffer.js` (page-world script), `background.js` (`fetchEnglishSubtitles`, `isTrustedCrunchyrollCdnUrl`), `content.js` (`pairEnglishCues`, `dropBackgroundEnglishCues`, `updateEnglishCue`, `bridgedEnglishText`, `JP_GAP_BRIDGE_SECONDS`, `resetEnglishCaptions`, `renderedJpWindow`), `content.css` (`#jp-immersion-subtitle-en`)

**Invariants:**
- **Passive only:** the caption URL is read from the `play` response Crunchyroll's own player fetches; the extension never calls Crunchyroll's API itself — dubs included.
- `background.js` fetches a caption file only from an HTTPS `*.crunchyrollcdn.com` host (`isTrustedCrunchyrollCdnUrl`), because any script on the page could forge the message carrying the URL.
- `pairEnglishCues` is the single source of truth for which English cues translate a Japanese cue window — the on-screen line and the Anki `Translation`/sentence merge use it alike, so they can't disagree.
- English shows only while Japanese shows, and never before the Japanese line has been painted.
- English cues are cleared only on an episode change, never by the Japanese loader.
- English cues are timed against the raw `video.currentTime` — the manual offset is for community files and would misalign Crunchyroll's own, already-synced captions.
- Diagnostics are unconditional and go to the console, never to on-screen UI.
- *Decided, not built:* a sign, typesetting or on-screen-text cue never becomes the English line or the `Translation`, never drives the sentence merge and never widens the audio clip (Roadmap, next up).

**How it works now:**
- **Source:** the `play` response (Crunchyroll's own playback-session call) carries a `subtitles` object — and a `captions` object — keyed by locale (e.g. `"en-US"`), each entry giving a `format` and a time-signed CDN URL to a raw caption file (ASS so far, parsed by the same `parseAss()` as Jimaku files). This internal API is undocumented.
- **Sniffer:** `caption-url-sniffer.js` runs in the page's MAIN world at `document_start`, overriding `window.fetch` and `XMLHttpRequest.prototype.send`. It recognizes the response by its JSON *shape* (a `subtitles` or `captions` object whose locale keys each carry a `url`), never by request path, so an endpoint rename doesn't break it. It reads `subtitles ?? captions` and prefers `en-US`, then any `en*`. The URL crosses to the isolated world by `window.postMessage`, stamped with the pathname it was captured on. The sniffer caches the last URL and replays it when `content.js` (which loads later, at `document_idle`) sends a handshake; a replay for the current pathname is accepted and one from the previous episode discarded. (The same script also captures sibling episode titles — Episode & file selection.)
- **Fetch:** `background.js` downloads the file behind the host allowlist (the real signed host is `vod-fy-mod.crunchyrollcdn.com`) and parses it; the dual-language strip is never applied to English captions.
- **Diagnostics:** the sniffer logs locale, format and host on a hit; warns with the available locale keys when the shape matches but there's no English entry; and warns on any non-ASS format, because a non-ASS track is parsed by `parseSrt` and WebVTT cues with position settings would silently never show (gap PA20 in `docs/audit/failure-taxonomy.md`, pinned as an expected-fail check in `scripts/test-subtitle-parser.js`). `background.js` names a rejected hostname; `content.js` logs the loaded cue count; and `window.__jpImmersionSnifferStats()` (page console) reports whether the overrides see page traffic at all — telling "never saw the response" (e.g. a request made from a Worker, whose separate global scope the overrides never touch) from "saw it, didn't recognise it".
- **Reset:** `resetEnglishCaptions` runs on `jp-immersion-locationchange` only.
- **Pairing (`pairEnglishCues(window)`):** an English cue belongs to a Japanese cue window if the English cue is centred inside the window, or the window's centre falls inside the English cue — parameter-free. That keeps 1:1, one Japanese line to two English sentences (both shown) and two Japanese lines to one English sentence. `dropBackgroundEnglishCues` then drops a top-positioned cue (`\an8`, read through `parseAss`'s `assAlignment`) only when a bottom-positioned one competes for the same moment; a lone top-positioned cue is dialogue placed away from a speaker at the bottom of frame and stays. Because it runs inside `pairEnglishCues`, the screen and the `Translation` field make the same call — and it also un-blocks the sentence merge, which refuses when a window pairs with several English cues.
- **Timing:** `updateEnglishCue` runs unconditionally on every `timeupdate` tick (from `handleTimeUpdate`, not behind the Japanese half's early return), and pairs against `renderedJpWindow` — the window whose text is actually painted, reported by `renderCue`'s completion callback — rather than `activeJpWindow` (what the clock says should be showing).
- **Gap bridging:** `bridgedEnglishText()` uses `japaneseDisplayAt` to look at the Japanese cues either side of a gap, and holds the English line across a gap shorter than `JP_GAP_BRIDGE_SECONDS` (1.0s) only when both flanking cues pair to the same English text — so a split sentence's English doesn't blink as the Japanese line changes beneath it. Display-only; the Anki merge is unaffected.
- **Per audio version** (live probe 2026-09-22, `scripts/probe-play-captions.js`, data in `fixtures/captions/caption-probe-2026-09-23.json`): on the Japanese-audio version, `subtitles.en-US` is the full translation (KonoSuba 3 ep 6: 395 ASS events, dialogue plus signs) and `captions` holds nothing. On the English dub, `subtitles.en-US` is signs and on-screen text only (4 events: the title card, one sign, the next-episode preview text and title), and `captions.en-US` is a 585-cue WebVTT transcript of the dub's own English script — never read, since the sniffer takes `subtitles ?? captions`. Both versions share one timeline (the signs carry identical timestamps; the title card runs 0:15.99–0:20.47). The response also carries `audioLocale` (`ja-JP` / `en-US`), seen on these two responses only. Every English track read so far has been ASS.
- **Dubs:** Japanese subtitles keep loading over English audio; the `Translation` should be empty there because the dub's English track is signs only — which depends on the sign-cue rule, not yet built.
- **Status:** working end to end since 2026-07-26 (Frieren ep 7); pairing confirmed live 2026-07-31; checklist F8 passed 2026-09-22 (each audio version loaded its own English track). Unverified parts: Roadmap → Built, not verified live; the `\an8` premise and re-checking pairing after the sign-cue change are open questions.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- Permanently dependent on Crunchyroll's undocumented `play` response: if its shape changes, English silently stops appearing (no error) — Open questions.
- 2026-07-23 — **Why the English text can't be read from the page** (six live fact-finding rounds, all negative): `video.textTracks.length` was 0 and no `<track>` elements exist with English captions on, so there are no native caption tracks for `cue.text` to read; a class/id-name search found only this extension's own `jp-immersion-subtitle` box (Crunchyroll's classes are likely generated); a document-wide MutationObserver caught only the scrubber timestamp (`data-testid="trickplay-timestamp"`), suggesting a shadow DOM (shadow roots don't bubble mutations to an observer on `document.body`, even with `subtree: true`); a shadow-root-aware observer found exactly 1 open shadow root and no caption text; `document.querySelectorAll('canvas').length` was 0; the DevTools element picker — even with the extension disabled — kept selecting a full-size transparent `player-controls-root` hit-target div above everything; and a full-text DOM search (Cmd+F in Elements) for words visible on screen returned 0-of-0 every time. Network-tab keyword filters (`m3u8`/`subtitle`/`caption`/`vtt`) matched nothing either; an unfiltered look at Fetch/XHR during a fresh load is what surfaced the `play` request.
- 2026-07-23 — **Why not English from Jimaku:** some fansub releases bundle both languages as separately styled lines in one `.ass` (SubsPlease's `..._ja-en.ass`, each language independently timed within ~100–200ms), but not universally (KitaujiSub's `[CHS, JPN].ass` pairs Japanese with Chinese; others have no second track), and an independent English Jimaku fetch would need its own fetch/rank/offset-sync pipeline with no guaranteed timing match to the Japanese file.
- 2026-07-26 — The pipeline had never actually run: the first live round's on-screen English came from a dual-language Jimaku file. Three defects were found and fixed that day, among them a `play` response resolving between `document_start` and `document_idle` being lost with no retry, and `loadSubtitles` clearing a correctly loaded 317-cue set before it could render.
- 2026-07-26 — JP/EN desync: `updateEnglishCue` sat behind the Japanese half's `if (text === lastText) return`, so an English cue starting or ending mid-line appeared late, lingered or never rendered. Simulated at Chrome's real ~4Hz tick over deliberately misaligned boundaries, the old arrangement showed the wrong English on 14 of 29 ticks and the current one on 0 of 29 (and staggered ticks fell from 5/51 to 0/51).
- 2026-07-26 — The first pairing test (`overlap / duration >= 0.25`, either side) stacked two unrelated English sentences when a Japanese cue's tail clipped the next English line. One Japanese line legitimately translating as two English sentences was measured at 6.4% of Japanese cues across four episodes.
- 2026-07-27 — A show switch destroyed English cues already loaded for the new episode, because the pathname poll notices navigation up to a second after Crunchyroll has fetched and the sniffer has posted.
- 2026-07-27 — The first subtitle after a page load or alt-tab appeared in English before Japanese: the signature of a cold MV3 service worker, which the Japanese path needs (up to four `sendMessage` round trips per line) and the English path doesn't.
- 2026-07-29 — Measured 14,742 gaps across 24 real files: no trough in the distribution (78% under 100ms, then smooth decay), and it varies more by provider than by show — 43% vs 78% sub-100ms on two releases of the *same* episode. `JP_GAP_BRIDGE_SECONDS` = 1.0s was confirmed by eye on real footage on 2026-07-31.
- 2026-07-31 — "English only while Japanese is showing" confirmed working and not distracting, including the mirror case (a Japanese line like んっ with no English shows Japanese alone).
- 2026-08-15 — A capture's `Translation` carried an unrelated background line ("Move your feet.") beside its own sentence. **Unverified premise:** no real Crunchyroll caption file has been inspected for `\an8` on such a line. The first real file seen (2026-09-22) puts KonoSuba 3 ep 6's 26 top-of-screen dialogue lines in a `Konosuba_Main_Top` *style* with no override tag, so the override-only check sees none of them, and its title card carries `\an3` (bottom); the style's own alignment value wasn't captured.
- 2026-09-22 — The KonoSuba 3 ep 6 title card (bottom-right) pairs by timing with whatever Japanese line is spoken under it — the reason for the sign-cue rule.
- 2026-09-23 — Measured offline (the real `content.js` pairing functions, the caption fixture's English events, the live Jimaku `…S03E06.2024.1080p.CR.WEB-DL` Japanese file, offset 0): on the Japanese-audio version the title card is the first of four English lines paired with 「ねえ　カズマ…」 (16.50–19.84), so it lands in `Translation`, and `dropBackgroundEnglishCues` keeps all four because the three `Konosuba_Main_Top` lines carry no override tag. On the dub the same line pairs with the title card alone, and 「うん」 (17:21) pairs with the `KONOSUBA` sign. Neither merges in that file, but a sign centred across two Japanese cues would anchor a merge and widen the clip.

**See also:** Subtitle overlay, sync & offset (the English box's layout); Anki export: cards & capture (the `Translation` field and the split-sentence merge).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → English subtitles.

### Anki export: cards & capture

**What it does:** Sends a clicked word — with its sentence, translation and any opted-in metadata — straight into the user's own Anki as a sentence-first card, instantly, with Undo.

**Key files:** `background.js` (`invokeAnkiConnect`, `ensureAnkiSetup`, `addAnkiNote`, `ANKI_MODEL_FIELDS`, `ANKI_BACK_TEMPLATE`), `content.js` (`onWordClick`, `renderGroups`, `buildSentenceHtml`, `buildMergedAnkiSentence`, `formatShowEpisode`, `currentShowEpisode`, the "+ Anki" button)

**Invariants:**
- Capture sends immediately on click — no pre-send edit step — with Undo (and the edit panel) afterwards.
- The sentence context is captured synchronously at click time, before any async work, so a line change can't swap it.
- **Bolding is markup, never a text edit:** the recorded offset is only a hint, verified against the text before use; the fallbacks are a search and then no bolding. The sentence's own characters can't change under any input.
- `Translation` is on the back only — on the front it would give away the answer before the recall attempt.
- A capture undone before its audio lands is never written to again (tracked by note id, not by "latest card").
- An opted-out metadata field is sent as an empty string, not omitted, so every note has every field.
- The split-sentence merge falls back to the single displayed line whenever anything is ambiguous, so its worst case is the old behaviour.
- Field text is HTML-escaped and newlines converted: no raw `\n`, no unescaped `<`/`&`.

**How it works now:**
- **AnkiConnect**, the local server at `http://127.0.0.1:8765`, called with `fetch()` from the background worker — the same shape as the Jimaku calls. `invokeAnkiConnect(action, params)` posts AnkiConnect's `{action, version, params}` and throws on either a network failure (Anki not open, or AnkiConnect not installed) or a non-null `error` field, with distinct messages so the UI can tell "Anki isn't running" from "Anki rejected this request". Anki desktop must be running at capture time.
- **Setup:** `ensureAnkiSetup()` checks `deckNames`/`modelNames` first, then idempotently creates the "Japanese Immersion" deck and its dedicated note type (`createDeck`/`createModel`), adds any field missing from an existing note type (`modelFieldAdd`), and reapplies the current card template on every call — to the note type's *actual* first template name.
- **Fields** (`ANKI_MODEL_FIELDS`): Word, Reading, Gloss, Sentence, POS, Frequency, JLPT, Source, Audio, Translation. The front shows the Sentence with the target word in `<b>` (green via CSS) plus the Audio; the back shows `{{FrontSide}}`, the Translation directly beneath it (Japanese above English, matching the subtitle stack), then word/reading/gloss below a divider and the metadata fields, each conditional (`{{#POS}}...{{/POS}}`). The Gloss matches the popup's numbered-sense layout exactly.
- **Capture:** each popup card has its own "+ Anki" button, so for a homograph the user picks exactly which reading and sense to save. `onWordClick` captures the sentence (`sentenceHtml`, with the audio `cueEntry` and the show/episode label) synchronously; `renderGroups` has already recorded every clickable span's exact character offset, so `buildSentenceHtml` bolds the right occurrence even when the word appears twice in the line. Success replaces the button with "Added to Anki" plus Undo (AnkiConnect `deleteNotes`) and Edit; a failure shows "Failed — retry".
- **Two-phase send:** the note is written at once with every text field (`addAnkiNote({word, reading, gloss, sentenceHtml})` wraps `addNote`; the caller supplies `sentenceHtml` with the word already in `<b>`, and the function does no text processing of its own); when the audio clip is ready, a second `updateNoteFields` attaches it through AnkiConnect's `audio` parameter (which stores the file and writes the `[sound:...]` reference itself). The wait for the clip is up to 20s. The Audio field has no toggle and is simply left empty when no clip could be made.
- **Split-sentence merge:** at click time, `buildMergedAnkiSentence()` rejoins a sentence split across consecutive Japanese cues **for the card only** when `pairEnglishCues` shows them translating as one English sentence. The displayed line is spliced into the merged string verbatim, so the clicked word's offset stays valid (shifted by `offsetShift`), and the audio clip is widened to the same span (Anki export: audio capture). It falls back to the displayed line when no English is loaded, the window pairs with zero or several English cues, or there's nothing neighbouring.
- **Metadata fields:** POS, Frequency, JLPT and Source, each behind its opt-in toggle (Storage & settings); the `LOOKUP_WORD` response carries the current toggle values. Source is one combined string from `formatShowEpisode()` (e.g. "Witch Hat Atelier — Episode 5") built from `currentShowEpisode` (set alongside `lastLoadedIdentity`); the season is included only when it's set and not 1, because `partOfSeries.name` stays the same across a franchise's seasons, and "Season 0" is omitted for films. The label is installed through the request binding (Show & episode detection).
