- **Verified live:** the core flow on 2026-07-17 (a real test card matching the design; a word appearing twice bolded at the right occurrence; Undo removing the note from Anki; closing Anki producing "Failed — retry"); the POS field 2026-07-17; Frequency 2026-07-19; JLPT 2026-07-22; Source and all four toggles 2026-07-26; the `Translation` field 2026-07-26; the split-sentence card text 2026-07-27. Unverified parts: Roadmap → Built, not verified live.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-17 — **AnkiConnect's `webCorsOriginList` doesn't trust extension origins by default** — a near-universal gotcha for any extension-to-AnkiConnect integration. The user has to add `"*"` (or the extension's `chrome-extension://` origin) to AnkiConnect's config before any request succeeds; the first setup also needed a JSON-syntax fix from a stray leftover config line. Checked with `invokeAnkiConnect("version")` from the service worker's own console, which returned `6`.
- 2026-07-17 — Eric's Anki before setup: a fresh install with only a "Kaishi 1.5k" study deck and the stock note types, none with more than 3 fields (read with `deckNames`/`modelNames`/`modelFieldNames`, not assumed).
- 2026-07-17 — A card created before the POS field existed still opens fine in Anki's Browse window, with the field empty.
- 2026-07-26 — "The Translation shows on the card front": the template never contained it — the field appeared once, inside `{{#Translation}}` on `ANKI_BACK_TEMPLATE`, and the one thing left to check was whether Eric's front template literally contained `{{Translation}}`; the English was a dual-language `[SubsPlease]` file putting English *into the `Sentence` field* (confirmed by Eric). Three real defects found while investigating: `Sentence` and `Translation` emitted raw `\n` (Anki fields are HTML, so a cue split across simultaneous events arrived as one run-on line); `Translation` was injected unescaped, so `<` or `&` rendered wrong or swallowed text; and template updates were pushed to the hardcoded name `"Card 1"`, which AnkiConnect's `updateModelTemplates` silently ignores when the card has been renamed — every template update was a no-op there while every add still reported success.
- 2026-07-26 — Split sentences, measured before deciding: across four episodes and 1,448 Japanese cues (an independent Jimaku Japanese file against Crunchyroll's own English, extracted from SubsPlease's dual-language release), 12.4% of Japanese cues are half of a sentence Crunchyroll translates as one; 97% of those are genuine continuations, and several of the other 3% are legitimate too (Japanese puts a vocative after the question mark, English merges it). Sentence-final punctuation is an uploader habit, not a property of the sentence — on 10% of cues in one file and 87% in another — so a Japanese-only rule would merge ~37% of cues on unpunctuated files.
- 2026-07-31 — The edit panel's word change overwrote part of the sentence (the old splice trusted the offset); Eric's exact reported string is now a regression test. The capture chip had waited seconds for a split sentence's second half before the two-phase send.

**See also:** Anki export: audio capture (the Audio field); Anki export: capture chip & edit panel (verification and editing after capture); Storage & settings (the toggles); Dictionary data & lookup (Frequency and JLPT data).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Anki export: cards & capture.

### Anki export: audio capture

**What it does:** Continuously records the episode's audio into a rolling buffer, so a capture can attach exactly the clicked line — with a little padding — to the card as a loudness-normalized WAV clip, without ever pausing or rewinding the video.

**Key files:** `audio-capture.js` (a content script loaded before `content.js`: `captureNow`, `markCueBoundary`, `noteSeek`, `noteDiscontinuity`, `resetCaptureForNavigation`, `flushPendingCaptures`, `sliceClipWavWhenReady`, `normalizeLoudness`), `background.js` (the Audio field), `content.js` (click-time wiring)

**Invariants:**
- **The audio graph is only ever added to, never disconnected.** Dropping a `createMediaElementSource()` connection silences the page's audio until a reload. The capture tap is a parallel, silent-output branch beside the unchanged direct `source -> destination` link.
- Only the current graph writes to the buffer (each graph carries a generation number); a graph superseded by a `<video>` swap goes quiet instead of interleaving its samples.
- **The capture clock is the buffer's own write count,** and nothing is written while the `<video>` is paused or seeking — so a position on the clock is a position in the buffer, by construction.
- A clip never spans a discontinuity (seek, navigation, `<video>` swap): a capture in flight at a jump is flushed up to that instant, widening never reaches across one, and edit-buffer padding is clamped to the nearest one.
- A flushed clip is capped at the clicked line's own subtitle window + 0.25s, so audio from the next episode can't leak in; every clip is capped at 20 seconds.
- A capture below the silence floor exports no audio at all — never silence, never amplified noise.
- Capturing never pauses, seeks or rewinds the video.
- *Decided, not built:* no audio clip when the playing audio isn't Japanese (Roadmap, next up).

**How it works now:**
- **Tap:** a `ScriptProcessorNode` on the `createMediaElementSource` graph continuously fills a 45-second mono Float32 ring buffer — 45 seconds of real audio, since pauses aren't written.
- **Clock:** `captureNow()` = samples written ÷ sample rate.
- **Cue timeline** (`cueTimeline`): `markCueBoundary` records, in lockstep with `content.js`'s `lastText`, each cue's `[audioStart, audioEnd]` on the capture clock plus its subtitle-file window (`jpStart`/`jpEnd`); the timeline also records empty entries for the gaps between lines. The timeline carries an epoch, bumped on a seek (`noteSeek`) and on navigation (`resetCaptureForNavigation`, which also clears the buffer); `flushPendingCaptures` runs in `noteSeek` just before the bump; `noteDiscontinuity` records one discontinuity per seek, navigation or `<video>` swap.
- **Slicing:** a clip spans the cue's own timestamps, not the click moment, padded asymmetrically — 0.2s lead-in, 0.6s tail — and the slice waits (bounded, 2s) until that tail has actually been recorded. The clip is computed when "+ Anki" is clicked, not when the popup renders, because the cue's end may not be final yet; `sliceClipWavWhenReady()` waits (bounded) for a cue still playing, while the video keeps playing normally.
- **Merged sentences:** `sliceClipWavWhenReady()` takes optional `mergeStart`/`mergeEnd` (subtitle-file timestamps spanning the merged group) and widens the clip to them, waiting (bounded) for a second cue still playing and keeping the widened start if it never finishes. It matches them against each timeline entry's recorded `jpStart`/`jpEnd` (timing, not text). The display window locates the sentence in the buffer, but the clip's edges are interpolated back to the sentence's own subtitle timings, clamped to that window.
- **Loudness:** `normalizeLoudness()` scales each clip to a fixed RMS target (`AUDIO_TARGET_RMS`, ~-21 dBFS) before WAV encoding, with a peak ceiling keeping the gain below clipping and a max-gain limit so a near-silent capture isn't amplified into hiss. Below the silence floor nothing is exported, and a console line says so.
- **Export:** WAV (a header plus the raw samples). The card's Audio field — no toggle — is on the front template, so it plays as part of the recall prompt and still shows on the back via `{{FrontSide}}`; it's filled by the second phase of the send (Anki export: cards & capture).
- The edit panel's retained copy of the samples is described in Anki export: capture chip & edit panel.
- **Status:** working live since 2026-07-22, with that round's fixes confirmed live 2026-07-23. Checklist B ran live on 2026-08-15, before that day's rework of the capture path: split-sentence audio (on a `[NanakoRaws]` file), ordinary single-cue capture and the skip-then-capture clip length passed. Unverified parts, including the rework itself: Roadmap → Built, not verified live.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-17 — **DRM feasibility, measured live** on 2 different real episodes with identical results. Screenshot: `canvas.drawImage()` doesn't throw but paints a blank frame (sampled pixel data all zero), and `video.captureStream()` throws `NotSupportedError: Stream capture not supported with EME` — two independent methods agreeing, so screenshot capture is confirmed blocked. Audio: an `AudioContext.createMediaElementSource()` connection that doesn't throw proves nothing (DRM can mute silently), so an `AnalyserNode` measured 3 seconds of real dialogue — max deviation 34, average ~7.9 across 363 samples, far above the ~0 silence baseline — confirming capture is viable.
- 2026-07-17 — **`createMediaElementSource()` permanently claims the video's audio for the page:** the first test script's own cleanup (disconnect after 500ms) silenced the episode until a page reload. It can also be called only once per `<video>` for the life of the page.
- 2026-07-22 — The clip was cut off mid-sentence when "+ Anki" was clicked before the line finished: `sliceClipWav`'s `end = cueEntry.audioEnd ?? audioCtx.currentTime` fallback used the click moment as the end. Fixed by awaiting the cue's real end (then bounded to 8s, so an episode's last line or a long pause can't hang the button); confirmed live 2026-07-23.
- 2026-07-22 — Pausing mid-line and then capturing spliced the pause's dead air into the clip. Accepted at the time as the design's trade-off (a silence-trimming pass was rejected as threshold-tuning risk for a narrow case); the 2026-08-15 pass showed it as a 44-second clip of silence, and the sample clock fixed the cause.
- 2026-07-26 — Exported loudness tracked Crunchyroll's volume slider at every level, not just at mute: `createMediaElementSource()` taps *after* the element's own volume attenuation (per the Web Audio spec), and `captureStream()` — the only tap before it — is DRM-blocked, so the correction has to happen on the clip. Unit-tested on synthetic signals: the same tone captured at player volumes 1.0, 0.5, 0.2, 0.05 and 0.01 exports at an identical RMS, loud captures are turned down as well as quiet ones boosted, and no input clips. A muted capture produces no `[sound:…]` reference and no play button (confirmed with Eric).
- 2026-07-27 — Split-sentence audio captured only the first half after a long busy wait, because widening matched cues by display text: the timeline records the *join* of every cue visible at a moment, the merge names one cue. On Frieren ep 7, `[Moozzi2]` shows one cue at a time (0% of display windows hold two or more) while `[NanakoRaws]` shows two or more 80.5% of the time — both ordinary Japanese-only releases the switcher offers.
- 2026-07-31 — Four capture defects from the first full live pass: ring-buffer corruption after a `<video>` swap (two graphs writing interleaved into one buffer — the reported waveform corruption); wrong-episode audio after in-page navigation; seek desync and its 22–40s clips; merged-clip edges over-capturing on stacking providers. One second-half capture also ran on into the following line — two possible causes, one of them fixed (display windows wider than the sentence).
- 2026-08-15 — **Checklist B's findings**, all fixed: a clip of "44.29 seconds of nothing, then audio at the very end" (a paused media element still feeds its graph, so the old `audioCtx.currentTime` clock kept running while the buffer filled with silence); the very end of sentences usually omitted (subtitle end times are routinely authored a little before the speech finishes, and a cue's end is noticed by the next `timeupdate`, when the buffer holds almost nothing past it); an empty audio field on a capture interrupted by a seek or an episode change; and pulling the trim handles outward after skipping around splicing two unrelated moments together. In-page navigation is noticed by a 1s poll, so the next episode's audio can already be arriving when a capture is flushed — hence the cap.
- The one thing an offline harness can't check (the harnesses drive the clock by hand, not through a real `ScriptProcessorNode`): whether a paused media element really stops producing input as the sample clock assumes. If it doesn't, clips will come out short rather than long — the safer failure, but still a failure.

**See also:** Anki export: cards & capture (the two-phase send and split-sentence merge); Anki export: capture chip & edit panel (the retained PCM buffer and trim editor); Platform & cross-cutting (why a re-injected script can't capture audio).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Anki export: audio capture.

### Anki export: capture chip & edit panel

**What it does:** After a capture, a short-lived chip shows what was actually saved so a mistake can be spotted at a glance; the in-page edit panel then fixes the card — sentence, translation, word choice and audio trim — without leaving the episode.

**Key files:** `content.js` (`showCaptureChip`, `chipExpandedMs`, `mountChip`, `openEditPanel`, the "Edit" and "Edit last card" controls, `audioBufferNoteId`, `forgetAddedNote`, `buildGroupsForText`), `audio-capture.js` (`retainClipForEditing`, `AUDIO_EDIT_PAD_SECONDS`, `sealRetainedClip`, `clearRetainedClip`, `isMoreComplete`, `retainedClipToken`), `background.js` (`editAnkiNote`)

**Invariants:**
- Word, Reading and Gloss are never free-text: they change only by re-picking a dictionary entry, which cascades Word/Reading/Gloss/POS/Frequency/JLPT together.
- POS, Frequency, JLPT and Source are read-only; re-picking a word rewrites each only if it was already populated (a card captured with a toggle off stays without that field).
- The panel always reads the note fresh (`notesInfo`) on open — never values cached from capture, since the note may have been edited in Anki since.
- One Save = one `updateNoteFields` call. Replacing audio blanks `Audio` in that same call (otherwise AnkiConnect appends a second `[sound:…]` and the card plays both clips); the superseded media file is deleted only *after* a successful update, and a failed delete never fails the edit.
- Cancel writes nothing; a failed save keeps every pending edit and stays retryable.
- The retained audio buffer belongs to the capture that made it (a click-time token), never to "whichever note is newest", and editing an older note degrades the audio section rather than trimming a different clip.
- The trim editor never pauses the episode: preview playback mutes it instead.
- The retained PCM is kept un-normalized, so repeated trims can't compound the loudness gain.

**How it works now:**
- **Trigger surfaces:** the "Edit" button in the popup's success row (beside Undo), shown right after capture; and a persistent "Edit last card (word)" control in the top-left tool column, which appears after the first capture of a session, survives episode changes, clears on a full page reload, and holds the note's `nid` in memory only (never persisted).
- **Capture chip** (`showCaptureChip()`, built once the card is written): **expanded** for `chipExpandedMs()` — scaled with the captured content's length (2400ms + 110ms per character, capped at 12s, a few seconds under the chip's lifetime so the collapsed state still appears) — showing the Japanese sentence with the captured word highlighted by its recorded *offset* (the same word can occur twice), the reading, the gloss, the English translation, and inline Edit/Undo; then **collapsed** to the word label for the rest of its ~15s life, with hover (CSS-only) re-expanding it. Undo works in both states. It deliberately leaves out POS/Frequency/JLPT/Source. `mountChip()` gives it the lookup chip's single-slot lifecycle, video-anchored position and fullscreen re-parenting. Show/hide is instant; a smoother morph is deferred to Phase 6.
- **Edit panel** (`openEditPanel(nid)`): Sentence (JP) and Translation (EN) are free-form textareas. Word/Reading/Gloss show as a summary row plus "Change word", which re-renders the stored sentence through `buildGroupsForText` — the same pipeline as the subtitle line — with the current word marked; picking a different sense or token cascades the derived fields as a pending preview. Tags, note type and deletion are absent by design and routed to "Open in Anki". Save is disabled until something changes. A note deleted since capture gets its own explicit, non-retryable message instead of a silent no-op; an unavailable audio buffer degrades only the audio section. Panel keystrokes don't reach Crunchyroll's player shortcuts.
- **Retained audio:** `retainClipForEditing()` keeps raw PCM over the exported clip's bounds plus `AUDIO_EDIT_PAD_SECONDS` (3s) on each side, so a clip can be extended as well as tightened. Ownership is a token issued at click time (`retainedClipToken`): the note claims the buffer only once *that* capture's clip has landed, and while a capture is still in flight the audio section says so and waits on it. `audioBufferNoteId` records which note the buffer belongs to, and `forgetAddedNote()` (via `clearRetainedClip()`) releases it only when that note is deleted. On an episode change the buffer is **sealed** (`sealRetainedClip()` tops it up one last time and drops its bounds) rather than cleared, because it's an independent copy — navigation only removes the ability to re-cut it. A re-cut is accepted only if it's *more complete* than what's held (`isMoreComplete`). Padding is clamped to the nearest discontinuity (Anki export: audio capture).
- **Trim editor** (rebuilt 2026-08-15) — an NLE's model, not a DAW's: scrub a playhead over the waveform, listen, then set the in or out point *at* the playhead. Click or drag anywhere to move the playhead (playback follows a scrub made while playing); play/pause/resume; loop the selection; a ruler labelled relative to the *captured* clip's start, so pre-roll reads as negative; dimming outside the selection; dashed markers where the clip sat as captured; keyboard control (Space, I/O, L, arrows to nudge with Shift for coarse, Home/End). "Snap to speech" walks *outward* from the loudest bucket inside the selection to the first sustained quiet stretch on each side, so it extends a late-starting clip as readily as it trims an early one. The whole padded buffer is encoded once and played through one `<audio>` element (its `currentTime` *is* a position in the buffer — what makes the playhead possible). The editor keeps re-reading and rebuilding in place — carrying the in-progress edit across, never mid-playback — until the trailing pad is full or the buffer is sealed, because post-roll only exists once the episode has played it. Adjustments are staged until Save. Handle dragging remains for coarse adjustment.
- **Escape hatch:** `editAnkiNote` (`guiEditNote`, falling back to `guiBrowse` with a `nid:` query only on an unimplemented-action error) is reachable only from the panel's "Open in Anki" control.
- **Live verification:** unverified parts are listed in Roadmap → Built, not verified live.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-30 — Post-roll can't exist at the moment a clip is finalized (the cue has only just ended), so the buffer was first retained twice — at once, and again after the padding window — rather than delaying the card's export.
- 2026-07-31 — The first live pass found four panel failures, all fixed: the sentence corrupted when changing the target word; overlapping preview playback; playback surviving the panel's close; panel keystrokes reaching the player's shortcuts. Also fixed: the chip showing the wrong English line, and too short an expand window for long merged sentences (the timing was raised then). **Unexplained:** a report that changing the word left the original note in place and created a second one — the panel's only Anki write is `updateNoteFields` on the existing note id, with no `addNote` path reachable from it (Open questions). The right-hand padding was also reported unreachable — a fixed top-up timer, replaced by an on-demand re-cut.
- 2026-08-15 — Four more from checklist B/D's live reports: "Audio for this card is no longer available to edit" the moment the user moved to the next episode, for a clip sitting complete in memory; the preview playing the *previous* card's clip, because the buffer is cut only when a capture resolves (seconds later for a split sentence) while the newest note had already claimed it; re-cutting a clip whose start had aged out of the 45-second window returning a shorter buffer with shifted markers; and the right-hand pad "never reaching 3 seconds while the left always does" — the editor read the buffer only once at build time, while the left pad is complete by definition. Two cases where a short right-hand pad is now correct and stated in the UI: the buffer was sealed by an episode change before 3s had played, or the padding hit a seek and was clamped to it.

**See also:** Anki export: audio capture (the capture clock, discontinuities, the ring buffer); Anki export: cards & capture (bolding as markup, which the panel's sentence rebuild relies on); Tokenizing & grouping (`buildGroupsForText`).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Anki export: capture chip & edit panel.

### Storage & settings

**What it does:** The toolbar popup (Jimaku API key and the "Extra info" toggles) and everything the extension keeps in `chrome.storage.local`.

**Key files:** `popup.html`, `popup.js`, and the storage reads/writes in `content.js` and `background.js`

**Invariants:**
- The Jimaku API key lives in `chrome.storage.local`, set through the toolbar popup — never in a config file.
- One shared toggle set governs both the popup and the Anki card, so the two can never disagree about what's shown for a word.
- Every metadata toggle is opt-in and off by default.

**How it works now:**
- **Toolbar popup:** a "Jimaku API Key" password field with Save (stored as `jimakuApiKey`), and an "Extra info (popup + Anki cards)" group with four checkboxes: Part of speech (`metaShowPos`), Frequency rank (`metaShowFreq`), JLPT level (`metaShowJlpt`) and Show/episode — Anki only (`toggle-source` → `metaShowSource`). The frequency and JLPT boxes started as disabled "Coming soon" placeholders so the set wouldn't visibly grow later, and graduated when their data shipped (2026-07-19, 2026-07-22).
- **How toggles reach the page:** `background.js`'s `LOOKUP_WORD` handler reads all four toggles and returns them in the same response `content.js` already waits for, so no separate storage read is needed. The toggles gate only what's *shown* (the POS chip line, the badges) and what's *filled* on the card; internal uses of the same data are unaffected.
- **Keys in `chrome.storage.local`:**
  - `jimakuApiKey`; `metaShowPos`, `metaShowFreq`, `metaShowJlpt`, `metaShowSource`
  - `offset:<pathname>` and `offsetProvider:<series>:<season>:<releaseTag>` — per-episode and per-release subtitle offset (Subtitle overlay, sync & offset)
  - `uploaderPref:<seasonMemoryKey>` and `entryPref:<seasonMemoryKey>` — the remembered uploader and Jimaku entry (Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload)
  - `siblings:<seasonMemoryKey>` — accumulated sibling episode titles (Episode & file selection)
- Planned, not built: a settings panel consolidating the offset, upload and switcher controls, and a way to view or clear saved per-season memory (both Roadmap, Phase 6). What Phase 7 settings work is needed beyond this persistence is an open question.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-07-17 — The POS toggle was the first of the "opt-in metadata toggles" built end to end, because its data already existed; frequency and JLPT each needed real prerequisite work first.

**See also:** Popup display (badges), Anki export: cards & capture (fields), Dictionary data & lookup (frequency and JLPT data).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Storage & settings.

### Test & audit tooling

**What it does:** The offline tests, corpus runs, live audits and browser-console collectors that check the extension against real data — and the live regression script for entry resolution. Which command to run when is in `CLAUDE.md` → Commands & testing; this section records how the tools work, what they can and can't prove, and what they've found.

**Key files:** `scripts/batch-test.js`, `scripts/test-*.js`, `scripts/audit-resolution.js`, `scripts/sweep-resolution.js`, `scripts/analyze-crunchyroll-fixtures.js`, `scripts/audit/` (harness), browser-console scripts `scripts/collect-crunchyroll-fixtures.js`, `scripts/collect-jsonld-blocks.js`, `scripts/probe-play-captions.js`; data in `fixtures/`; audit write-up in `docs/audit/`

**Invariants:**
- **Tests run the real code, never a copy:** `background.js` is loaded whole into a `vm` sandbox, and `content.js` functions are extracted from the source file, so a test can't agree with itself while disagreeing with what ships.
- **Real-world data is real:** every Crunchyroll- or Jimaku-derived value in a fixture is captured verbatim from the live source or explicitly labelled as constructed or a guess.
- **Infrastructure noise never reads as a defect:** only successful responses are cached, network errors are retried like a 429, and a transport failure that still gets through is `REQUEST-FAILED`, not a verdict.
- The resolution audit separates what it PROVES from what it only FLAGS and what it can't check at all — it never claims to prove the resolved entry is the *right* one.
- The audit never invents an episode number: an item with no position is `NO-POSITION` and unscored.
- Live Jimaku tools never run alongside each other (they saturate the rate limit, and failures then read as regressions).
- Full Crunchyroll caption captures stay local and gitignored, backed up outside git in Eric's Documents folder; only the sign-signal measurement script reads them, and it fails with a clear message when they're missing. The committed caption fixture holds the labelled lines verbatim, plus each file's `[V4+ Styles]`, its per-style event counts and its source.
- No pinned test depends on a gitignored file.

**How it works now:**
- **`scripts/batch-test.js`** — kuromoji tokenization + JMdict lookup over real Jimaku subtitle files outside the browser, so segmentation and lookup bugs surface in minutes instead of hours of playback. Its corpus is 3 shows' episodes (Bocchi the Rock! ep 1, Frieren ep 1, One Piece ep 894), chosen for uploader and register diversity. It reports corpus-validation patterns numbered 1, 2 and 4–11 (Pattern 3, a "dangling single-char fragment" heuristic, was removed), whose counts form the regression baseline — currently `92/49/57/33/354/109/9/9/35/2` (Pattern 2 rose from 39 on 2026-09-18 because grunts now miss honestly instead of resolving to unrelated verbs). Update it here when a change moves the counts on purpose. It checks classification, **not ordering** — ordering changes need a manual spot-check. Needs `scripts/node_modules` (`cd scripts && npm install`) and `JIMAKU_API_KEY`.
- **Pinned tests** (`scripts/test-*.js`) — offline, seconds each; the property test `scripts/test-parse-invariants.js` fuzzes the real text pipeline; several replay verbatim live Jimaku responses recorded into `fixtures/jimaku/` by `scripts/audit/record-scenario.js`, failing on any request the fixture doesn't hold. `scripts/test-entry-resolution.js` runs the real `resolveTextFiles` end to end in a `vm` sandbox (`fetch`, `console`, `chrome` and `importScripts` stubbed) — the query ladder, file lookup, cour-sibling retry, unfiltered fallback, archive filter and logging — with no browser, network or AnkiConnect; **82 cases as of 2026-08-13**, each asserting the selected entry, the exact console lines and the UI state (`confident` / warning + candidates / unresolved / thrown error), logging unconditionally (a correct resolution that emits no entry line fails), and for the unresolved case that **no file request was made at all**. All its data is real, except one labelled constructed value (an empty `?episode=24` on entry 712, the only way to reach the cour retry). `scripts/test-season-resolution.js` stays alongside it for helper-level coverage.
- **`scripts/audit-resolution.js`** — every episode of every season in a capture, through the real resolver against live Jimaku. **PROVEN** (a flag is a defect): MIXED (one episode's files state episode numbers with nothing in common), DUPLICATE (two episodes of a season return an identical list), COLLISION (two seasons resolve to one entry), EMPTY (nothing loaded and it wasn't a deliberate refusal). **FLAGGED** for a human: DECLINED, NARROWED (only on entries whose files state no episode identities), UNACCOUNTED, MISSING (the season's own entry states no such episode and no offset could be hiding it, in either direction), SHARED-ENTRY (Jimaku holds only one TV entry for the show). **Unscored:** NO-POSITION, REQUEST-FAILED. MIXED is suppressed where one number is inside the season's range (its highest Crunchyroll episode number, not its count) and one above it. Episode identities are compared as *sets* of episode numbers and disagree only when they share none. **Not checked, because no automatic ground truth exists** (also written into the script's header): whether the resolved entry is the *right* one (a show resolving every season to one wrong-but-consistent entry would pass everything); whether a file stating no episode belongs to this one; and whether the subtitles match the scene, which only a person can judge. `--background <path>` runs a different `background.js`, which is how the audit is proven: `fixtures/known-bugs-2026-08-02.json` against the resolver from commit `6ff83e8` must report 9 proven defects (covering all six 2026-08-02 bugs), and 0 against current code. The failed-request count is reported so a run's integrity is visible.
- **`scripts/sweep-resolution.js`** — every *season* in a capture through the real resolver against live Jimaku, scored OK/SUSPECT/ASKS/ERROR. SUSPECT is a reading list, not a verdict (a franchise Jimaku keeps in one entry is SUSPECT on every season, correctly).
- **`scripts/analyze-crunchyroll-fixtures.js`** — sweeps a catalogue capture for metadata shapes only: season-title classification (using `background.js`'s own `nonEpisodicClass`, `looksLikeStandaloneWork` and `contentTitleCandidates`), `name` shapes, numbering conventions and the code-vs-number split (a standing check). It never calls `resolveTextFiles` and never contacts Jimaku. Unclassified side formats are labelled REVIEW, not MISSED.
- **Correctness-audit harness** (`scripts/audit/`, 2026-09-18; dependencies isolated in `scripts/audit/node_modules`): `jimaku-client.js` caches every live Jimaku response on disk in `.audit-cache/`, keyed by URL, paced from the rate-limit headers, with `JIMAKU_CACHE_MODE` = `live` (default: cache, fetch on a miss), `offline` (cache only; a miss is an error) or `refresh`, so before/after comparisons replay identical data; `scripts/audit/detection-sweep.js` runs the real `fetchSubtitles` (not just `resolveTextFiles`) over captured episodes (first live run: 1,485 sampled episodes across all three captures), with `--lists` + `diff-lists.js` for before/after diffs that list every file removed; `build-corpus.js` builds a structurally diverse corpus (one file per release group and format per entry, since parsing faults follow the provider); `scripts/audit/parse-sweep.js` runs every corpus file through the real text pipeline (`parse-pipeline.js`: `cueDisplayText` + `buildGroupsForText` from `content.js`, `tokenize-utils.js`, `background.js`'s lookups over the shipped JMdict) at every distinct on-screen moment, with UniDic (via lindera) as a second analyzer (the 2026-09-18 corpus: 386 real files, 233,963 lines); `entry-name-census.js`, `file-identity.js` (an independent filename parser, so the audit can catch the resolver's mistakes instead of sharing them), `load-background.js`, `record-scenario.js` and `show-cluster.js` support them. `docs/audit/` holds the pipeline map, failure taxonomy, progress log and report. Its three standing rules: fix mechanisms, not instances; never load subtitles without a confident season identity; keep what was checked against the live API separate from what was only checked offline.
- **Browser-console collectors** (pasted into a logged-in Crunchyroll page; not node scripts, because Cloudflare blocks direct fetching): `collect-crunchyroll-fixtures.js` bulk-collects catalogue data (2026-08-01), `collect-jsonld-blocks.js` records real `TVEpisode` blocks and how fast they follow a navigation (2026-09-20; `__jpJsonLd.report()` / `__jpJsonLd.save()`), and `probe-play-captions.js` lists every caption track in the `play` response and what each English file holds (2026-09-22).
- **Captures in `fixtures/`:** `crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-01.json` (30 series, 134 seasons, 4,829 episodes), `crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-01b.json` (45 series, 75 seasons, 2,385 episodes), `fixtures/crunchyroll-catalogue-2026-08-04.json` (90 seasons; the full 2026-08-13 audit covered 2,526 episodes), `known-bugs-2026-08-02.json`, `jimaku/` (recorded live responses for three 2026-09-18 pinned tests), `captions/caption-probe-2026-09-23.json`, `jsonld/jsonld-blocks-2026-09-23.json` (the only JSON-LD fixture).
- **Debugging tips** (headless-Chrome layout tests, the service-worker console) are in `CLAUDE.md` → Commands & testing.

#### Entry-resolution live regression script (formerly checklist group A)

**✅ Passed in full, live, 2026-08-13.** Run against the eight defects fixed on 2026-08-11/12, four of which had been silently serving the wrong episode. Found two faults no offline run could have shown (Chronicle's hidden picker, and its lone wrong-film candidate), both fixed the same day. **Kept below as this area's regression script, not as outstanding work** — re-run it if `resolveTextFiles`, the sibling sniffer or the episode-number extraction changes.

Reload the extension first (`chrome://extensions` → reload). **Read the SERVICE WORKER console** (`chrome://extensions` → the extension's "service worker" link), not the page console — resolver logging runs in the background worker, and three live rounds were lost to reading the wrong one. Filter to `jp-immersion`. A load producing **no** `Jimaku entry …` line at all is a failure however right the screen looks.

*A1 — must load, and load the RIGHT episode. These are the 2026-08-12 fixes; each was serving wrong content.*
1. **Dr. STONE SCIENCE FUTURE episode 13** — expect `…Cour 3 … covers episodes 25–37, not 13 — looking further`, then Cour 2. The subtitle must match 同じ月を見て, **not** 未来を唆るもの (episode 37, the old bug).
2. **SPY x FAMILY season 1 episode 13** — expect `dropped 7 of its 13 file(s) that state a different episode`. Subtitles must be PROJECT APPLE, not FIRST CONTACT (episode 25).
3. **Mushoku Tensei season 1 episode 12** — same shape, expect `dropped 6 of its 14`. (The figure was written as 4 before `[23]`-style bracket numbering was recognised; six is correct.)
4. **MHA: Vigilantes season 2 episode 6** — expect `carries a second numbering running 13 ahead — episode 6 is its 19`. Previously loaded nothing. Also try episodes 9–13.
5. **Haikyu!! TO THE TOP episode 14** — expect `continues from 14 (Jimaku splits this season across cours under an arc title)`. Previously failed outright; 12 of 25 episodes were dead.
6. **My Hero Academia "I am a Hero too"** — expect `holds one file and it names no episode`. Previously loaded nothing.
7. **Fruits Basket season 2 episode 1** — expect `also searched "Fruits Basket", which adds 3 entries`, entry **3850**. Previously played season 1.
8. **Chainsaw Man — The Compilation, both episodes** — each must get its OWN part (`then to the 2 numbered part 1/2 of it`), not the same four files.

*A2 — must load NOTHING and offer the picker. Any subtitles here is the failure.*
9. **Dr. STONE Season 2 or 3** — expect `its own name says it is not season 1 — declining to fall back`. Previously played season 1, silently.
10. **Chainsaw Man The Stage** — a stage play; expect no entry identified.
11. **Attack on Titan: Chronicle** and **Tokyo Ghoul: Root A** — both should decline, with the amber "pick one" banner and the video playing normally.

*A3 — must be UNCHANGED. The regression guards; all of them worked before and must still.*
12. **Naruto: Shippuuden episodes 1, 33 and 144** — the highest-value check. A later-season rule nearly blanked this show entirely; each episode uses a different trigger, and 144 was once unreachable.
13. **One Piece, any recent episode** — console must name the episode Crunchyroll displays (e.g. `episode 1156`, not `episode 1`), and show **no** `episode filter is unusable` line.
14. **Frieren season 2, episode 9** — must still offer its full file list; its files legitimately mix `09` and `37` numbering and both are the same episode.
15. **My Dress-Up Darling season 2, and an ordinary TV season** (KonoSuba S3, Demon Slayer's Entertainment District Arc) — plain `matched by …` lines, no retry lines at all.

*A4 — diagnostics, wherever they appear.* `Jimaku has nothing indexed under "…"` is the query ladder working; on ordinary TV it is a bug. `also searched this title's own name "…"` should appear **only** on films/OVAs/specials. `Jimaku's own episode filter is unusable …` should appear **only** on long franchises whose files carry 第N話 — on Frieren or One Piece it means the trigger is over-firing. **If anything resolves wrongly**, copy the full `jp-immersion` output plus the page's JSON-LD (`JSON.parse(document.querySelector('script[type="application/ld+json"]').textContent)`) — rounds have been lost to fixtures built on guessed metadata.

*A5 — sibling capture.* On any watch page run `__jpImmersionSnifferStats()` in the **page** console (that script is page-world). Expect `siblingEpisodesSeen` ≥ 1. It reads Crunchyroll's own adjacent-episode lookups, so it fires on load with no navigation; titles accumulate across visits.

**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- 2026-08-01 — Three live rounds were spent reading the page console before anyone noticed resolver logging runs in the **service worker** console.
- 2026-08-01 — A truncated candidate list can manufacture a bug as easily as an invented response shape: the sweep flagged "Sword Art Online EXTRA EDITION" as an unclassified side format; a hand-written 4-entry candidate list showed it resolving to season 1, and a fix was half-designed before the real 13-entry search result was checked — Jimaku holds an entry named exactly that, and the season-name tier resolves it. Pinned with the full real result list.
- 2026-08-02 — **`analyze-crunchyroll-fixtures.js` never tested resolution**, and its "zero misses" was twice summarised as if it meant the shows resolved correctly. They didn't: Tokyo Ghoul's "Root A" and ":re", Slimes 300 Years' season 2 and My Dress-Up Darling's season 2 were all serving season 1's subtitles while that claim stood. Hence `sweep-resolution.js` and then `audit-resolution.js`.
- 2026-08-02 — Running the sweep beside `test-render-pipeline.js` produced a confusing failure that was rate-limit contention, not a regression.
- 2026-08-04 — Proving the audit against pre-fix code found a flaw in the audit first: comparing identity *labels* made `[Cleo]Tokyo_Ghoul_-_01` and `…S01E01` read as different episodes. Two more defects inflated its first result: cached failures turned one transient 429 into a permanent failure (59 of 84 EMPTY findings were spurious) — verified by inspection to inflate only EMPTY, since every resolver fetch throws on a non-OK response, so MIXED/DUPLICATE/COLLISION could be masked but never manufactured; and NARROWED fired on nearly every subset of a 277-file Frieren entry (661 findings burying the shape it exists for).
- 2026-08-11 — The audit scored refusals as defects: KONOSUBA's dubbed season 11s (OVAs under separate entries), KONOSUBA 3's last two episodes (blank episode codes) and Steins;Gate 0's episode 24 (the unaired 23β, not in entry 466) were "proven EMPTY"; Black Clover's four Crunchyroll "parts" were flagged COLLISION for sharing the only Black Clover entry; JUJUTSU KAISEN Season 2 is episodes 25–47, so the MIXED boundary must use the highest episode number; SPY x FAMILY's episode 13 correctly stays flagged (13 and 25 both inside season 1's range). The first MISSING rule handled an offset in only one direction (Vigilantes' files run to 26 for a 13-episode season; Shangri-La's season 2 is episodes 26–51 on Crunchyroll and 1–26 on Jimaku) and so excused three of the six bugs the audit exists to catch — caught by the `--background` proof.
- 2026-08-11 — The known-bug regression run caught a fix regressing a clean show (Mushoku Tensei, COLLISION 0 → 1 against committed code via `--background`).
- 2026-08-12 — Crunchyroll lists PVs inside a season with no code and no number; defaulting to 1 made Haikyu!!'s PV a DUPLICATE of episode 1 and Fairy Tail's a MIXED (that season is numbered from 176) — two of the three findings left in the full run.
- 2026-08-12 — One transient `fetch failed` inside the resolver was scored as a proven EMPTY and put a phantom Haikyu!! failure into a regression run after the Chainsaw Man work; the integrity counter tracked only the audit's own requests, and a dropped connection throws rather than returning a status, so it had escaped the retry loop.
- 2026-08-13 — Batch sizing for `audit-resolution.js`: `--only` batches of ~150 episodes (~1.5s per episode; larger batches exceed the 600s foreground limit).
- 2026-09-23 — `probe-play-captions.js` kept only a 10-event sample of a file over 60 events, cut every event at 200 characters and never saved `[V4+ Styles]`: `caption-probe-2026-09-23.json` holds 10 of the Japanese-audio track's 395 events (its style counts are complete). Too thin to measure any sign-cue signal against real dialogue — hence the recapture (Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-09-23).

**Decisions:** see Decisions → Test & audit tooling.

## Roadmap

Phases in order. Completed phases are summarized; what they built is described in [Architecture](#architecture) and why in [Decisions](#decisions).

### Completed phases

- **Phase 0 — Finalize spec.** ✅ This document.
- **Phase 1 — Environment & extension skeleton.** ✅ A Manifest V3 skeleton injecting into the Crunchyroll page, verified on a real watch page. → Platform & cross-cutting.
- **Phase 2 — Subtitle pipeline proof of concept.** ✅ Query Jimaku for one show, fetch a file, parse it and display raw Japanese synced to playback (no clicking yet), verified end to end on Witch Hat Atelier ep 1. → Jimaku entry resolution; Subtitle parsing & display filters; Subtitle overlay, sync & offset.
- **Phase 3 — Word parsing & dictionary lookup.** ✅ Clickable segmented words with a JMdict popup (verified on Witch Hat Atelier ep 1), auxiliary-verb merging (the origin of Rule 3) and the first popup enrichment — furigana, inflection line, POS labels, a common-word badge — all substantially reworked in Phase 4. → Tokenizing & grouping; Dictionary data & lookup; Popup display.
- **Phase 4 — Sync tooling & multi-show validation.** ✅ Closed 2026-07-15 by Eric's call, not by exhaustion. Delivered the offset tools and hotkeys, `scripts/batch-test.js` and its corpus, the full grouping-pipeline rebuild, TUBELEX frequency ordering, the from-scratch compact-file rebuild, numerals, name runs, the manual upload fallback and many popup refinements, through live rounds on Bocchi the Rock! ep 1 (8 bugs found and fixed on the first pass, a 26-item QA pass on 2026-07-05), Frieren, One Piece ep 894 and Witch Hat Atelier. → Subtitle overlay, sync & offset; Tokenizing & grouping; Dictionary data & lookup; Popup display; Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload; Test & audit tooling.
- **Phase 4.5 — Jimaku integration.** ✅ Created 2026-07-05, closed 2026-07-17 by Eric's call. Delivered show/episode auto-detection from the page's JSON-LD, in-page navigation handling, best-subtitle ranking, the switcher panel and per-season uploader memory with sticky fallback, plus eight bugs fixed in one long live session, every one confirmed live before closing. → Show & episode detection; Jimaku entry resolution; Episode & file selection; Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload.

## Decisions

Settled choices between real alternatives, so they don't get re-debated. Grouped under the same component headings as [Architecture](#architecture), plus two groups with no component (Product & scope; Process & roadmap), and chronological within each group. A decision that was later revised appears once, showing its final state, with a dated reason for each revision. Findings with no real alternative live in the component's Architecture entry instead.

### Product & scope

**2026-06-22 — Browser extension, not a desktop app.**
Simpler payments, distribution and cross-platform story. *Rejected:* a desktop app.

**2026-06-22 — Universal Jimaku import from day one, no curated show list.**
Any show Jimaku has is supported, and sync/offset tooling is treated as a must-have rather than a later add-on, since community subtitles drift. *Rejected:* a curated show list.

**2026-06-22 — V1 platform scope is Crunchyroll only.**
Depth on one platform over breadth across many (see Product → Positioning). *Rejected:* supporting other platforms (Netflix, YouTube, manga, the general web) in v1.

**2026-06-30 — Lean into differentiation, not feature parity with ManabiDojo.**
A direct review of ManabiDojo's site confirmed that several planned "differentiators" (romaji, hotkeys, POS/inflection, furigana) are already parity or behind theirs. So the product leans into Crunchyroll-only depth, an AI-free architecture as a stance, and a structurally lower cost base (Competitive landscape). *Rejected:* chasing feature-for-feature parity (AI breakdown, wordbook/quiz, multi-platform) — infeasible for a solo project.

**2026-06-30 — Anki export in scope, instead of an in-house wordbook.**
Anki export moved from excluded to in scope. The target user already has SRS history in Anki, and feeding that system fits "capture and keep watching" better than a second, competing one would. *Rejected:* an in-house wordbook/SRS/quiz system.

**2026-06-30 — AI explanations excluded on principle, not on cost.**
Reaffirms the exclusion on stronger grounds. It was originally excluded as "expensive to replicate"; it is now excluded because it works against the product's purpose — it breaks immersion and competes with a more-trusted resource like Bunpro — however it's built. *Rejected:* an opt-in BYOK version (the user's own LLM key, called client-side), for the same reason: the issue isn't technical feasibility.

**2026-06-30 — Subscription + lifetime pricing, not a flat one-time price.**
Matches standard practice (WaniKani, Bunpro, ManabiDojo), and was Eric's explicit preference: the market has normalized subscription + lifetime enough that a flat one-time price would read as unfamiliar rather than as a selling point. *Rejected:* the original flat one-time-price plan.

**2026-07-02 — JLPT allowed only as an opt-in, off-by-default tag.**
A narrow reversal, not a full reopening. JLPT is in scope only as an opt-in, off-by-default toggle — never an always-visible label, filter or sort. The original exclusion targeted active mid-episode teaching that breaks immersion (the same reasoning as the AI-explanation exclusion); passive metadata the user deliberately enables doesn't do that. It does not reopen the AI-explanation or wordbook/SRS exclusions, which stand for unrelated reasons. At the time, sourcing JLPT data if jmdict-simplified lacked it was left as a separate, unscoped build task. *Rejected:* a full reopening — JLPT as an always-visible label, filter or sort, the kind of active mid-episode teaching the original exclusion targeted. *Revised 2026-07-04:* sourcing settled — see Dictionary data & lookup, "JLPT sourced externally rather than dropping the toggle".

**2026-07-02 — Anki export moved into the core loop.**
Anki export moved from the differentiation layer into the core-loop must-have list, because it is the endpoint of the "capture and keep watching" loop, not an add-on feature. *Rejected:* keeping it in the differentiation layer as an add-on.

**2026-07-04 — Kanji ON/KUN readings rejected, even as an opt-in.**
ManabiDojo has this, but it fails the litmus test differently from a simple missing feature: ON/KUN readings are kanji-level pedagogy (how to read a character in isolation), not confirmation of the specific word or inflection in context — the job of a kanji-SRS tool (e.g. WaniKani) the user already trusts. *Rejected:* an opt-in toggle — the objection isn't intrusiveness, it's the same reasoning that excludes AI explanations and the built-in wordbook however unobtrusively they're offered.

**2026-07-04 — Names dictionary and custom dictionary buttons declined.**
Both JMnedict integration and custom dictionary URL buttons / a choice of dictionary source are real ManabiDojo parity items, and both are declined, not deferred. A names database adds a second data source and ingestion pipeline for a problem the kanji-name fix already covers well enough (Tokenizing & grouping); custom buttons and source-switching cut against "nothing stands between the learner and the dictionary" — there is one dictionary, not a menu. Not tracked as an open question or a gap to close, because Eric decided against both rather than postponing. *Rejected:* building either (reasons above), or deferring them as open gaps.

**2026-07-04 — Romaji subtitle mode excluded, not deprioritized.**
A subtitle-level romaji layer is a total-beginner feature for learners who can't read kana yet, which the target user by definition can. Same shape as the kanji ON/KUN exclusion: not a priority call — this isn't the product's job for this persona, so it isn't built even as a low-priority extra. *Rejected:* keeping it as a low-priority extra.

**2026-07-04 — Kana-only subtitle mode excluded, for a sharper reason.**
Considered as a possibly more useful alternative to romaji, then rejected: kana-only doesn't just fail to help this persona, it works against furigana mode, which keeps kanji visible and adds the reading on top — reinforcing kanji the user likely already studies. Stripping the kanji out is a step backward for that goal, not a lighter version of the same feature. *Rejected:* offering kana-only as a lighter alternative to romaji (reasons above).

**2026-07-04 — Sentence-only capture kept deferred, with its reasoning corrected.**
The original "speculative demand, no confirmed evidence" framing was too dismissive: a sentence with no confusing vocabulary can still be worth saving, a real use case distinct from target-word capture. Still not built, because it needs its own capture paradigm (a line-level trigger, and a decision about what fills the word/reading/gloss fields with no target word) rather than a toggle on the existing flow, and Eric expects it to come up less often than ordinary target-word capture. Real post-Phase-5 usage of the word-click flow is a better signal than guessing now. *Rejected:* building it now.

**2026-07-06 — Karaoke-style word highlighting declined, not deferred.**
No per-word timing data exists in Jimaku, .srt/.ass or Crunchyroll's own audio. Real sync would need audio-text alignment — a nontrivial ML problem, against the no-AI/no-backend stance — and an even-timing approximation would drift out of sync almost immediately on real dialogue. Same shape as the mono-ruby naive-split rejection (Popup display): a naive approximation would actively mislead. *Rejected:* keeping it as a deferred nice-to-have.

### Platform & cross-cutting

**2026-08-15 — An orphaned content script asks for a reload.**
After an extension reload the old content script keeps running with every `chrome.*` call throwing — the reported "only the offset, upload and switcher panels appear, and no subtitles". It now removes everything it mounted and shows one banner with a Reload button. *Rejected:* re-injecting content scripts into the open tab — everything but audio would recover, but `createMediaElementSource` can be called only once per `<video>` for the life of the page and the dead script's graph still holds it and can never be disconnected (2026-07-17), so the result would look completely fixed while silently having no audio field, which is worse than the state it replaced. *Rejected:* reloading the page automatically — a reload mid-episode is a bigger interruption than a click, and one triggered by a Web Store update would be unexplained.

### Show & episode detection

**2026-07-05 — Detection built and validated before any other Phase 4.5 work.**
Every later step in the phase — Jimaku matching, ranked subtitle selection, auto-select, the switcher panel, per-show-per-season memory — depends on reliably knowing what's playing with zero user input, so building any of them first would build on an unverified foundation. (At the time this also made TMDB/AniList ID resolution a hard dependency; that plan was replaced by title matching on 2026-07-15 — see Jimaku entry resolution.) *Rejected:* building the dependent features first.

**2026-07-15 — Episode changes detected by polling `location.pathname`.**
Live testing showed Crunchyroll doesn't reload between episodes, so once-per-load detection left the previous episode's subtitles showing. The detect-and-fetch block was extracted into a reusable `loadSubtitles()` so a navigation re-runs only the data part, not `init()`'s one-time DOM and listener setup. *Revised the same day:* the first trigger wrapped `history.pushState`/`replaceState` to dispatch a custom event (plus `popstate`) — the standard technique for observing SPA routing without the `webNavigation` permission — but it failed a second live test because an isolated-world override never reaches the page's own calls (Platform & cross-cutting). Now `location.pathname` is polled every 1s, which only reads shared state; `popstate` stays as a faster path for real back/forward navigation. The lesson worth keeping: a widely documented technique still has to be confirmed in *this* context (a content script, not page JS). *Rejected:* injecting a `<script>` tag or an MV3 `"world": "MAIN"` script to patch history and dispatch a DOM event back — more moving parts (a second script file, more manifest surface, still needing the isolated-world listener) for no benefit over polling.

**2026-07-17 — Detection retries on missing or stale page data.**
Next-episode navigation sometimes showed the manual-upload message until a reload. The root cause couldn't be observed from here; the leading hypothesis was that the URL updates slightly before the new `TVEpisode` block is in the DOM. A bounded retry (2 retries, 500ms apart) shipped as a mitigation: low-risk, since it fires only on outright detection failure, and it does what a manual reload did — wait. Later the same session a real stale-block case appeared (Witch Hat Atelier episode 1→2→1→2 landing back on episode 1's uploader), so a stale guard was added: on navigation-triggered reloads only (the initial load has nothing to compare against), a detection that still matches the last loaded identity counts as stale and goes through the same retry, rather than a second, parallel mechanism. At first, exhausting the retries proceeded with whatever was detected, on the reasoning that possibly-stale data beats blocking indefinitely. *Revised 2026-09-18:* the correctness audit found a block still stale after 1s of retries being loaded anyway; the budget became a 5-second wait with nothing shown meanwhile, and responses were bound to their request (below). *Revised 2026-09-20:* staleness is now read from the block's own URL, and running out of retries loads nothing (below). *Rejected:* waiting for a confirmed root cause before shipping anything.

**2026-07-17 — `video` rebound on every navigation through module scope.**
A full show switch left subtitles permanently blank until a reload, because `video` was captured once at `init()`. It is now a module-scope `let`, re-queried on every navigation, so every consumer (`loadSubtitles`, `renderSwitcherOptions`, `buildUploadControl`, the `timeupdate` handler) reads the same live value; the listener became a named `handleTimeUpdate` so it can be `removeEventListener`'d from the old `<video>` and added to the new one instead of piling up. *Rejected:* threading `video` through as a parameter — `buildUploadControl(video)` captures it in a closure that a `FileReader.onload` callback uses later, so a swapped element would still be targeted stale, the same bug in a different spot.

**2026-07-17 — Watch pages recognized by the `/watch/` URL segment.**
The "couldn't detect the show/episode" error appeared on the homepage and search page, whose autoplay promo `<video>` passed the old `document.querySelector("video")` check. A URL gate is now required alongside it. *Rejected:* treating "no parseable `TVEpisode` block" as "not a watch page" — a slow-loading real watch page legitimately has no block for a moment (the same race as the retries above), so it would reintroduce false negatives on pages that only needed to wait.

**2026-07-27 — Stale-state recovery moved to a periodic watchdog.**
The `<video>` binding and the loaded identity were checked only at the instant a pathname change was noticed, assuming everything had settled by then. A show-to-show switch breaks that — Crunchyroll rebuilds the player and its metadata block on its own schedule — and the symptom was no subtitles until a hard reload. A 2-second watchdog re-checks both for the life of the page (what a full reload did by hand, minus the reload), self-heals cases nobody has reproduced yet, and won't fire on top of a load in flight. *Rejected:* extending the bounded retries (then 2 × 500ms) — any fixed window is either too short for a slow switch or too long a delay for a fast one, and it would still be one bounded sequence rather than something that recovers whenever the page changes.

**2026-08-01 — Episode number read from the page title's code, not `episodeNumber`.**
One Piece's Elbaph arc resolved episode 1156 against episode 1, live: the entry was right, the number wasn't. Two captures settled it — `episodeNumber` is season-relative on one page and absolute on another, while the code inside the compound `name` matched the displayed episode (and the CMS) on both (table in Architecture). So the code is preferred wherever it is numeric. It also retired a conclusion from earlier the same day: the catalogue sweep's absolute-vs-per-season table came from the CMS, not the JSON-LD the extension reads. What survives is that the magnitude rule never needed a per-catalogue convention. *Rejected:* trusting `episodeNumber`.

**2026-09-18 — Subtitles are bound to the episode they were requested for.**
Found by the correctness audit (`docs/audit/`), not by a report. Three mechanisms could land one episode's subtitles on another, all silently: a `FETCH_SUBTITLES` response arriving after navigation overwrote the new episode's cues; the identity the staleness check and watchdog compared (series + season number + episode number) was shared by 37 real episodes; and a block still stale after 1s of retries was loaded anyway. One mechanism replaces all three — each load is a request with a unique identity, installed only if still newest and still what the page reports (Architecture). *Rejected:* cancelling the in-flight load — a message to the service worker can't be aborted, and cancelling does nothing about the stale-DOM half. *Rejected:* trusting the watchdog to repair it — it compared the colliding key, and would repair only after wrong sentences could already have been captured.

**2026-09-20 — Stale metadata recognised by the block's own episode URL.**
Eric checked a live watch page (the audit's Open Question 21): the `TVEpisode` block carries the episode's watch URL in `url` and `@id`, and the whole block — URL, `episodeNumber` and compound `name` together — is replaced in place on in-app navigation, already updated when read right after clicking to the next episode. So "is this the previous episode's block?" is answerable exactly, and a block naming this page is used with no wait. *Rejected:* keeping the 5-second wait as the mechanism — a guess about how slow a page is, which stalls a legitimate URL rewrite for 5s and is weakest in the sub-second window right after navigation that couldn't be observed by hand. *Rejected:* treating a URL mismatch as an error — a block mid-replacement is normal, so it goes into the retry/watchdog path. *Revised the same day, on review:* when retries run out on a URL-less block that still reports the loaded episode, the load is refused instead of proceeding "as the same episode". That fallback was the last route to silent wrong content (a badly late block serving the previous episode's subtitles under the new one) and is unreachable while Crunchyroll publishes the URL. Cost: a genuine URL rewrite with no URL in the block loses its subtitles until the block settles, which the watchdog then repairs; the user is told, and manual upload still works. Pinned by `scripts/test-stale-timeout.js`. The premise was later measured live (Architecture, 2026-09-22).

### Jimaku entry resolution

**2026-07-01 — Exact-title match preferred over the first search result.**
Resolution prefers an exact case-insensitive `name`/`english_name` match over `entries[0]`. *Rejected:* hardcoding known-good entry IDs per tested show — it doesn't generalize to a real user's search.

**2026-07-04 — Shows matched by title, not by TMDB/AniList IDs.**
Originally decided to resolve the title mismatch (Crunchyroll's English titles vs Jimaku's JP/romanized indexing) through TMDB/AniList IDs from Crunchyroll's metadata: more build effort than fuzzy matching, but an ID lookup doesn't depend on how closely the two titles happen to render. *Revised 2026-07-15:* dropped, because the premise was false — a DevTools dump of a real logged-in page (the page itself blocks direct fetching) showed Crunchyroll exposes no external ID anywhere, so there was nothing to hand to Jimaku's `anilist_id`/`tmdb_id` parameters. Plain title matching was then verified against the live API instead of assumed risky: the exact title from the page's schema.org markup ("Witch Hat Atelier") returned exactly one match through `english_name`, confirming Jimaku indexes English titles as aliases — the case the original decision feared would break. Detection was built on the JSON-LD `TVEpisode`/`TVSeries` markup, feeding the existing exact-title-preferring search in `fetchSubtitles` unchanged. *Rejected (2026-07-04):* a fuzzy-search fallback layer on Jimaku's own search. *Rejected (2026-07-15):* getting an ID from a third party (search TMDB's or AniList's API by title, then query Jimaku by that ID) — it doesn't remove fuzzy title matching, only moves it to another vendor's search, adding an API key and a failure mode for no clear reliability gain.

**2026-07-17 — Apostrophe and season-selection fixes shipped together.**
Two stacked bugs, both confirmed with direct `curl` calls before any code changed: a straight-vs-typographic apostrophe broke the search entirely, and nothing chose between Jimaku's separate per-season entries. Apostrophes are stripped from the query and both sides of the comparison; the already-captured `seasonNumber` was threaded through `FETCH_SUBTITLES` into `resolveTextFiles`, which then required a parsed "Season N"/"Nth Season" marker (default 1) to match before accepting an exact title, falling back to the season-agnostic match only when no season-qualified one existed. A heuristic, since Jimaku has nothing structured to key on — the same seed-and-refine shape as the uploader list. *Rejected:* shipping the apostrophe fix alone — the search would have started succeeding and the `entries[0]` fallback would then silently serve season 1's subtitles for season 2 under a matching episode number, net-negative for the exact case under test. *Rejected:* guessing which Unicode apostrophe to convert to.

**2026-07-26 — Season markers parsed by convention, guarded by a title match.**
Fixing the KonoSuba wrong-season bug meant accepting a bare trailing number as a season marker, which is genuinely ambiguous ("Mob Psycho 100", "Jujutsu Kaisen 0"). What makes it safe is how the parse is *used*: the season-stripped base title must still equal Crunchyroll's title, so a title that really ends in a number strips to something that no longer matches, while a real suffix strips to exactly the franchise title. The parse proposes; the title match decides. The 2–20 bound on the bare form is a second, cheaper guard. *Rejected:* recognizing only explicit "Season N" wording (the status quo, which missed KonoSuba entirely). *Rejected:* a per-show override list — unbounded manual work that fails on every show nobody has hit yet.

**2026-07-27 — Seasons matched by Crunchyroll's season name, not its number.**
Crunchyroll's `seasonNumber` is a list position, not the anime's season, so Re:Zero loaded the next season's subtitles from the OVAs onward. Both sides already carry the same string — Crunchyroll's official English season title and Jimaku's `english_name` — so the name is matched first, then a season number parsed out of the name, with Crunchyroll's number kept as the last fallback so a show that publishes no season name behaves as before. A season name identical to the series title is ignored: it carries no season information, and matching on it would pin every season of such a show to the bare-franchise entry, a regression for the multi-season shows the number path already handled. It rested on one unverified assumption (that the field exists), logged loudly at runtime rather than assumed; confirmed on 2026-07-31. *Revised 2026-07-29:* name matching tries three title forms rather than exact equality, part of stating rules by class (Subtitle parsing & display filters, same date) — five previously unresolvable seasons across three shows then resolved. *Rejected:* correcting the number arithmetically (subtracting the non-season collections before it) — per-show data the extension doesn't have, so a guess that happens to fix Re:Zero. *Rejected for now:* a manual per-show season override in the switcher — a real escape hatch, but it makes the user do the work every time, and is worth building only if reading the page proves impossible.

**2026-07-31 — An unidentified entry loads nothing and asks.**
Films, OVAs, specials and compilations were falling through to the franchise's season-1 entry, silently, and because every diagnostic was conditional on a *detected* mismatch, the console stayed empty. Resolution is now logged on every load, and a title that can't be positively identified came back as `confident: false` plus the candidate list, surfacing the entry picker listing every candidate. Identification is deliberately strict: an entry name contained *in* Crunchyroll's title is the franchise, not the film, and accepting that direction would reintroduce the bug. *Rejected:* keep guessing but log it — the guess was wrong too often to only note, and the panel was already the place to fix it. *Revised 2026-08-01:* the no-valid-entry state now loads **nothing** (Attack on Titan's Chronicle, which has no Jimaku entry at all, prompted it). The line is drawn at the `entries[0]` fallback specifically — every other tier is a real match against the title, so a title that matches still loads even when the requested season isn't on Jimaku (guarded by its own fixture). Since every remaining tier identifies what it selects, the computed `confident` flag and its "using X as a guess" warning were deleted, and the suite asserts that an entry is returned only if identified. The switcher's *other* warning ("check these are the right subtitles") was briefly judged unreachable too and was kept: `renderSwitcherOptions` is also called from the picker's change handler, where the user has just chosen an entry and something *is* loaded — reachability has to be judged across both call sites. *Rejected (2026-08-01):* keep auto-loading the nearest plausible entry (in practice the franchise's season 1) behind the warning banner — a missed banner puts wrong-show sentences into permanent Anki cards, and the card outlives the mistake while a blank track doesn't. Playback is never blocked; only subtitle loading stops.

**2026-07-31 — Jimaku searches widen instead of failing.**
Jimaku's search is a substring match on its own titles, so a spelling it doesn't hold returns nothing — how four titles that exist in the catalogue came back "No Jimaku entry found" in the live pass. A zero-result search now retries on progressively broader queries (the six-rung ladder), each rung and match rule measured against live responses. *Rejected:* treating zero results as genuinely absent and leaning on manual upload — a real dead end for content Jimaku actually covers.

**2026-08-01 — "Is this episodic TV?" asked of the season name.**
The 2026-07-31 work treated a missing `partOfSeason` block as the definition of non-episodic content, which is wrong both ways: Crunchyroll lists OVA/OAD collections *as* seasons, and a film has no block but got a fabricated `wantedSeason` of 1. A fixture suite found three live failures resting on that one proxy. All three now branch on a format classifier read from the season name, plus a tier matching Crunchyroll's format against the format in a Jimaku entry's own name. OVA/OAD/ONA are deliberately one class — the same format under different names, and Jimaku files Attack on Titan's OADs under "OVA". Ambiguity degrades to the picker: a franchise with several side formats of the same class matches none of them. *Rejected:* fixing the three symptoms where they surfaced (a special case for OVA-named seasons, a widened file fallback, a corrected log string), which would have left the same wrong question asked in four places.

**2026-08-01 — Non-episodic content identified by its own title.**
Three films failed three different ways from one gap: nothing in resolution read the episode's own title, so nothing said *which* film a page was — and the search never looked for the film at all. `content.js` now reads `name` from the same JSON-LD block; for non-episodic content the resolver runs an **additional** search on that title and merges the results (the ladder can't help — it only widens when a rung returns nothing, and a franchise query returns plenty); and `matchEntryByContentTitle` identifies the entry, ties broken by format class. **Films must be identified by title; collections need not be:** a franchise often has several films, so "the only film-class entry here" is whichever film the search happened to return (the Mugen Train failure), whereas a franchise has one OVA bucket and one specials bucket, where the format word *is* the identity. A movie with no title evidence resolves to nothing and asks. *Rejected:* patching each symptom where it surfaced, which would have left the missing signal missing. *Revised the same day, three times, as live pages arrived:* (1) Mugen Train's `partOfSeason.name` already *was* the film title, so the failure was purely search coverage; and the compound `name` is not a title (verbatim it returns zero results), so it is split back into its parts, with the season name as a third candidate. (2) Memory Snow's `EEX` code and "(Director's Cut)" qualifier broke that split, so the code became an opaque token, anything after a pipe is taken, a qualifier-stripped variant was added, and format-word-only candidates are dropped — the second time an inferred fixture shape had passed while the real page failed, which became the verbatim-fixture rule. (3) Infinity Castle and The Last Attack name the title twice with no code and have no format word, so both were classed as TV and reproduced the original bugs on fixtures that had passed on invented metadata; the duplication itself became the signal (`looksLikeStandaloneWork`), and a single-result search became an identification (`soloHit`) — ordered below the format match (an episode-title search can return one unrelated show) and above the exact-title match (whose bare-franchise match is the flagship-series fallback), under a two-way franchise check, because the two films are arranged differently on Crunchyroll and that's why they failed differently.

**2026-08-01 — A lone search result must contain every query word.**
Chronicle loaded The Last Attack's subtitles, confidently and silently — the shape the "load nothing" decision was meant to prevent, arriving through `matchEntryByFullTitle`'s single-result branch (`wasFullQuery && entries.length === 1`), which accepted it with no containment check at all and predated the franchise check. The floor: every word of the query must appear in the entry's name. Weaker than containment on purpose — it still admits an entry that merely says more ("the Movie:"), while rejecting a bucket name ("movies") the entry doesn't contain. *Rejected:* reusing the two-way franchise check, which rejects both cases. Both are adjacent fixtures, so the floor can't be tightened until it breaks the one it must not.

**2026-08-02 — On a split franchise, the bare-franchise entry is season 1.**
Tokyo Ghoul's "Root A" resolved confidently to season 1's entry: nothing lexical reaches Jimaku's "√A", so the exact-title tier matched the franchise name. The fallback is now declined — but only on visible evidence of splitting (some entry with a season marker above 1). Where the season truly can't be reached, the picker is the honest outcome. *Rejected:* declining the bare-franchise fallback everywhere — One Piece's single "ONE PIECE" entry genuinely holds all 24 arc seasons and every arc resolving to it is correct. Both arrangements were measured before the rule was written; both are fixtures.

**2026-08-12 — A later season's own name refuses season 1's entry.**
Crunchyroll's generic "Season N" titles and Jimaku's arc-named entries share nothing, so Dr. STONE and Fruits Basket later seasons loaded season 1's subtitles silently. Decided 2026-08-11 to decline rather than guess, but left unbuilt because "no entry matches season N" had no precise definition (the tiers tested only the chosen entry, not the candidate set). Built 2026-08-12 by avoiding that definition problem: we never decide which entry a season *should* be, only that the bare-franchise entry is season 1 (2026-08-02) and this season isn't, so that entry is wrong by definition. Two measured constraints: read the season *name*, never `seasonNumber` (a list position — it would decline all of One Piece's arcs); and only where the franchise genuinely has several works, not counting Jimaku `movie`-flagged entries (Naruto: Shippuuden would otherwise be blanked). Fruits Basket also needed the ladder to look past a single first hit, and its season 2 now resolves outright. Consistent with the 2026-08-01 no-valid-entry reasoning: wrong-show sentences reach permanent Anki cards, and a missed banner is cheaper than a wrong card. *Rejected:* mapping "Season N" onto the Nth Jimaku entry of the franchise — Jimaku orders search results by relevance, not chronology, so it would be a guess that *looks* authoritative, the exact failure mode of the bug.

**2026-08-12 — A stage play gets the entry picker, not the anime.**
"Chainsaw Man The Stage" rendered the TV series' episode 1 over a stage play. A `stage` content class is refused unless something names it specifically — a different medium an anime subtitle index will never hold, so this is classification, not a heuristic, and a false positive only costs the picker. *Rejected:* broadening `franchiseIsSplit` to "any entry extending the franchise name" — One Piece's results are full of such entries, and its 24 arcs correctly resolve to the one "ONE PIECE" entry, so the broader rule would decline all 24.

**2026-09-18 — A named season is never matched by its list position alone.**
The season-number tier trusted Crunchyroll's `seasonNumber` whenever the season name had no number of its own — but that field is a list position, so for a season with a distinct name the position is the only evidence, and it is known not to be evidence (Kaiju No. 8: Mission Recon). A number is now used only when it came from the name, or when the page publishes no distinct name (KonoSuba 3 still resolves); what falls through meets the existing guards and declines to the picker. *Rejected:* trusting the position when it agrees with an entry's marker — Mission Recon's does, which is the bug.

### Episode & file selection

**2026-07-05 — Best-subtitle ranking: real popularity signal first, uploader list as fallback.**
Ranking would use a real Jimaku popularity signal if one existed, with a small, explicitly temporary hardcoded uploader list as the fallback; whatever the method, candidates are always hard-filtered to the exact show and episode. *Rejected:* ranking by an uploader's episode-completeness across the season — most active groups sub a whole season, so it ties constantly and doesn't discriminate. *Rejected:* recency as a tiebreak — trusted groups often upload *later* than obscure ones (more time on QC), so it would favour the wrong provider. *Revised 2026-07-15:* the fact-finding check this was gated on came back "no" — Jimaku exposes no per-file or per-entry popularity signal at all (Jimaku entry resolution) — so the hardcoded list is the only path, not a later choice. And since uploader identity exists only as a bracket tag in the filename (e.g. `[Haruhana]`), matching against the list has to be filename-substring based, not a field comparison.

**2026-07-06 — `fileHint` kept as a narrow language-track rule.**
Added as a stopgap when Naruto: Shippuuden's per-season-numbered releases (all tagged "episode 1" by Jimaku) would have served the wrong episode: `FETCH_SUBTITLES` took an optional case-insensitive filename substring, preferring a matching file and otherwise behaving as before — a narrow, temporary knob for getting a known-good file during testing. Used again on 2026-07-12 when Witch Hat Atelier episode 1's files included a Chinese+Japanese "[CHS, JPN]" track from the wanted uploader: `FILE_HINT` was set to `"[JPN]"` for the Japanese-only cut. *Rejected:* building automatic episode-number extraction from filenames (`SxxE01`/`第NNN話`) right then — real best-subtitle-selection territory that Phase 4.5 already scoped deliberately, so an ad hoc version risked a worse, less-validated copy of planned work. *Revised 2026-07-17:* the hint had been searching *every* candidate, silently overriding both the ranking and the saved uploader preference on Witch Hat Atelier; it now matches only among files sharing `ranked[0]`'s own bracket tag. *Rejected (2026-07-17):* removing the hint once ranking existed — it solves a different problem (choosing *within* one uploader's releases) that ranking between uploaders structurally can't. *Revised 2026-08-01:* rechecked whether the general non-episodic fix made it redundant; it doesn't, and its original purpose is gone — Naruto: Shippuuden is episodic (so the non-episodic `?episode=` change never touches it), and its files are unbracketed Hulu rips, so `topTag` is undefined and the tag-scoped hint returns before comparing anything. What the hint does is pick one uploader's JPN-only cut over its dual CHS+JPN cut, confirmed still live on Witch Hat Atelier's Haruhana release: a general language-track rule born from a per-show bug.
