Extracted into `applyFileHint` and pinned by four cases (one asserting it can't fire on the Naruto shape) so the question isn't asked a third time.

**2026-07-15 — Uploader list seeded from this project's own testing.**
With no popularity signal, ranking needed a starting list. Eric was asked rather than a list picked from outside knowledge (a product-quality judgment, not a technical unknown), and chose SubsPlease, Haruhana and VCB-Studio — each already seen working in this project's live testing — treated as a living list to extend later. *Rejected:* researching a fuller list from general fansub reputation before any real multi-uploader case had been hit.

**2026-07-29 — Cour-split entries retried on empty, not merged up front.**
Jimaku splits a two-cour season into separate `Part N` entries while Crunchyroll presents one continuous season, so episodes past the boundary had no files in the chosen entry and failed outright. Siblings are retried only when the chosen entry returns nothing — an extra request exactly on the episodes that would otherwise have errored, with the normal path untouched. *Rejected:* merging every same-season entry's files up front — it doubles the request count on every load to serve the minority past a boundary, and blurs which entry a file came from in the switcher, which exists precisely so the resolved entry is visible.

**2026-08-01 — Non-episodic files narrowed by episode title first.**
An OVA collection has one file per OVA, so listing them all showed Memory Snow and The Frozen Bond the same four files — the switcher offering nothing to choose by. The listing is now narrowed by the episode's own title first, on the punctuation-insensitive key, so release-name separators don't defeat it. *Rejected:* narrowing to an empty list when nothing matches — worse than not narrowing, so only a match narrows (asserted by a fixture).

**2026-08-01 — Long-franchise episodes resolved from the entry's own filenames.**
On a series a provider splits into cours, Jimaku's `?episode=` filter collides across seasons (Naruto: Shippuuden), including a confident answer for the wrong episode. Unlike the OADs this can't be fixed by not asking, because the episode number is genuinely meaningful, so it is resolved locally: the entry's files carry both numberings, season boundaries are derivable from the listing, and magnitude separates the two readings without knowing which convention Crunchyroll uses. *Rejected:* the obvious trigger, "the results disagree about which episode they are" — it false-positives on ordinary shows (Frieren S2 legitimately mixes Amazon's `S02E01` and Netflix's `S02E29`); `第N話` is the discriminator instead.

**2026-08-01 — Jimaku's `?episode=` filter skipped for non-episodic content.**
Five consecutive OADs loaded a file whose uploader release tag ("OAD2", "OAD3") happened to match Crunchyroll's episode number. The matching happens in Jimaku's server-side filter; the legacy assumption on our side was that a non-empty `?episode=N` answer is meaningful — true for a numbered TV run, false for a side-format entry. Worse, title matching ran only when the filter returned *nothing*, so a wrong-but-non-empty answer was trusted outright. For non-episodic content the filter is now skipped and the whole listing goes through title matching and sibling exclusion. This was the general case of the 2026-07-06 per-show `fileHint` workaround, never revisited when the non-episodic work landed; it affects any side-format entry whose uploader numbering collides with Crunchyroll's. *Rejected:* validating the digits — there is no digit to validate on this side; the parsing happens on Jimaku's server. The fix is the mechanism: don't ask a question whose answer can't be meaningful.

**2026-08-01 — Non-episodic files ruled out by sibling episode titles.**
The fallback was showing files that belong to *other*, identifiable episodes (The Frozen Bond's list included Memory Snow's). Each file is now checked against every episode title in the season and dropped if it matches a different one, even when it can't be confirmed as this one. The asymmetry is the point: confirming a file *is* this episode needs the current title to survive translation into the uploader's naming (which fails on Re:Zero's OVAs); confirming it's *another* episode needs only one of a season's titles to land. If exclusion leaves nothing, that's reported as its own error — the entry was right, and a list of confirmed-wrong files is worse than an honest "nothing here". The titles come from Crunchyroll's own adjacent-episode lookups, accumulated across visits because one load yields only one or two; partial coverage still helps because this rules files out rather than in. The cache is keyed on the page's series + season titles, and both must agree before a title is trusted, checked in `content.js` (which has the JSON-LD) rather than in the MAIN-world sniffer (which has no extension state); it stays passive, with no extra request and no auth token. *Rejected:* only trying to positively match the current episode's title — it does nothing in exactly the case that was showing wrong files. *Rejected:* keying the cache on Crunchyroll's `season_id` — it appears only in sniffed responses, while the titles are on every page, so a cache written on one visit is findable on the next before anything is sniffed.

**2026-08-02 — The numbering offset is asked of Jimaku, not parsed from filenames.**
Shangri-La Frontier and My Dress-Up Darling are numbered absolutely on Crunchyroll and from 1 in Jimaku's season-2 entries, so the file exists but the number asked can't reach it. When a later-season lookup comes back empty, `?episode=1` is requested and the highest position stated in those filenames is read as that episode's absolute number, because Jimaku's own index already files the absolutely-numbered release under episode 1. Gated on a later-season entry, since a season-1 entry has no offset and a batch file under its episode 1 would send the retry somewhere arbitrary. *Rejected:* the Naruto filename method — it relies on files carrying both numberings, and neither entry has one. *Rejected:* comparing numbering populations — My Dress-Up Darling's two uploaders number differently with nothing in the number saying which, so it would derive an offset of 0.

**2026-08-11 — Only a bare trailing number is an ambiguous cour marker.**
Haikyu!!'s back-half entry is its arc title plus a bare number, which `parseSeasonMarker` reads as a season, so the cour retry rejected its own continuation. Two things had to be right: *which* names count as a continuation — only the chosen entry's name plus a **bare** trailing number (an explicit "Season 2" says which it is, and "Part 2" is already the cour retry's job); and that even that shape isn't conclusive (KonoSuba's "… 2"/"… 3" are real seasons), so the sibling's episodes must start *above* everything the chosen entry holds. The first attempt used `parseSeasonMarker`'s stripped base instead, which accepted Mushoku Tensei season 2 as a continuation of season 1 on a show that had been clean. *Rejected:* loosening the season comparison inside `courSiblingEntries`, which can express neither check.

**2026-08-12 — Cour answers checked for coverage and for the episode.**
Both faults were found by verifying the second-numbering fix, and both served an hour of the wrong show without complaint. A cour sibling must state the requested episode in its own filenames before being asked (Dr. STONE's Cour 3 answered "episode 13" with episode 37), and, within the cour retry, files positively stating a different episode are dropped (a cour numbered from its own start returns its Nth file beside episode N). *Rejected:* dropping differently-numbered files everywhere, not just in the cour retry — it would break the rule that narrowing keeps valid alternates, since Frieren's season 2 legitimately answers episode 9 with files numbered both 09 and 37.

**2026-08-12 — A second numbering is derived from the entry's own numbers.**
MHA: Vigilantes season 2 lost six episodes that exist only in the entry's second, offset numbering. It had been recorded as blocked on Crunchyroll's season episode range — wrongly: anchoring on "does the entry state episode 1" separates the three real arrangements from the entry alone (a back-half cour never states 1, so its episode 14 can't be sent to 27). Because a derived offset is a guess until confirmed, it is guarded (a contiguous run of at least 3 files, the episode absent from the lower run, the result must return files) and runs last, only after everything cheaper has failed, so nothing that works today can reach it; My Dress-Up Darling, with one contiguous run, stays with the Jimaku-index method. *Rejected:* passing Crunchyroll's season episode range into `resolveTextFiles` — never needed.

**2026-08-12 — A title-narrowed work then selects its numbered part.**
Chainsaw Man – The Compilation's two recap episodes narrowed to the same four files, each offering the other's subtitles. This re-admits per-file numbering, withdrawn on 2026-08-01 after Jimaku read release tags like "OAD2" as episode numbers, so it is fenced by three conditions that each rule that failure out: only among files already narrowed to one work by title; every file must state exactly one position (an unparseable tag disqualifies the set rather than being guessed at); and the positions must run 1..N with no gaps. *Rejected:* keeping per-file numbering fully withdrawn, which left each Compilation episode offering the other's subtitles.

**2026-09-18 — Every path that loads an entry's files applies the same episode rules.**
The remembered entry and the entry picker bypassed every episode rule and auto-loaded whatever file topped a whole-entry listing, marked confident. File retrieval is now one function (`filesForEntry`) for all three paths; a remembered entry that yields nothing falls back to ordinary resolution, and a manual pick that yields nothing lists the entry's files without loading any, so the user chooses. *Rejected:* just deleting the unfiltered fallback — it would break correct picks on absolutely numbered seasons, which only the resolver's offset retries can reach.

**2026-09-18 — An offset retry drops the population that stated the offset.**
Found while pinning the change above on Shangri-La's real listing: for a local number the entry doesn't hold, Jimaku answers with the absolutely-numbered uploader's file of that number, a different episode. Files whose name prefix matches the population that stated offset+1 are dropped from the retry. *Rejected:* requiring each retried file to state the local number — the absolute file states it too; only the population, not the numbers, tells them apart.

**2026-09-18 — A fractional-episode file never serves an integer episode.**
My Hero Academia season 1 episode 1's list carried a 13.5 recap special. Files stating a fractional position are dropped from an integer episode's list, and a list of nothing but such files fails loudly. *Rejected:* widening the `第N話` disagreement trigger to decimals — it re-resolves the episode from `第N話` alone and would drop every alternate that lacks one (Netflix, the "[Fixed]" retime), the narrowing the 2026-08-02 rule forbids. *Rejected:* treating differing `SxxEyy` season labels as proof of a mix — providers label the same episode's season differently.

### Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload

**2026-07-02 — Per-season uploader and offset memory: deferred, then designed.**
First pushed to Phase 6: a quality-of-life layer on an already-working manual offset tool, better designed once a real user base existed — while still a known UX shortfall against ManabiDojo. *Revised 2026-07-04:* un-deferred with a concrete design, because a show's uploader and offset choice turns out to be stable for its whole run in practice (not a per-episode judgment), which removes most of the design risk behind deferring: cache the chosen uploader and offset per show and season (a new season may reasonably use a different uploader); auto-apply on any new episode of a configured show+season with no re-prompt; keep the per-episode manual override for the rare episode that needs it, without overwriting the season default; make it viewable and clearable. *Revised 2026-07-05:* moved into the new Phase 4.5 (Process & roadmap). Since built: uploader memory (2026-07-16), entry memory (2026-08-13), offsets carried per release group (2026-08-15). The view/clear UI is still unbuilt. *Rejected (2026-07-04):* waiting for a real user base before designing it.

**2026-07-05 — Uploader preference scoped per show and season.**
Reuses the show+season scoping already designed for offset memory rather than inventing new scoping — one design for both features, not two to keep in sync. *Rejected:* one global "preferred uploader across all anime" — different shows are subbed by different groups with different reliability. *Rejected:* per-episode scope (re-prompting every episode) — defeats "no re-prompt" memory, and a season's uploader choice is stable in practice. *Revised 2026-09-18:* the key now includes the season name (below).

**2026-07-05 — A saved uploader preference is sticky.**
When the preferred uploader has no file for an episode, that one episode silently falls through to the best-ranked option and the saved preference stays intact for the rest of the season. *Rejected:* clearing or overwriting the preference on a single missing episode — a temporary gap (an uploader skipping one episode) shouldn't discard a standing preference the user chose.

**2026-07-05 — Phase 4.5's UI ships rough; polish deferred to Phase 6.**
Auto-select and the manual switcher panel ship functional only, matching the project's function-before-polish pattern; polish is bundled with the dictionary-popup redesign already planned for Phase 6. *Rejected:* polishing Phase 4.5's UI in isolation now and revisiting it later.

**2026-07-06 — Manual upload parses in the content script.**
`subtitle-parser.js` is loaded as a content script, so `content.js` calls `parseSrt`/`parseAss` directly after reading the file with `FileReader` — the same sharing pattern as `tokenize-utils.js`. The cue-rendering `timeupdate` listener was refactored to attach once, reading the shared `cues`, so a Jimaku fetch and an upload both just populate it (previously only the Jimaku path attached a listener); an upload dispatches a synthetic `timeupdate` so a paused video updates at once. At the time the Jimaku path was left without that forced refresh, as an unrelated quirk outside the task. *Revised 2026-07-17:* the forced refresh was added to the automatic and switcher paths too (Subtitle overlay, sync & offset). *Rejected:* routing uploads through a `background.js` message — a `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` round trip plus the serialization problems of passing a `File` object.

**2026-07-15 — Switcher data rides on `FETCH_SUBTITLES`, not a new message.**
The panel needs the full ranked candidate list, which `fetchSubtitles` already computes before picking its winner, so its existing response was extended with `files` and `selectedUrl`; a manual pick uses its own `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE` message (just `url`/`name`, nothing to search). *Rejected:* a separate `LIST_SUBTITLE_FILES` message — it would query Jimaku's search and file list twice per episode for identical data, doubling real calls against the 25-requests-per-minute budget.

**2026-07-16 — Uploader preference applied inside the ranking pass.**
`rankFiles()` takes the preference directly and ranks it first, so `fetchSubtitles` downloads the right file the first time — and the sticky fallback needs no special-case code, since an unmatched preference behaves exactly like an absent one. *Rejected:* auto-loading the default file and then swapping with a second `FETCH_SUBTITLE_FILE` call — an extra download and parse on every episode of every show with a saved preference, the common case once the feature is used.

**2026-07-16 — Only the uploader's bracket tag is remembered.**
The tag is the one part of a filename that stays stable across a show's episodes. Unbracketed direct-source files (Netflix/Amazon rips) create no preference when picked, which is exactly the behaviour from before the feature existed — a case it doesn't extend to, not a regression. *Rejected:* saving the file URL (stale as soon as the episode changes) or the raw filename (episode numbers and titles differ file to file). *Rejected:* a source-name heuristic for unbracketed files — over-engineering a real but rare case with no clear general pattern.

**2026-07-26 — The resolved entry name is shown in the switcher.**
Logged because it addresses *why* the wrong-season bug kept recurring: filenames look equally right from any season's entry, so a wrong entry was invisible until the subtitles went wrong mid-episode. The entry title makes the season in use visible at a glance, and a season fallback also logs a console warning. *Rejected:* making a name mismatch an error state — shows whose seasons Jimaku doesn't split legitimately resolve to one season-1-looking entry for every season, so a mismatch is a signal to surface, not a failure to block on.

**2026-08-13 — The picked Jimaku entry is remembered per season.**
Declining to guess (Jimaku entry resolution, 2026-08-12) traded a silent wrong load for an entry picker — acceptable once per season, but the pick was being repeated every episode because it was never saved. The chosen entry id is now stored under the same key as the uploader preference. Three deliberate properties: reachable only after a manual pick; any failure to use it falls back to ordinary resolution, because a memory the user can't escape is worse than none; and the picker stays visible, since hiding it would make the memory a one-way door. *Rejected:* leaving the pick unsaved, which made a declined season cost one pick per episode instead of one per season.

**2026-08-13 — An already-rejected lone candidate isn't offered as a choice.**
Showing the picker for a single candidate (previously it needed several) exposed a trap: on Chronicle the one candidate is a different film the resolver had already ruled out, so "pick one" moved the wrong-subtitles bug one click away. That lone candidate now says what it is and points at manual upload, keeping the dropdown for anyone who knows better. *Rejected:* presenting it as an ordinary choice.

**2026-09-18 — Per-season memory keyed on the season's name, not its list position.**
The remembered entry, the uploader preference and the sibling-title cache were stored under series + season number, which is a list position shared by different works in the live captures — so a pick made for a film would apply to every episode of the TV season sharing its slot. One key function (`seasonMemoryKey`) now adds the season name, or the work's own title where a page has no season. *Rejected:* validating the remembered entry against the season at use time — nothing can, which is exactly why the user had to pick it. Cost: picks under the old key aren't migrated, since that key is the ambiguous thing being removed; a season needing a pick asks once more. *Confirmed by Eric 2026-09-20:* don't migrate — one extra ask is cheaper than carrying a pick that may have been made for the other work sharing the old key.

### Subtitle parsing & display filters

**2026-06-30 — Stage directions filtered in the extension, not only the batch script.**
`STAGE_RE` lives in `content.js`, because lines like （ドアの開く音） are never useful to the learner. *Rejected:* keeping the filter in the batch-test script only.

**2026-07-01 — Speaker-name prefixes stripped from the display entirely.**
Both conventions — `(Name)` and `Name:` — are removed, one of three user-directed fixes from that day's live test. *Rejected:* normalizing both conventions to one displayed style.

**2026-07-05 — Decorative markup stripped by Unicode class; real punctuation kept.**
Fansub markup (➡ continuation arrows, 》 bracket markers, 📺 TV-dialogue emoji, 🎵〜 music-note markers) carries no linguistic content in any provider's convention, so one global filter strips it — the same treatment as `STAGE_RE`, no per-provider logic. 「」 quote brackets stay: real Japanese punctuation, not a fansub artifact. *Revised 2026-07-27:* the rule had listed three specific characters (`➡📺🎵`), which is why a loudspeaker emoji on a KonoSuba stage direction sailed through; it became `\p{Extended_Pictographic}`, the "decoration, not language" class, with `‼`/`⁉` carved out (Unicode calls them pictographic, but they're ordinary sentence-final punctuation) and `♪`/`〜` outside the class anyway. *Revised 2026-07-29:* when Eric asked whether the 2026-07-27 fixes were general or fitted to the reported cases, an audit against real data found them still partly fitted. The principle adopted: state a rule as the class of thing it covers, and label any unavoidable enumeration as deliberate. The rule became the pictographic property plus the whole U+2600–27BF span (two Unicode blocks dedicated to decoration); the bracket pair `《》⸨⸩` stays an explicit, documented-as-forced list; geometric shapes and unit signs were exempted from measurement, not intuition; and `‼`/`⁉` stopped needing a special case once the rule keyed off symbol-vs-punctuation, which Unicode already encodes. (The same principle changed season name matching the same day — Jimaku entry resolution.) *Rejected (2026-07-27):* enumerating characters — the set of pictographs a fansub might use is open-ended, and each addition costs a code change. *Rejected (2026-07-29):* adding the specific characters the corpus scan turned up (`♬➨⚟`) — the same enumeration one round later.

**2026-07-26 — Dual-language files cleaned per ASS style, not excluded.**
Found while investigating the "Translation on the card front" report: `parseAss` kept every `Dialogue:` line and discarded `Style`, so a dual-language file fed both languages into the cues — confirming a gap `rankFiles`' own comment had flagged in the abstract, and affecting this project's main test show by default. Reported first rather than fixed, because the three possible designs (by style name, by per-cue script, or preferring a `[JPN]`-tagged filename) have different failure modes and choosing was a scope decision for Eric. Eric's call, the same day: keep these providers and strip the embedded track. Each *style* is classified by the majority script of the lines under it and kept or dropped wholesale — the style is the unit the fansubber used to separate the tracks, so the lyric-line and incidental-Latin cases fall out correctly for free. No per-provider special case was needed. *Rejected:* matching style names — real English styles are called `Default`, `Flashback`, `Signs`…, and in SubsPlease's own file the only informatively named style is the Japanese one. *Rejected:* per-line script detection — it deletes the genuine English lyric lines inside a Japanese ED and the incidental Latin lines of Japanese-only files. *Rejected:* excluding dual-language providers. *Revised 2026-09-18:* Japanese lines inside a dropped style are now rescued (below).

**2026-07-27 — One display-filter function for both screen and Anki.**
The filter chain existed twice, verbatim, in `updateJapaneseCue` and `cueDisplayText`, the second carrying a comment promising it matched the first, with nothing enforcing that — and the same day's markup-ordering fix would have reached only one copy, silently desyncing the screen from a merged Anki sentence. Now one function called by both. *Rejected:* keeping two copies in sync by hand.

**2026-08-15 — The Japanese full stop stripped from display; the comma kept.**
Eric's request from live use: Japanese subtitling uses a space where prose uses 。, most releases already do, and the ones that don't look inconsistent beside them. Applied in `cueDisplayText`, so the Anki `Sentence` matches the screen by construction; replaced with a space, not deleted, so two sentences in one line don't run together. *Rejected:* also stripping 、 (mid-sentence phrasing, not asked about) or ？／！ (they carry tone the sentence would lose).

**2026-09-18 — Timestamps read as base-60 fields of any count.**
The corpus sweep found film releases writing times past one hour with four fields, which the three-field reader turned into NaN, silently dropping the second half of each film. *Rejected:* a special case for the four-field shape — the positional reading also covers the `.`-separator and missing-hours shapes the audit's adversarial fixtures exercise, which no enumeration of seen shapes would.

**2026-09-18 — .srt tag-shaped markup stripped as a class.**
`<i>`, `<font …>`, `<rb>` and similar were rendering literally, on screen and in the Anki sentence; `parseSrt` now strips anything tag-shaped and replaces gaiji placeholders with 〓. Amazon-style ruby with a lost closing tag is kept character-for-character rather than guessing where the reading ends. *Rejected:* listing the tags seen — the same enumeration-one-round-later the 2026-07-29 decision rejected.

**2026-09-18 — Typesetting layers kept out of the dialogue track.**
Karaoke typeset one glyph per event, and some files emit each line once per layer, so lyrics rendered one clickable character per line and lines showed (and reached Anki) doubled. Both are defined by structure, not content: a single-glyph event among three or more others is an effect layer (dropped at parse time), and identical simultaneous text is shown once (at the display join, which the Anki merge reads). *Rejected:* dropping events by style name or by `\pos` — `\pos` is on most events in an ordinary dialogue file, and style names identify nothing (2026-07-26).

**2026-09-18 — Display filters run per line, after removing invisible characters.**
The sweep's largest parsing cluster was speaker labels surviving on a cue's *second* line, because the label and stage-direction tests only ever saw the start of the whole cue; invisible direction marks and names carrying their own katakana reading defeated the same anchored tests. Format characters are now removed first, a whole-cue stage direction is still dropped whole, and the rest is handled line by line, readings before labels; a colon between digits is never a label. *Rejected:* widening the speaker regex to match after a newline — it would still miss the invisible-mark and nested-reading shapes, the same failure of a test that assumes one clean line. *Kept as-is, as ambiguous:* parenthesised speech, which shape alone can't tell from a sound effect.

**2026-09-18 — Japanese lines rescued from dropped dual-language styles.**
The style-level strip was dropping kana-bearing dialogue that fansubbers had put in English-majority or oddly named styles. Inside a dropped style, a line is kept when hiragana is at least a quarter of its Japanese characters: Japanese carries particles and okurigana, while the Chinese credits in these styles contain at most a の inside a name. Katakana is deliberately not counted, since credits use it for names — so a katakana-only exclamation in a dropped style is still lost, accepted. *Rejected:* lowering the style-level threshold — it is what keeps English lyric lines inside a Japanese style (Frieren's `ED_JP`), so moving it trades one loss for another.

### Subtitle overlay, sync & offset

**2026-07-05 — Offset-reset hotkey matched by `event.code`, not `event.key`.**
On a Mac, Alt+0 produces "º", so the `event.key === "0"` check never matched. `event.code` reports the physical key whatever character a modifier produces. *Rejected:* enumerating every possible Alt-shifted character across keyboard layouts.

**2026-07-05 — Auto-offset-detection on Jimaku import scheduled for Phase 6.**
Align the subtitle offset automatically against Crunchyroll's own subtitle timing, replacing the first-watch manual nudge; the per-episode manual offset stays as an override. This closes the last open Jimaku-import UX gap against ManabiDojo — per-season memory and the title-search mismatch had already been decided on 2026-07-04. *Rejected:* keeping the first-watch manual nudge as the only way to set the offset.

**2026-07-17 — Forced re-render reused on every path that sets cues.**
The manual-upload path already dispatched a synthetic `timeupdate` after setting `cues` (a paused video won't fire one otherwise, so the display sits stale); the automatic load and the switcher pick didn't, which caused "sometimes stuck on *Loading subtitles*". Both now do the same thing. At the time `video` was threaded to them as a parameter, rejecting a module-scope variable as an unnecessary global for three call sites. *Revised the same day:* `video` became module-scope after all, because a closure elsewhere kept a stale element (Show & episode detection). *Rejected:* inventing a different mechanism for each path.

**2026-07-26 — Subtitle boxes stacked in one flex column, not two viewport anchors.**
The JP/EN vertical-order bug had two fixes on the table. The order is now structural: one bottom-anchored `#jp-immersion-subtitle-stack` with the two boxes as flex children, so the order can't vary at any caption length. The stack sits at the Japanese box's *original* 60px rather than the English box's 20px, so the Japanese-only case — the already-live-tested primary feature — renders pixel-identical, with no regression. *Rejected:* keeping both boxes independently `position: fixed` and recomputing the English box's offset from the Japanese box's rendered height — the fragile option the bug report itself warned against: a read-then-write layout dependency that must re-run on every caption change, font/zoom change and fullscreen toggle, and gets the order wrong for one frame each time.

### Tokenizing & grouping

**2026-06-23 — Stay client-side; kuromoji.js is the v1 analyzer.**
kuromoji.js is pure JS with no WASM toolchain, and shares MeCab/IPADIC lineage with Ichiran itself. lindera-wasm/Vibrato-wasm are to be benchmarked only if real corpus testing shows a quality gap. *Rejected:* matching ManabiDojo's server-side stack (Ichiran/Ichimoe + CaboCha) — it would take on the backend-cost structure implicated in their reliability complaints and undercut our pricing position.

**2026-06-24 — Auxiliary merging fixed by a grouping rule, not a tokenizer swap.**
Merging 助動詞 tokens into the preceding word (生まれた had been splitting into 生まれ + the unrelated noun 田) was a grouping/boundary problem, not a tagging-accuracy one — the origin of today's Rule 3. *Rejected:* pulling the lindera/Vibrato benchmark forward, which wouldn't have helped.

**2026-06-30 — Te-form chains split into two clickable units.**
Main verb + て is one unit and the auxiliary is another, so the learner can look up both the verb's meaning and the auxiliary's nuance. *Rejected:* merging the whole chain into one unit.

**2026-06-30 — Shared `tokenize-utils.js` instead of a copy in the batch script.**
`groupTokens`/`JAPANESE_WORD_RE` live in one module used by the extension and `scripts/batch-test.js`. *Rejected:* duplicating them in the batch script, where the two implementations would silently diverge.

**2026-06-30 — Rule 1 always merges a verb with て/で.**
The te-form merge no longer depends on the following token. *Rejected:* keeping that constraint, which blocked correct merges for terminal and non-auxiliary uses (乗り遅れて, 止まっていい).

**2026-07-01 — Fragmented words rejoined by a JMdict-anchored merge.**
The planned kuromoji "user dictionary" doesn't exist (the feature is dead code), so `findKanaMergeCandidates`/`applyKanaMerges` rejoin fragments whose joined form exists in JMdict — self-generalizing to any show with no per-word maintenance. *Rejected:* recompiling the binary IPADIC dictionary offline — too heavy for an open-ended problem that's never "complete" (anime slang is an unbounded long tail). *Rejected:* a hardcoded broken-word list — the same objection, and the root cause turned out to be Viterbi cost and punctuation adjacency, not missing vocabulary.

**2026-07-01 — Kana-merge gated on function-word POS codes.**
Every correct merge matched a function-word POS code, while every false positive matched only a content-word one. *Rejected:* gating on the `common` flag — it rejected real fixes like かくれんぼ. *Rejected:* no gate — real false positives at 1257-line corpus scale.

**2026-07-01 — A general phrase-matcher, not phrase-by-phrase code.**
Built to an explicit design mandate and hardened in three corpus-driven rounds: existence only → a function-POS gate (to reject false positives like にし→"west"); the gate then wrongly rejected real compounds (文化祭), so all-名詞 spans need only existence while mixed spans need the gate; that wrongly rejected ひとりぼっち (mis-tagged by the ぼっち segmentation bug), so any 3+-group span was accepted on existence alone, since every false positive found was 2-group and every real phrase 3+. (The gate's current form is in Architecture.) *Rejected:* hardcoding phrases one by one.

**2026-07-01 — Pronoun + plural suffix handled by a deterministic Rule 0.5.**
私たち/僕たち are merged by a dedicated rule, because `pn` isn't a function-word POS code (so they'd fail the matcher's own gate) and the pattern is always grammatical whatever the pronoun's headword status. *Rejected:* leaving them to the general phrase-matcher.

**2026-07-01 — Rule 3 absorption narrowed to an explicit allowlist.**
Two passes the same day: first restricted to verb/adjective-preceding tokens (fixing 僧侶だろ and freeing んだ for the phrase-matcher), then rewritten from "any 助動詞 not excluded" to a positive allowlist (`PURE_INFLECTION_AUX` + a plain-copula check via `conjugated_form`), so でしょう/だろう/らしい/べき/まい stay separate. Chosen by applying Eric's "does this carry independent meaning?" test uniformly. *Rejected:* special-casing each construction individually.

**2026-07-01 — Katakana runs inert; kanji names clickable like ordinary words.**
The first fix suppressed the popup entirely for any proper-noun-tagged word with no JMdict entry. *Rejected (2026-07-01):* adding JMnedict name translations instead — deferred at the time, declined outright on 2026-07-04 (Product & scope). *Revised 2026-07-04:* narrowed to katakana-only runs. Kanji names carry real character-level meaning worth a click even without a name entry; arbitrary katakana transliterations (ヒンメル, アイゼン) have nothing to decompose, which is exactly what the inertness targets. The real problem was over-suppression, not missing name data. *Revised 2026-07-05:* implemented by deleting `onWordClick`'s `isProperNoun` silent-close branch and its plumbing, because katakana-only runs never reach the click handler at all (they're already inert upstream), so that branch only ever suppressed kanji names. *Rejected (2026-07-05):* adding a kanji-vs-katakana distinction there — it would duplicate a check already made upstream.

**2026-07-01 — Katakana names detected by JMdict existence, not the tag alone.**
kuromoji's 固有名詞 tag is inconsistent on fictional names, so a standalone katakana word with no JMdict entry is treated as a name too. *Rejected:* relying on the tag alone.

**2026-07-01 — Phrase-matching runs on raw kuromoji tokens.**
Per an explicit design requirement: 帰ったら arrives as one combined token, so Rule 3 would absorb it before any scan of grouped output could see a たら candidate. Implemented via a `fuseSpans` parameter into `groupTokens` plus per-group token-range tracking. *Rejected:* scanning `groupTokens`' output after the fact.

**2026-07-01 — A matched phrase can attach as a note instead of replacing words.**
Dual-view applies when both boundary tokens are genuine content words worth their own click (もの…なる). *Rejected:* dual-view for every match — じゃ/か/ら/だ carry no useful standalone meaning the way もの/なる do, so exposing them again would undo the Rule 3 allowlist work.

**2026-07-01 — と always gets dual-view, never a fuse.**
Even beside a negative verb (探さないと). *Rejected:* treating と like じゃ/か/ら/だ (always fuse) — と keeps a common, unrelated job (plain reported-speech and conditional と) far more often than the ないと "have to" idiom, so a fuse would mis-gloss ordinary quotation as obligation.

**2026-07-01 — ん + copula handled by a deterministic Rule 0.6.**
JMdict has a headword for んだ but not for んだろう/んでしょう, so the matcher's exact-string anchoring can't reach the extended forms however it's tuned. *Rejected:* leaving it to the phrase-matcher.

**2026-07-01 — Phrase candidates must align with the baseline grouping.**
A candidate span that `groupTokens` already produces correctly on its own is rejected. *Rejected:* accepting the resulting noise as residual (like があん) — this collision (い+た vs 板 "board") is far higher-frequency, appearing in nearly every past-progressive scene. *Revised 2026-07-05:* generalized from exact-span matches to boundary alignment — a candidate must start and end exactly on a baseline-group edge, never inside one. *Rejected (2026-07-05):* a name-specific exception for each of the two reported cases.

**2026-07-01 — Suspicious-fragment signal: two adjacent short verb/adjective tokens.**
`hasSuspiciousFragment` fires on two adjacent ≤2-character 動詞/形容詞 tokens, the signature of a segmentation error like ぼっ+ち. Reached through two corrections. *Rejected:* any single 1-character verb/adjective token (too broad — it caught normal conjugation stems like し for する and い for いる and caused new collisions). *Rejected:* exactly 1 character — it missed ぼっ, which is 2 UTF-16 code units.

**2026-07-03 — 王都 fixed by keeping pure noun+noun spans out of dual-view.**
The fuse gate already accepted such spans; the bug was `isDualViewMatch` claiming them first and burying the compound's own definition (王都) under its first half's (王, "king"). Confirmed it doesn't regress dual-view's real case: もの…なる always has a particle between its boundary tokens, so it's never all-名詞. *Rejected:* changing the fuse-eligibility gate.

**2026-07-03 — でしょう/だろう resolved by an override in Rule 3.**
Rule 3's group construction uses the reconstructed surface as the lookup word, mirroring how Rule 0.6 special-cases んでしょう/んだろう, so でしょう/だろう reach their own headword instead of です/だ's "be; is". The baseline grouping's *span* was right but its *word* wasn't, which the redundancy filter can't see. *Rejected:* relaxing the phrase-matcher's baseline-redundancy filter, whose job is still valid in general. *Revised the same day:* extended to the bare conjecture stem with no う (そうだろ/そうでしょ, a real spoken form, not a truncation), after Eric asked whether the fix was general — it now always uses the reconstructed surface. *Revised the same day again:* narrowed from `conjugated_form !== "基本形"` to exactly `=== "未然形"`, after Eric's corpus run caught the broad gate turning past-tense でした/だった into lookup misses.

**2026-07-03 — ば conditional absorption added through the litmus test.**
Unlike れる/られる/せる/させる (a bug), ば had never been in the allowlist, so adding it was a real "should we build this" question. Leaving it the one pure-inflection auxiliary not merged contradicted why て/た merging was worth doing: two clicks are worse than one on the conditional verb. Gated on `conjugated_form === "仮定形"`, so ば's unrelated "place" noun sense can't misfire. *Rejected:* leaving ば as a separate click.

**2026-07-04 — この先's dual-view primacy accepted as low severity.**
No other instance of this pattern (a demonstrative + noun competing with a lexicalized idiom) had appeared in testing; そのうち is a hypothetical parallel, not a seen one. Severity is low because dual-view hides neither reading: この/先 and the "Also, as a set phrase" note show together with no extra click — unlike the multi-reading bug (wrong furigana with no sign anything's off) or duplicate-gloss noise (contradictory-looking cards). Accepted as-is, but not unexamined. *Rejected:* fixing the primacy — dual-view already shows both readings together, and no second instance of the pattern had appeared.

**2026-07-05 — Numerals made clickable with their own check, not a wider regex.**
First deferred the same day, as a small new feature rather than a bug: `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` has no digits, and JMdict has no entries for bare numeral glyphs, so it needs reading logic. *Revised the same day:* scheduled into Phase 4 and built — a dedicated `NUMERAL_ONLY_RE` check in `groupTokens` plus reading synthesis at lookup (Dictionary data & lookup); 一人-style kanji counters needed no new work. *Rejected:* extending `JAPANESE_WORD_RE` to digits, as first scoped — it would leak numeral tokens into phrase-matching windows, kana-merge and katakana-run detection.

**2026-07-05 — Colloquial contractions accepted as a limitation after a general search.**
The decision was to look for a general reverse-expansion signal first — mirroring the godan potential-form fallback — and accept a permanent limitation if none existed. Investigated the same session: none does (Architecture). *Rejected:* hardcoding contractions case by case — the whack-a-mole already rejected for kana-merge and kanji heteronyms. *Also set aside:* letting background.js's POS-category filter hide 100% of results when kuromoji's tag and JMdict's POS codes fully mismatch — a broader change to a safety net that protects unrelated words, not a contraction mechanism.

**2026-07-05 — Multi-kanji name runs always fused; existence decides the display.**
Designed as "fuse a proper-noun span only if the whole span has a JMdict entry", reusing the existence pattern from kana-merge and katakana names. *Revised the same session after corpus testing:* the real bug (伊地知虹夏) has no JMdict entry either, so that rule wouldn't have fixed it; a detected run is now always fused, with existence deciding only what's displayed, and a length gate on `一般` tokens keeps false positives low. *Rejected:* the existence-gated fuse as originally scoped.

**2026-07-06 — Multi-word idioms come only from real JMdict headwords.**
Scoped as new work: a combination whose words are each correct but which means more together (それもそうか and 2–3 similar). The rule set before building: if the combination has its own JMdict headword, catch it with the existing existence-gated phrase-matcher; if not, leave it as an accepted limitation. *Revised the same session:* fact-finding showed the variants with headwords already fuse through the existing matcher, so nothing needed building. *Rejected:* writing a project-authored note explaining an idiom — content JMdict itself doesn't source, the same precedent as the kanji-heteronym, 入る and まま limitations.

**2026-07-30 — One render pipeline, `buildGroupsForText`, for the screen and the edit panel.**
The edit panel's "Change word" needs the stored sentence tokenized exactly as the subtitle line was, or a word could segment differently in the panel. The four-stage async chain was extracted from `renderCue` into one awaited function used by both. Behaviour change is confined to superseded lines finishing their group-building before being discarded rather than bailing mid-chain — a few wasted lookups on rapid subtitle changes, with the generation check still guarding the DOM write. *Rejected:* a second copy of the chain for the panel — the same duplication undone for the display filters on 2026-07-27, with a worse failure mode, since drift would silently produce a different word.

**2026-09-18 — kuromoji is only ever given text it returns intact.**
kuromoji deletes characters after runs of astral-plane characters, and the screen, the Anki sentence and the bold offset all inherit the loss. One wrapper, `tokenizeLossless`, isolates astral characters and verifies the round trip, falling back to per-character tokens if it ever fails. *Rejected:* relying on the display filter — it strips emoji but not astral kanji or symbols outside the pictographic class, and would rest the invariant on an unrelated filter. *Rejected:* patching kuromoji — a vendored third-party file this project doesn't otherwise modify; the wrapper protects against the whole class, not just the one length bug found.

**2026-09-18 — Grunts and stutters aren't looked up as verbs.**
A hiragana token in an attach-only form with nothing attached is looked up as itself under the interjection filter (showing "no entry" honestly when JMdict lacks it), kanji keeps the verb reading, and stutter fragments are unclickable — they have no meaning of their own. *Rejected:* a list of known grunts — the grammar (an attach-only form with nothing attached) states the class; a list would be the next round's bug report. *Rejected:* switching analyzer — UniDic disagrees in the other direction just as often (it splits counters and names differently), and the 2026-06-23 rule to benchmark alternatives only on evidence of a gap is served by the harness, not a swap. *Confirmed by Eric 2026-09-20:* "no entry" is the right answer for a cut-off kana verb (`あたっ…`); no warning-plus-verb variant.

### Dictionary data & lookup

**2026-06-23 — jmdict-simplified JSON, not raw EDRDG XML.**
The data comes from jmdict-simplified's JSON build. *Rejected:* parsing the raw EDRDG XML directly — its DTD entities are awkward to parse in JS.

**2026-06-30 — Particles clickable, filtered to their `prt` entries.**
An `isParticle` flag threaded from `content.js` to `background.js` keeps only `prt`-tagged entries, giving grammatical definitions instead of homophone nouns. *Rejected:* suppressing particles entirely (frustrating). *Rejected:* showing every matching entry (misleading homophone nouns).

**2026-07-01 — Half-width katakana normalized once, at the lookup layer.**
`normalizeHalfwidthKatakana()` runs at the single point every JMdict lookup passes through, so word clicks, kana-merge, phrase-merge and the katakana-name check all benefit from one fix. *Rejected:* normalizing at each candidate-generation call site — the same fix repeated four times. (On 2026-07-05 the subtitle text itself also started being normalized before tokenizing, so half-width releases *display* full-width too — Tokenizing & grouping.)

**2026-07-01 — いる/居る ordered by rare-kanji tags, then by real frequency.**
Fixed with the rK/sK ("rarely-used kanji") tags already in the source: an entry whose kanji forms are all rK/sK (or none) is conventionally kana-written, so it ranks first for a kana key. *Rejected:* sorting by priority tier or frequency — not possible, because jmdict-simplified's JSON exposes no priority data at all (confirmed by inspecting the raw release). *Revised 2026-07-03:* real usage frequency from TUBELEX now supersedes the heuristic wherever it has data (next entry); rK/sK stays as the fallback tiebreak.

**2026-07-01 — Homograph sense ordering solved with TUBELEX frequency data.**
こと's ordering needed real usage data. *Deferred on 2026-07-01:* the ideal domain-specific frequency list was gone from GitHub, the Leeds Corpus alternative had an unclear/NOASSERTION license (a risk this project avoids), and TUBELEX (BSD-3-Clause) looked viable but deserved its own focused, corpus-validated session. *Rejected (2026-07-01):* integrating TUBELEX as a rushed tail-end addition. *Revised 2026-07-03:* un-deferred and solved. TUBELEX was re-verified against the actual data rather than assumed: BSD-3-Clause, and its lemma field distinguishes exact kanji spellings (事 vs 琴), not just readings — exactly the missing signal. Built as a general re-ranking of every multi-entry index key (`scripts/apply-tubelex-frequency.js`), not a こと-specific patch. *Rejected (2026-07-03):* re-touching the raw jmdict-eng release — the compact index already links every spelling to its entry, so scores could be derived from data already in the repo plus the TUBELEX file.

**2026-07-02 — Frequency badge: a placeholder toggle first, real tiers later.**
Frequency had the same root cause as the こと ordering gap (only a collapsed `common` flag exists), so the toggle shipped as a UI placeholder to keep the toggle set's shape stable, with the real logic gated on the same dedicated session planned for こと; the separate common-word toggle was to go only once frequency shipped for real. *Revised 2026-07-04:* graduated to a real, graduated frequency tier built on the TUBELEX data from homograph ordering, replacing the binary common-word flag — closing a genuine parity gap with ManabiDojo's colour-coded badges. *Rejected (2026-07-04):* adding the tiers alongside the common-word toggle — a graduated signal supersedes a binary one outright, and keeping both would be redundant UI.

**2026-07-03 — Multi-reading display fix: deferred, then built with `rs`.**
An investigation into entries showing the wrong reading (くる → 刳る showing えぐる) corrected its own diagnosis (real multi-reading words, not index corruption) and was then deliberately not fixed — Eric's call, given ~11.2% of cases involve a common entry — and logged as a known limitation to prioritize later against real-world frequency. *Rejected:* a string-similarity threshold to separate the cases — it doesn't (でけえ/でかい scored lower than くる/えぐる). *Revised 2026-07-04:* rescheduled into active work (Eric's call: fix it now rather than wait), requiring compact entries to carry more than one reading, not a data prune; the ~37,000 affected (index key, entry) pairs and the ~11.2% common overlap were the starting reference. Built 2026-07-05 as the `rs` field plus a reading swap in `lookupWord`.

**2026-07-03 — Kanji heteronyms (一行) accepted as a permanent limitation.**
Checked before building anything: kuromoji's per-token reading is unreliable as an anchor, both in the reported case and in general. *Rejected:* anchoring sense choice to kuromoji's reading. *Rejected:* real sentence-level disambiguation — a much bigger lift that cuts against the no-AI/no-backend architecture, the same grounds as the AI-explanation exclusion. *Revised 2026-07-04:* re-verified before accepting permanently, because a second approach had never been evaluated — a small hardcoded collocate lookup per heteronym pair ("一行 preceded by 勇者/私たち-type words → いっこう, otherwise いちぎょう"), buildable without AI or a backend. Rejected too, not as impossible but because it doesn't scale: every pair needs its own hand-curated list maintained forever — the whack-a-mole already rejected for kana-merge — and only one real occurrence (一行) exists. Accepted on scope and maintenance grounds, not technical impossibility.

**2026-07-04 — JLPT sourced externally rather than dropping the toggle.**
Closed the 2026-07-02 question: the toggle stays either way — JMdict's own JLPT tags if jmdict-simplified had them, otherwise an explicit build task to find and integrate an external source (it didn't; see 2026-07-22). *Rejected:* dropping the JLPT toggle if the data wasn't built in.

**2026-07-04 — Frequency tiers: three text labels, no numbers, no default.**
Common / Uncommon / Rare, and no badge when TUBELEX has no data. Exact thresholds were left as an implementation detail (ideally percentile-based at the time), and visual treatment was explicitly out of scope, bundled into future card-visual design. *Rejected:* 4 tiers split Common/Very Common at the top — both mean "don't dwell", a distinction without a difference; a 4th tier would help more at the bottom ("Very Rare") if ever added. *Rejected:* showing the raw TUBELEX number — meaningless without context and forces interpretation mid-episode. *Rejected:* defaulting a no-data word to "Rare" — absence of data isn't evidence of rarity (the same reasoning as the そうか scoring fix). *Revised 2026-07-19:* thresholds locked as absolute counts (below).

**2026-07-05 — TUBELEX scores gated on POS-category compatibility.**
Found while investigating why 子's ね-reading (the zodiac sign) outranked こ ("child"): the earlier contamination fix only guarded against a *different* reading sharing a key (そうか/そうけ), not the *same* reading used by two grammatical categories, and TUBELEX files all ね usage under the particle. A coarse category cross-check (JMdict POS code vs TUBELEX's UniDic POS column), layered on top of the own-reading check, catches it. *Rejected:* a stricter reading-only gate — already tried, and it collapsed coverage from 59,703 to 9,450 entries.

**2026-07-05 — The compact file rebuilt from scratch rather than accept the 子 tie.**
After the POS gate, ね and こ tied on every scored criterion and the tiebreak (array position) reflected a stale order baked in by the earlier contamination bug, not JMdict's real order. First left unresolved and flagged to Eric rather than attempted solo: a clean fix needs a full rebuild, the original 2026-06-24 script was never saved, and reconstructing it from a description risked silent format drift across all 217k entries. *Rejected (then):* a one-off hardcoded reorder — the whack-a-mole rejected elsewhere. *Revised later the same day (Eric's call):* rebuild, reasoning that a contamination-era tiebreak likely affects more ties than 子. The "rebuild is risky" concern was met by reverse-engineering the exact algorithm and validating it statistically against the existing file before trusting it (Architecture). *Rejected:* patching the tiebreak logic (re-deriving "true" position from another signal) — the array position's only claim to meaning is that it traces back to JMdict's own curatorial order, so patching around a corrupted position would be guessing at a symptom fix.

**2026-07-05 — お's interjection ranking accepted as a permanent limitation.**
Initially assumed to share 子's root cause; it doesn't — お has no TUBELEX data at all, so there's no contamination to remove and no signal to add, and its "oh!" sense simply loses the common-flag-first tiebreak. First left open rather than claimed fixed by the bundled work; accepted permanently later the same day. *Rejected:* sourcing frequency data that doesn't exist, or weakening the common-flag tiebreak, which protects そうか's correct sense from TUBELEX's own coverage gaps — neither worth the cost for one word.

**2026-07-05 — 入る and まま orderings accepted as permanent limitations.**
Same grounds as the kanji-heteronym precedent: for 入る, TUBELEX collapses いる and はいる into one lemma with one count, so no data prefers はいる; for まま, all four homograph entries are real and none has TUBELEX data, leaving only accidental array position. *Rejected:* hardcoding "はいる wins" — the whack-a-mole already rejected for kana-merge and heteronyms.

**2026-07-05 — Godan potential-form fallback fires only after a lookup miss.**
kuromoji gives no conjugation signal for forms like 輝ける, so the fallback reverse-derives the plain godan form from the spelling (e-row → u-row) and looks that up, filtered to `v5*` entries. Firing only after a direct miss was deliberate: it bounds the false-positive risk Eric flagged (an e-row word that isn't a potential form) to words kuromoji didn't recognize at all whose derived form happens to be a real godan verb — anything with its own headword (食べる, 見える) never reaches it. Validated on the full 3-show corpus (Pattern 9) before being trusted, at Eric's request. *Revised the same day:* an irregular-verb (`vk`) match is preferred over a godan one under the same derived key, since ら抜き applies to 来る specifically (来れる had resolved to きたる). *Rejected:* running the fallback on every lookup.

**2026-07-05 — Vowel elongation collapsed for lookup only, never on screen.**
おぉ (a small vowel after its own large vowel, a real expressive convention) has no JMdict reading, the same shape of gap as half-width katakana, so the lookup falls back to the collapsed form. *Rejected:* also normalizing the displayed text, as the half-width fix does — the elongated spelling is the subtitle author's deliberate stylistic choice, not an encoding artifact, and should stay visible as written.

**2026-07-06 — Rare kanji spellings filtered out of `k` at generation time.**
Approved in a live-test round to fix 来る showing the obsolete kyūjitai 來る and じゃない showing ぢゃ無い: show only standard modern kanji spellings (`entry.k`) and, as first scoped, alternate kana readings (`entry.rs`), by excluding rK/sK/oK-tagged forms — the tag family already used for いる/居る. It was expected to need a data-model change (per-form tags on `k`/`rs`). *Revised the same day in the build:* `k` only ever needs to carry spellings worth *showing*, so rare forms (rK/sK/oK/iK) are simply excluded when the file is generated — no new field. `rs` was deliberately left unfiltered: it is never displayed, and filtering it would break its real job of resolving to the reading the user actually clicked, against the same day's "show what was actually on screen" rule. A bug caught mid-build: the first version fell back to the full kanji list when every form was rare, but an entry whose every kanji form is rare *is* the "conventionally kana-only" signal — the fallback was reintroducing ぢゃ無い — so it was removed. *Rejected:* adding per-form tags to the data model.

**2026-07-06 — Phrase-fused words filtered through a `__phraseFuse` POS sentinel.**
そうか showed unrelated nouns (層化, 装花) because phrase-fused groups never carried a `pos`, so the existing category filter (`POS_CATEGORY_MATCHERS`, built 2026-07-01 for exactly this class of collision) silently never ran on any of them. The fix reuses that filter: fused groups carry the sentinel `__phraseFuse`, mapped to `exp`/`int` — one more map entry and 100% of the existing plumbing, including the safety net. *Rejected:* deriving `pos` from one of the fused tokens (そう = 副詞) — そうか's real entry is `exp`, which no existing category matches, so it would filter out the right sense too, trip the "never narrow to zero" safety net and silently restore the bug.

**2026-07-06 — `m`/`ki` describe only the primary sense and reading.**
Once the archaic-tag check ran on every card (Popup display, same day), unions leaked tags from senses and readings never shown: こと showed "archaic" from its second sense, 君 "obsolete" from its third, じゃない "dated reading" from the alternate ぢゃない. The file was regenerated so `m` is the first included sense's tags and `ki` the primary reading's — what the headword and the duplicate check already treat as the card's identity. Re-validated on all three cases and the 2026-07-05 precedents (あたし/わらわ/酒), with batch-test unchanged. *Rejected:* restoring the `isDuplicate` gate on the tag check — it would resurrect the 呉る/こかす bug fixed that same day.

**2026-07-13 — Sense cap raised to 8 for particle/interjection entries only.**
Two live cases (の needs 6 senses, ただ今 needs 4) prompted a scan of the raw release first: content words have an unbounded tail of real senses, while particle/interjection-primary entries (806) are a small closed set of distinct grammatical functions, and a cap of 8 covers 99.5% of them. *Rejected:* a small global bump to 5 or 6 — it would just clear the two cases while still clipping real content for common verbs and cluttering the popup for words that don't need more.

**2026-07-19 — Frequency tiers use absolute TUBELEX counts, not percentiles.**
Eric was shown three absolute-count schemes and three percentile schemes, each with real bucket sizes and boundary-word spot-checks. His deciding factor was precision over recall on "Common": the badge is a trust signal for people who run their own SRS, so a false "this is common" is worse than an under-tagged common word. The Strict (20/500) scheme's boundary words read as more solidly common than the looser schemes' — that comparison, not the percentages, decided it. *Rejected:* percentile tiers (top 20%/30%/50%…), which would stay meaningful if TUBELEX coverage changed — in practice they landed almost exactly on the absolute "Middle" scheme, because the distribution is so Zipfian that the floor (10 vs 15 vs 20) barely moves the buckets and only the high cutoff does. Revisit only if a new TUBELEX version or corpus changes the distribution's shape.

**2026-07-19 — Orphaned entries found by a direct signal, not a proxy.**
Entries JMdict marks `common` but TUBELEX never scores are now identified directly — the `common` flag cross-checked against a real zero-TUBELEX-score test reusing `apply-tubelex-frequency.js`'s exact scoring — confirming 7,737 of them across both function and content words (closing the question of whether the gap was function-word-only). *Rejected:* the structural proxy "words whose reading splits into 2+ kuromoji tokens" — it tested out false; fragmentation and TUBELEX coverage are almost uncorrelated.

**2026-07-19 — Orphaned entries tiered by corpus counts, then nf priority.**
Real counts from the batch-test corpus win whenever a word appears; the raw XML's priority tags (a one-time scoped regex lookup, not a reversal of the 2026-06-23 JSON decision) are the fallback; and a bounded, enumerable political/institutional keyword check demotes that one confirmed newspaper-bias cluster by a tier — explicitly not a general register classifier. *Rejected:* corpus-only tiering — too incomplete, since most of these words (even でしょう) won't appear in a 3-episode corpus. *Rejected:* skipping nf priority entirely — throwing away complete, real data over a partial bias.

**2026-07-19 — A raw JMdict `id` added to every compact entry.**
The JMdict `ent_seq` lets orphaned entries be cross-referenced against the raw XML's priority tags, and was expected to serve the later JLPT work the same way (it did). *Rejected:* re-deriving the match by kanji/kana text every time — fragile, since homograph readings collide. The cost is small (~1.7MB) for a durable capability.

**2026-07-22 — JLPT source: stephenmk/yomitan-jlpt-vocab, not the elzup lineage.**
Both trace back to Jonathan Waller's list; the difference is packaging. stephenmk's CSVs carry the exact JMdict `ent_seq` per word, so the merge is a direct id join against the compact file's `id` (added 2026-07-19 anticipating this). *Rejected:* elzup/jlpt-word-list and its descendants — no JMdict keys, so it would need fuzzy kanji/reading matching (the approach that caused real contamination in the TUBELEX merge), and it re-licenses Waller's CC BY-SA content as MIT downstream, which doesn't obviously hold up. CC BY-SA 4.0 attribution still has to be surfaced (Open questions).

**2026-07-22 — JLPT conflicts resolved to the easier level.**
Inspected before assuming a parsing bug: the conflicting rows are Waller tagging different *spellings* of one JMdict entry at different levels, which a per-entry join can't preserve. The compact format already treats an entry's various spellings as context, not separately tagged data (the kanji-spelling display), so the easier level is the consistent default. *Rejected:* showing both levels, or picking the harder one.

### Popup display

**2026-07-03 — Noun + copula shown as "+ です (copula)", not hidden.**
凱旋+です had been mislabelled as the noun's own inflection. Both options were on the table; relabelling keeps the copula visible as real information. *Rejected:* suppressing the copula entirely.

**2026-07-03 — The popup label spec stayed a labelling change; grouping gaps split off.**
The POS-chip and inflection-line spec was kept to popup text and labels, even after discovering that られる/れる, せる/させる and ば weren't merging into the preceding verb at all. That was flagged as a separate gap, confirmed empirically as pre-existing rather than a regression (there was no git history to check), and fixed as its own follow-up. *Rejected:* silently expanding scope inside a content-only task.

**2026-07-03 — Mono-ruby deferred; a naive even split rejected.**
Per-kanji distributed furigana (おう over 王, と over 都) was requested for readability during Phase 4 testing. Done properly it needs a new data source (KANJIDIC, JMdict's sister project, for per-kanji on/kun readings) and an alignment algorithm, and can't work at all for jukujikun (大人 = おとな), so it's a deliberate Phase 6 item, not a quick fix. *Rejected:* an even-split heuristic — wrong most of the time (王都: おう is 2 mora, と is 1), actively misleading a learner rather than helping.

**2026-07-04 — Duplicate homographs get a sourced archaic tag; demotion is the fallback.**
First decided as demotion alone (mirroring dual-view), then refined: JMdict already carries real rarity data for exactly this — reading-level `re_inf` tags (`ok`, the same family as rK/sK) and sense-level `misc` tags (`arch`, `obs`, `dated`) — so a duplicate gets a labelled badge where its data has one, and is demoted only where it doesn't (not every rare-looking duplicate is formally archaic, which is checked per case against the raw release, not assumed). This needed `m`/`ki` added to the compact file, a low-cost regeneration. *Rejected:* demotion only — it deprioritizes a card without ever saying why, where a sourced tag (closer to what Jisho shows) is strictly more informative for the same UI cost. *Revised 2026-07-05:* the "TBD" comparison rule was settled as an exact `g[0]` string match, after checking the real cases (酒/君/私) against the raw release — each shares an identical first gloss. *Rejected (2026-07-05):* fuzzy/overlap matching built pre-emptively — no real case needs it ("don't build for a hypothetical gap").

**2026-07-06 — Homograph cards show their own kanji spellings.**
When a kana lookup resolves to several entries, identical kana headers made the cards impossible to tell apart at a glance; each card now shows its own kanji spelling. Approved conditional on a data check, which found the compact file had been discarding kanji spellings entirely, so it needed a regeneration (the `k` field), not just a display change. *Rejected:* regenerating twice — the `k` field was built in the same pass as the gloss restructuring below.

**2026-07-06 — Senses numbered, with up to three glosses each.**
Senses render with visible numbers (1., 2., 3.), matching a ManabiDojo-style reference layout, and the gloss-per-sense cap went from 2 to 3 (the sense cap stayed 3 at the time, pending real evidence). A flat gloss array can't be numbered by sense, so `g` became an array of arrays grouped by sense, and its two consumers moved to `g[0]?.[0]`. POS chips stay one line per entry rather than repeating per sense as in the reference image — `p` is a whole-entry union and per-sense POS wasn't in scope; revisit only if asked. *Rejected:* just widening a flat cap.

**2026-07-06 — The clicked surface stays the headword; kanji is an annotation.**
Confirmed as-is in a live round: the clicked surface always displays as the big headword, with the kanji spelling a small secondary annotation only when the click was kana — consistent with a kanji click showing kanji plus furigana. One rule ("show what was actually on screen as primary"), not two behaviours to maintain, matching the product's "confirm what's on screen" principle. *Rejected:* a standard dictionary's kanji-first convention.

**2026-07-06 — Pre-modern verb classes count as archaic, on every card.**
Approved to fix こかす/呉る-style entries whose POS label said "(archaic)" with no badge anywhere else on the card: Yodan/Nidan conjugation classes imply `arch` specifically (not `dated`/`obs` — the verb class only says "pre-modern grammar"), as a fallback after the entry's own explicit tags, never double-tagging. Built the same session, checking any `v4*`/`v2*` code. *Revised the same day:* live re-testing showed no badge on the very entries it was built for, because the tag check ran only for duplicates; it now runs on every entry, while demotion stays duplicate-only. *Rejected:* special-casing those two words — an archaic signal is worth showing on any card, not only one that happens to share a sibling's first gloss.

**2026-07-15 — Sense notes (`s_inf`) shown for every entry, not just particles.**
The originating report proposed scoping notes to particle/interjection entries (reusing `isFunctionWordPrimary()`), assuming content-word notes were mostly register or dialect information already covered elsewhere. Sampling the raw release first — as the report itself asked — confirmed function words carry notes far more *often*, but showed content-word notes are substantive (grammatical constraints, kanji-nuance distinctions), exactly what a learner clicks to check and not covered by the archaic badge or kanji filtering. This deviated from the report's own proposal, so Eric was asked and confirmed universal display; long-note wrapping was checked against the popup's CSS before building. *Rejected:* the proposed prt/int-only scope.

**2026-07-22 — Lookup chip: reuse the popup, 15-second life, one at a time.**
Rather than a new component, a popup that already finished loading turns into a chip instead of being discarded when the next cue arrives, and hovering re-reveals the same body — "+ Anki" included — so reading and committing stay one interaction. ~15 seconds balances staying actionable long enough to notice against lingering across several lines. It's anchored to the `<video>`'s live rect rather than a fixed viewport coordinate, deliberately avoiding the class of bug behind the 2026-07-17 stray subtitle box. *Rejected:* building a separate chip component. **Provenance:** this design arrived as an unusually complete, pre-formatted answer (framed as reusable "for the next Claude Code session") to a narrower question about capture timing, introducing two features not previously discussed; it was flagged and confirmed with Eric as genuinely Eric's before being treated as decided. (Its WAV-export half is under Anki export: audio capture.)

### English subtitles

**2026-07-23 — English comes from Crunchyroll's own captions, not from Jimaku.**
Crunchyroll's captions are official-quality and synced to the video by construction. The trade-off was needing fresh fact-finding on how the page exposes them, which only Eric's browser could check (like the DRM tests), so build work waited on it. *Rejected:* extracting English from dual-language Jimaku files — inconsistent coverage, since not every release bundles English. *Rejected:* a separate English Jimaku fetch — a whole second fetch/rank/offset-sync pipeline with no guaranteed timing match to the Japanese file.

**2026-07-23 — The caption URL is read passively from the `play` response.**
Put to Eric as a real choice, not decided silently: every other Crunchyroll-facing technique here (JSON-LD metadata, the Web Audio tap) reads what the page already hands a paying, logged-in session, while calling Crunchyroll's undocumented internal API ourselves would be a different category of thing — closer to acting as an unofficial API client, the kind of line screenshot capture landed on the wrong side of. Eric chose passive observation: a MAIN-world script overrides `fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest` to read response bodies the player was fetching anyway, forwarding the URL by `window.postMessage`; `background.js` does the actual fetch (content scripts can't fetch cross-origin) behind a hostname allowlist, since any page script — an ad, an XSS payload — could spoof that message. English cues use the raw video time, because Crunchyroll's own captions don't need the manual offset and would be misaligned by it. *Rejected:* calling the `play` endpoint ourselves. *Rejected:* `chrome.webRequest` — it can't read response bodies in Manifest V3. *Rejected:* recognizing the response by its request path — endpoint naming isn't something to hardcode a guess against; shape detection survives a rename.

**2026-07-26 — English paired to the Japanese cue window by midpoint containment.**
Fixes the JP/EN stagger at offset 0: one decision ("is Japanese showing, and over which window?") drives both boxes, the only way to get the exact-same-instant behaviour Eric asked for, because the discrepancy lives in the two independently authored files and can't be fixed by improving our own timing. The first version used a proportional overlap test (`overlap / duration >= 0.25` on either side), which live testing immediately showed stacking two unrelated English lines; it was replaced the same day with midpoint containment — a cue can only be centred in one place, so a mostly-elsewhere neighbour fails structurally rather than by tuning. *Rejected:* snapping boundaries within a tolerance — a partial fix that still staggers beyond the tolerance and adds a per-source magic number. *Rejected:* leaving the stagger as inherent. *Rejected:* raising the overlap constant — any threshold is a guess about how two independently authored files overlap, tuned on one show and breaking silently on the next. *Eric's call:* when one Japanese line's translation genuinely is two English sentences, show both. *Accepted cost:* English displays only while Japanese does, so a moment Crunchyroll translates but the Jimaku file has no line for (or an episode whose Japanese failed to load) shows no English — the two properties are mutually exclusive, and simultaneity was the one asked for (confirmed not distracting, 2026-07-31).

**2026-07-26 — English cues reset by episode navigation, not the Japanese loader.**
`loadSubtitles()` cleared the English cues (`englishCues`) on the reasoning that a new Japanese fetch meant they were stale — but the two pipelines run on different events and schedules (our Jimaku fetch vs Crunchyroll's own `play` request), and `loadSubtitles` also runs on first load and every retry, so a successful English fetch was discarded moments later, permanently. The reset now hangs off `jp-immersion-locationchange`. *Rejected (2026-07-26):* having the sniffer replay its cached URL after every reset — on an episode change that URL is still the *previous* episode's until the new `play` request lands, so it would load the wrong translations: plausible-looking wrong output, worse than none. *Revised 2026-07-27:* replay re-enabled, because not replaying turned out to destroy English cues already loaded for the new episode after a show switch. The risk was removed rather than accepted: each URL is stamped with the pathname it was captured on, so a current-episode replay is accepted and a previous-episode one discarded. *Rejected (2026-07-27):* polling the pathname faster — it makes a real ordering problem rarer instead of fixing it.

**2026-07-26 — The CDN host allowlist confirmed from a real URL, not widened.**
The `*.crunchyrollcdn.com` allowlist (in `isTrustedCrunchyrollCdnUrl` and `manifest.json`'s `host_permissions`) had been assumed from the CDN's name, never checked against a real signed URL, and became the leading suspect when the English pipeline never worked — Crunchyroll has historically served these from `v.vrv.co`, `static.crunchyroll.com` and various Akamai domains. It was left alone until the sniffer's new log line showed the real host — `vod-fy-mod.crunchyrollcdn.com`, already covered — the same day. Recorded for the process: the suspicion was reasonable on the evidence, and reading beat guessing. *Rejected:* broadening the allowlist and host permissions to cover historical hosts — a security regression and a store-review liability that would have permanently widened the extension's reach to fix a problem it didn't have.

**2026-07-26 — Unconditional diagnostics at every stage of the caption pipeline.**
Reverses the earlier implicit choice to degrade quietly — right for an *optional* field (like a missing frequency tag), wrong for a whole feature whose only failure mode is "nothing appears". Silent degradation let a completely non-functional pipeline pass a full live round looking correct, because the English on screen came from a dual-language Jimaku file. *Rejected:* a debug flag — it's a handful of lines per episode, and not having it cost a full testing cycle. *Rejected:* on-screen errors — they'd break "keep them watching" for something the user can't act on mid-episode.

**2026-07-27 — English waits for the Japanese line to be painted.**
Extends the pairing decision one step: that made one decision drive *which* English shows; this makes one event drive *when* — so any future slowness in the Japanese render can't reintroduce a stagger. *Rejected:* keeping the service worker warm with a periodic ping — it fights the platform's lifecycle for a symptom rather than removing the asymmetry, and would still lose the race after a long enough idle.

**2026-07-29 — Gap bridging gated on English-text identity, not a timer.**
Built to Eric's written spec (three zones by gap length), which left the Zone 2/3 cutoff open for tuning. Measurement showed no gap threshold can separate "same sentence" from "next sentence" — but none needs to: bridging already requires both flanking cues to pair with the same English cue, which is what makes them one sentence, so the threshold's only job is to stop English floating alone under an empty Japanese box through a long pause. Set to 1.0s as a working default, tunable by eye rather than derivable. Making English visibility depend on Japanese timing wasn't a new dependency — pairing had already made English entirely Japanese-window-driven, and the spec's Zone 1 was already built. *Rejected:* a fixed or duration-scaled threshold as the sentence test.

**2026-08-15 — Screen position tells a background English line from the translation.**
A top-positioned cue is dropped only when a bottom-positioned one competes for the same moment — exactly the situation a subtitler resolves by moving the secondary line to the top. Nothing in the text distinguishes them. *Rejected:* keeping only the single best-overlapping cue — one Japanese line legitimately translates as two consecutive English sentences, and the second would be thrown away. *Rejected:* never trusting `\an8` — a line placed at the top because the speaker is at the bottom of frame is ordinary dialogue, and the only line there. The mechanism is right; whether Crunchyroll's own files carry the override on such lines is not yet verified.

**2026-09-22 — Sign cues never pair as translations, on any track.**
Decided, not yet built. A sign, typesetting or on-screen-text cue must never become the English line or the Anki `Translation`, and must never drive the sentence merge or widen the audio clip — on every English track and every audio version; a track with no dialogue cues behaves as having no English track. Found with the caption probe. *Rejected:* treating it as low priority — rare, but during a capture it writes a wrong `Translation` into a permanent card, the failure ranked highest. *Rejected:* special-casing signs-only dub tracks — the same title card sits among real dialogue in the Japanese-audio track. *Rejected:* per-show style-name rules (e.g. `Konosuba_*`) — the fix has to generalize.

**2026-09-22 — Dubs keep Japanese subtitles; the Translation field stays empty.**
Reading Japanese over English audio is legitimate practice, so Japanese subtitles keep loading on a dub. `Translation` is empty there because the dub's English subtitle track holds only signs — which follows from the sign-cue decision and needs no code of its own. *Rejected:* using the dub's `captions` track (the 585-cue WebVTT) as the translation — it transcribes the English dub script rather than translating the Japanese line, so it would mislead a learner checking what the Japanese meant. *Rejected:* dropping dub support — the sentence and definition on a dub card are still correct. *Rejected:* fetching the Japanese-audio version's English subtitles while on a dub — same timeline and a real translation, but it means calling Crunchyroll's API directly, against the passive-observation decision (2026-07-23).

**2026-09-23 — Uncertain English cues are excluded, not kept.**
When the sign check can't settle whether an English cue is dialogue, the cue is treated as a sign: it never becomes the English line or the `Translation`. A missing translation is visible and can be fixed at capture time; a wrong one enters a permanent card unnoticed — the same call as `CLAUDE.md`'s "when unsure, fail visibly" rule and the Strict frequency scheme (Dictionary data & lookup, 2026-07-19).
