This is about the classifier's signals disagreeing, not a labeller's uncertainty. *Rejected:* keeping uncertain cues for better translation coverage.

**2026-09-23 — The sign signal is chosen from a multi-show recapture.**
The 2026-09-23 caption fixture classifies only 14 of 399 lines, from one episode, so no signal's false-positive rate on real dialogue can be measured from it. *Rejected:* choosing the signal from the current fixture and building now.

**2026-09-23 — Preview text blocks count as signs.**
A next-episode preview's block of on-screen text (KonoSuba 3 ep 6's 10-second `wall o text`) is a sign even where a narrator speaks it: it is on-screen text, and a block that long pairs against several unrelated narration cues. *Rejected:* treating it as the translation when the narration is spoken.

### Anki export: cards & capture

**2026-07-02 — AnkiConnect, not offline .apkg/CSV export.**
Superseded a provisional pick of offline export earlier the same session. AnkiConnect fits the no-backend architecture (a `fetch()` to a local server, the same shape as the Jimaku calls) and is a reasonable ask of a user who already has SRS habits — it's Anki's most-installed add-on. Known limitation to design around: Anki desktop must be running at capture time. *Rejected:* offline export — it breaks the "capture and keep watching" loop (at best: queue locally, stop watching later, import by hand — the failure mode that excluded the built-in wordbook). *Rejected:* building both — it would double the design and testing surface.

**2026-07-02 — Card core: word, reading, gloss and the whole marked sentence.**
*Rejected:* word-only cards — they lose the sentence context that gives real value over a standalone SRS deck. *Deferred, not rejected:* full sentence mining with audio and screenshot, pending the DRM feasibility test (see Anki export: audio capture).

**2026-07-02 — Sentence translation on the card deferred, then built 2026-07-23.**
Deferred pending Phase 6's English-subtitle pipeline, and cheap to defer: with the word's meaning already on the card, a full translation is mainly a comprehension backstop on later review. Built on 2026-07-23 when English display was pulled into Phase 5. *Rejected:* building the field before the English-subtitle pipeline existed, or dropping it outright — it was deferred, not rejected.

**2026-07-02 — Capture is an instant "Add to Anki" button in the popup.**
Sends on click, with undo/edit available afterwards — the proven Yomitan pattern that ManabiDojo's own users already pair with their tool. *Rejected:* a separate line-level hotkey or gesture — speculative demand with no evidence, carried as an open question instead. *Rejected:* a pre-send edit step — it reintroduces the "stop and fill in a form" friction the instant-send/undo pattern exists to avoid.

**2026-07-17 — Sentence captured at click time; the word located by exact offset.**
Two correctness risks, considered together. The sentence is captured once, synchronously, inside `onWordClick`, because the module-level `lastText` tracks whatever line is showing *now* — read after the async lookup and however long the user spends in the popup, it could attach the wrong sentence. And the word is bolded at the exact character offset `renderGroups` records as it builds the line (cheap, since it already walks every group). *Rejected:* re-reading `lastText` when the button is clicked. *Rejected:* locating the word with `sentenceText.indexOf(surface)` — it bolds the wrong occurrence when a word appears twice. "Edit last card" was deliberately left out of this pass as a separable follow-up (built 2026-07-29, redesigned 2026-07-30).

**2026-07-17 — A dedicated deck and note type; sentence-first cards.**
Both Eric's explicit calls, made after reading the real Anki state rather than assuming it. Immersion cards go to a new "Japanese Immersion" deck, kept apart from Eric's curated "Kaishi 1.5k" deck — a deliberate preference, not a passing comment — and a dedicated note type is provisioned automatically and idempotently rather than asking the user to set it up by hand. Card direction — sentence first, with the word bolded on the front and word/reading/gloss revealed on the back — was offered as an explicit choice and matches the core-loop framing: confirm what you half-know from the actual scene, not cold vocabulary recall. Verified end to end with a real test card from the service worker console. *Rejected:* Anki's stock "Basic" note type — only 2 fields, not enough to keep word/reading/gloss/sentence independently stylable and queryable. *Rejected:* word-first cards, closer to a traditional vocab flashcard.

**2026-07-17 — One shared `invokeAnkiConnect()` helper.**
Every Anki feature (card creation, deck and model lookups, connectivity checks) uses the same request/response shape (`{action, version, params}` in, `{result, error}` out), so one helper keeps the "network failure vs AnkiConnect error" distinction written once. It was tested directly as a real global in the service worker's console rather than through throwaway UI. The `webCorsOriginList` requirement was expected from general knowledge of this integration and flagged before the first test instead of letting Eric hit an opaque failure — which paid off. *Rejected:* a separate fetch call per feature.

**2026-07-23 — Translation on the back of the card only.**
Showing the English on the front beside the Japanese sentence would give away the answer before the recall attempt, defeating the card. *Rejected:* the front of the card, beside the sentence. *Revised 2026-07-26:* a live report of the translation on the front was investigated before anything changed: the template had only ever had it on the back, so no code was invented to "fix" a correct path. What could be verified was changed: Translation moved from among the metadata fields to directly under `{{FrontSide}}`, next to the sentence it translates, Japanese above English like the subtitle stack. (The report turned out to be a dual-language file putting English into the `Sentence` field.) *Rejected (2026-07-26):* changing template code to "fix" a path that was already correct.

**2026-07-23 — Show/episode source added as a fourth opt-in field.**
Eric's own written decision. Knowing where a word was met has real recall value (jogging memory of the scene, or rewatching that exact episode) — a different hook from the word in isolation. It's opt-in like POS/frequency/JLPT, because it doesn't aid the review itself. Scoped into Phase 5 because the data was already free (`detectShowEpisode()` resolves it for Jimaku), closer in cost to how POS shipped than to frequency or JLPT. One combined string field, not separate show and episode fields, unless sorting or filtering by show in Anki is ever needed. Anki-only, with deliberately no popup badge — "which episode is this" is redundant while watching it. *Rejected:* leaving it off the card. *Rejected:* making it always on — clutter for anyone who doesn't track sources.

**2026-07-26 — Split sentences rejoined for the Anki card only.**
Measured first, at Eric's request: 12.4% of Japanese cues are half a sentence that Crunchyroll translates as one. At capture time the whole sentence goes into the card's `Sentence`, bold offset and audio clip; the display is untouched. *Rejected:* merging the display too — it would make the Japanese line depend on the English track being available, so the same show would read differently depending on whether the caption pipeline fired, an unacceptable coupling for the primary feature (having just found that pipeline had never worked). *Rejected:* leaving it — 12.4% is frequent, and half a sentence is a bad card, while half a sentence on screen is fine (the next line is a second away). *Rejected:* a Japanese-only merge rule — sentence-final punctuation is an uploader habit, so it behaves completely differently per provider; English-driven is the only viable basis.

**2026-07-31 — Bolding the target word is markup, never a text edit.**
The edit panel's sentence rebuild took an offset on trust and spliced the new word in at it — correct only while the offset really spells that word, which changing the target word broke, overwriting part of the sentence. The offset is now a hint, verified before use, with a search and then no bolding as fallbacks. *Rejected:* passing the correct offset and keeping the splice — the same class of bug returns the moment any caller's offset goes stale for any other reason.

**2026-07-31 — The Anki card is sent before its audio.**
A capture waited for the line to finish before writing anything (the clip is sliced from the completed cue), which for a split sentence meant waiting out the second half — a multi-second delay before the capture chip. Now the note is written at once with every text field and the audio attached by a second `updateNoteFields` when ready, which lets the wait grow to 20s and shows the chip immediately. Cost: a card exists briefly without audio, and a capture undone in that window must not be written to afterwards — tracked by note id, since a second capture also moves the "last card" pointer. *Rejected:* shortening the wait — a wait that expires produces first-half-only audio, the other half of the same report.

### Anki export: audio capture

**2026-07-02 — Screenshot and audio fields gated on a hands-on DRM test.**
Left undecided until tested on Crunchyroll's real player, following the forced-subtitle precedent: verify a platform limitation hands-on before closing it. The session's research (Widevine vs `canvas.drawImage()`/`captureStream()`) was general, not Crunchyroll-specific. The rule set in advance: if blocked, drop the field from the roadmap entirely. *Rejected:* assuming both fields were blocked from that research. *Revised 2026-07-17:* the test ran live — screenshot capture is blocked by two independent methods on two shows, so the screenshot field was dropped entirely (Scope); audio is viable, confirmed by a real signal-level measurement. Where the audio field went in the build order, and its capture-timing and UI design, was left as Eric's call (built 2026-07-22). *Rejected (2026-07-17):* closing out audio on "no error means it works" — unlike `captureStream()`'s explicit error, a connection that doesn't throw proves nothing, since some DRM implementations mute captured audio silently.

**2026-07-17 — OS-level screen capture rejected without a test.**
`chrome.desktopCapture` grabs the rendered screen rather than going through the protected media pipeline, so it might sidestep the DRM block on some configurations. *Rejected, untested:* it needs a native "choose what to share" picker for every capture — a Chrome API constraint, not a design choice — which conflicts with quick capture and keep watching whatever the DRM answer, so a test cycle would confirm a capability we wouldn't ship. Eric agreed to drop it on this reasoning.

**2026-07-22 — Continuous rolling buffer, not rewind-and-recapture.**
Eric's own call, from a written analysis. A continuous tap on the same `createMediaElementSource` graph already proven DRM-viable fills a circular raw-PCM buffer in the background, connected straight through to the speakers the whole time — the graph is only ever added to, per the 2026-07-17 finding. *Rejected:* pause → seek to the cue start → replay while recording → seek back → resume — a visible rewind blip on every capture, undercutting "capture and keep watching". *Rejected:* dropping the audio field from Phase 5 — premature, with feasibility already confirmed.

**2026-07-22 — Clips cut at the cue's own timestamps, not at click time.**
Users routinely click after reading the popup, sometimes after the line has passed, so click-to-end would clip the start of the dialogue or capture nothing. The cue timing is already known (it drives the overlay), so the buffer is sliced by that window whenever the card is committed. The clock was `audioCtx.currentTime`, chosen because it keeps running through pause and seek, so buffer positions and cue timestamps share one clock. *Rejected:* `video.currentTime` or wall-clock `performance.now()` as the clock — two clocks that can drift apart. *Revised 2026-08-15:* the clock became the buffer's own sample count (below).

**2026-07-22 — Clip padding: 0.2s lead-in, 0.6s tail.**
First a small fixed 200ms on each side: cheap insurance against per-upload timing slop in community subtitle files, independent of the planned auto-offset-detection (which corrects one global offset per file, not per-cue slop). *Revised 2026-08-15:* padding became asymmetric, because subtitle end timestamps are routinely authored a little before the speech finishes ("the very end of the sentence is usually omitted"), and the slice now waits (bounded, 2s) for the tail to exist. *Rejected (2026-08-15):* compensating with `outputLatency` — it shifts both edges together, and its sign can't be verified from here.

**2026-07-22 — `ScriptProcessorNode`, not `AudioWorkletNode`.**
Eric's analysis left it open. For a non-real-time rolling recording, `ScriptProcessorNode` (deprecated but fully functional in Chrome) does the job directly in the page. *Rejected:* `AudioWorkletNode` — a separate worklet module, a new `web_accessible_resources` entry and chunked cross-thread messaging just to get samples back into `content.js`. Revisit only if Chrome actually removes it.

**2026-07-22 — Clips exported as WAV, not webm/opus.**
A WAV header plus raw samples fits the ring buffer's raw-PCM storage with no extra encoding step. *Rejected:* webm/opus — a second encoding pipeline for a file-size saving that doesn't matter much in local Anki storage. (Decided together with the lookup chip — Popup display.)

**2026-07-26 — Clips normalized by RMS; silent clips dropped.**
Exported loudness tracked the player's volume slider, and the tap point can't be moved, so this was correction on the clip or nothing. RMS-targeted normalization with a peak ceiling and a max-gain limit. **Silence was decided explicitly** (Eric flagged it as an open call): below a peak floor, no audio is exported, reusing the existing no-audio path; it's logged to the console, not shown in the UI — a muted player is the user's deliberate act, not an error worth interrupting a capture for, and the button has no partial-success state. *Rejected:* peak normalization — one loud transient over the line (a door slam, a sound effect) would leave the dialogue itself quiet, the failure that matters on a listening card. *Rejected:* normalizing a muted capture — it scales the noise floor up into a loud hiss, worse than no audio.

**2026-07-27 — Merged-audio cues matched by timing, not display text.**
Display text isn't a usable key: the timeline records the join of every visible cue, the merge names one cue's text, and they agree only when nothing else is on screen — which on stacking providers is rare. Timing is what the merge is computed from and what the clip needs, and it's unaffected by how a provider stacks its events. *Rejected:* normalizing the text on both sides so the keys match — a fragile key made slightly less fragile, still depending on two modules' filters staying identical.

**2026-07-31 — A superseded audio graph is silenced, not disconnected.**
A `<video>` swap built a second capture graph while the first kept writing into the same buffer. Each graph now carries a generation number and writes only while current; nothing is disconnected and routing never changes. *Rejected:* disconnecting the old graph — specifically forbidden, since it silences the page's audio until a reload (confirmed by accident, 2026-07-17).

**2026-07-31 — Merged clip edges come from subtitle timings, not display windows.**
On a stacking provider a display window routinely begins before the merged sentence and ends after it, so bounding the clip by the window over-captures by construction. The window still locates the sentence in the buffer (that mapping exists nowhere else), but the edges are interpolated back to the sentence's own timings, clamped to the window. *Rejected:* matching cues by text — replaced on 2026-07-27, and impossible when the displayed text joins several cues.

**2026-08-15 — The capture clock counts samples, not seconds.**
`audioCtx.currentTime` had been chosen because it keeps running through a pause or seek — and that property is what broke: a paused `<video>` still feeds its graph, so a cue left open across a pause recorded the pause as part of the line (the 44-second mostly-silent clip). The clock is now the buffer's write count and nothing is written while paused or seeking, so a pause takes zero space and clock and buffer can't disagree — while still never reconciling against `video.currentTime`, which the original reasoning wanted. This also reverses the 2026-07-22 acceptance of pause dead-air as a known trade-off, which the live pass showed was wrong. *Rejected:* capping the damage harder — the 20-second ceiling already existed and had already fired on this case; it bounds the export and says nothing about whether the recorded timings mean anything.

**2026-08-15 — An interrupted capture keeps the audio it heard.**
Since 2026-07-31 a capture spanning a seek or episode change resolved to no audio, on the grounds that the buffer would by then hold the wrong audio — right about after the jump, wrong about before it: at that instant the buffer still holds the clicked line, usually most of it. Pending captures are now flushed at the jump, before the epoch bump, so the card gets a short but correct clip; the wrong-episode risk is closed by capping a flushed clip at the line's own window + 0.25s. This is what makes "switch episodes before the chip appears" produce audio at all. *Rejected:* waiting to see whether the jump was navigation or a small seek — both invalidate the same timings, and the flush is right for both.

**2026-09-22 — No audio clip when the playing audio isn't Japanese.**
Decided, not yet built. On non-Japanese audio the card is created without a clip, and the capture UI shows a small note saying why — not a modal, so capture-and-keep-watching still holds. *Rejected:* recording anyway — it puts English speech under a Japanese sentence on a permanent card. *Rejected:* blocking card creation on dubs — it throws away a correct sentence and definition. The likely signal is the `play` response's `audioLocale`, seen on only two responses so far; it must be verified live before anything relies on it.

### Anki export: capture chip & edit panel

**2026-07-29 — "Edit last card" is an in-page panel, reached from two places.**
The build item said only "some way to jump into Anki's own card editor". The first design was about *where* the affordance lives, and built both places: a button in the popup's success row, and a persistent control. *Rejected:* the success-row button alone — the popup becomes a chip within a second or two, and a typo is noticed later than that. *Rejected:* the persistent control alone — the instant after capturing is when the user most wants it. The action behind them handed off to Anki's own editor, rejecting in-page field editing as "a second, much worse Anki" under the guardrail against a competing review system. *Revised 2026-07-30:* that rejection conflated building a rival *review system* (out of scope) with editing one card just created (not), and weighing only *where* the button lives meant never asking what pressing it should *do* — switching to the Anki desktop app is itself the kind of interruption the litmus test catches. AnkiConnect's `notesInfo`/`updateNoteFields` need no visible Anki GUI, so an in-page panel does the job without leaving the browser; the two trigger surfaces stayed, only the action changed. *Rejected (2026-07-30):* `guiEditNote`/`guiBrowse` as the primary action — kept only as a secondary "Open in Anki" escape hatch for what the panel doesn't cover (tags, note type, deletion).

**2026-07-30 — Word/Reading/Gloss edited by dictionary re-lookup, not free text.**
The three are derived together from one dictionary-entry choice at capture, so free-text editing would let them drift out of sync with each other and with POS/Frequency/JLPT. The panel reopens the same tokenize → lookup UI used at capture, and a new pick cascades all the derived fields through the existing pipeline, not a second one. *Rejected:* independent free-text boxes.

**2026-07-30 — POS/Frequency/JLPT/Source are read-only in the panel.**
They're deterministic outputs of the dictionary entry and episode metadata, so direct overrides could contradict the Word/Gloss they describe; they update live when a new entry is picked. Because POS/Frequency/JLPT are opt-in at capture, a re-pick rewrites each only if it was already filled, rather than smuggling metadata onto a card created without it. *Rejected:* making them directly editable.

**2026-07-30 — The trim editor works from a retained in-memory PCM buffer.**
Real edits go both ways — tightening (bleed from an adjacent line, dead air) and extending (a late start needing lead-in, or including the next line for context). The buffer dying on a full page reload was checked and found harmless: both entry points to the editor already live no longer than it (the persistent control's `nid` clears on reload too). Stored un-normalized, so repeated trims can't compound the gain. *Rejected:* re-decoding the exported clip (`retrieveMediaFile`) — it can only ever tighten, never recover audio outside the captured bounds.

**2026-07-30 — Retained buffer padded beyond the clip, so clips can be extended.**
A buffer trimmed to the exact capture bounds could only tighten, defeating the previous decision, so a few seconds are kept on each side; the exact padding was left as a tuning parameter (Open questions). *Rejected:* delaying the clip's own export until its post-roll exists — retention happens twice instead.

**2026-07-30 — Captured content is verified on a two-state chip, not the popup.**
*Rejected:* the popup as the verification surface, for three independent reasons: its layout is sized for dictionary lookup, not a sentence summary; its "Adding…" → "Added to Anki" state was gated on audio capture, not subtitle timing, so it was often still pending when the line changed; and some lines (single-word ones especially) don't stay on screen long enough for any popup-based window. The chip's lifetime is independent of the line's, which fixes the third, and it appears once the card is written. *(The second reason was removed at the source on 2026-07-31 by the two-phase send; the decision stands on the other two, and the chip now appears when the card is written rather than when its audio lands.)*

**2026-07-30 — The chip leaves out POS/Frequency/JLPT/Source.**
The fields worth a short glance are the ones the project's known bug classes hit (segmentation, sense selection, desync, merge glitches); POS/Frequency/JLPT/Source follow deterministically from a correct Word/Gloss and are rarely wrong on their own, so they'd spend scarce attention and space on low-value checks. It also decouples the chip from the real card layout, expected to change in Phase 6. *Rejected:* showing every field.

**2026-07-30 — The chip's expanded time scales with content length.**
One fixed duration would be too short for long sentences and too long for single words. The exact curve was left to live tuning (Open questions). *Rejected:* a single fixed duration.

**2026-07-30 — Chip hover morph animation deferred to Phase 6.**
The chip shows and hides instantly, consistent with the rest of the UI's polish level; a smoother morph between states waits for the broader Phase 6 redesign. *Rejected:* building it as a one-off ahead of that pass.

**2026-08-15 — The edit buffer is sealed on navigation, not dropped.**
Navigation cleared the retained PCM along with the ring buffer, so the editor declared audio unavailable for a clip sitting complete in memory. The retained copy is independent; what navigation invalidates is only the ability to grow it — so it is topped up once more and its bounds dropped. *Rejected:* re-decoding the exported WAV back out of Anki as a fallback — rejected on 2026-07-30 for the reason that still applies (it can only tighten), and a second, worse code path for a case that needed none.

**2026-08-15 — The audio buffer belongs to its capture, not the newest note.**
The note claimed the buffer when it was created, but the buffer is cut only when the capture resolves — seconds later for a split sentence — so the trim editor showed the previous card's clip. Each capture now carries a click-time token, and the note claims the buffer only once that token's clip has landed. *Rejected:* clearing the buffer at click time — it swaps the wrong clip for no clip, when the honest answer is "still capturing", which the panel now says while it waits.

**2026-08-15 — The trim editor gets a playhead and a transport, not more handles.**
The request was for it to work "like a video editing program". The task is always "find the exact moment this line starts" against a clip whose rough bounds are already right — a scrubbing problem, not a selecting one — so it follows an NLE's scrub-and-set-in/out model, keeping handle dragging for coarse adjustment. Previews mute the episode rather than pausing it, since pausing to check a card is exactly the interruption that ruled out rewind-and-recapture (2026-07-22). *Rejected:* a DAW's drag-to-select-and-audition model.

### Storage & settings

**2026-06-23 — The Jimaku API key is stored via a toolbar popup.**
Kept in `chrome.storage.local` and entered in the extension's toolbar popup. *Rejected:* storing it in any config file.

**2026-07-02 — One toggle set shared by the popup and the card.**
The metadata toggles (then POS, common-word, JLPT and a frequency placeholder) are one set governing both surfaces. The set's *composition* changed on 2026-07-04, when a real frequency tier replaced the common-word flag and placeholder, and a fourth (source) toggle was added on 2026-07-23; the one-shared-set design stands. *Rejected:* separate settings per surface — the popup and the card could silently disagree about what's shown for the same word.

**2026-07-17 — Metadata toggles built one at a time, as their data became real.**
The single build item "opt-in metadata toggles" turned out, on checking readiness before writing code, to be three very different sizes of work: POS data already existed (it only needed a gate), frequency needed a compact-file regeneration and real threshold work, and JLPT needed a new external data source chosen first. POS was built end to end (toggle, popup gating, Anki field); the other two became their own scoped items, shown meanwhile as disabled "Coming soon" boxes so the toggle set wouldn't visibly grow later. *Rejected:* building all three as inert placeholders and wiring data later — a toggle for data that doesn't exist either does nothing (confusing) or needs its own not-yet-real fallback logic.

**2026-07-22 — Settings panel kept separate from the audio work.**
Raised in the same design discussion as the audio chip, but independent scope: a UI consolidation of three existing, unrelated controls (offset, upload, switcher), not part of what makes the audio field work. Logged as its own follow-up with no build slot at first. *Revised 2026-07-23:* scheduled into Phase 6 (Process & roadmap, 2026-07-23); its priority within Phase 6 isn't decided. *Rejected:* building it alongside the audio field and chip.

### Test & audit tooling

**2026-08-01 — Entry resolution tested by running the real function, not a copy.**
`test-entry-resolution.js` loads the whole of `background.js` into a `vm` sandbox with `fetch` mocked and calls `resolveTextFiles` directly, asserting the entry, the exact console lines and the UI state on every case — because the 2026-07-31 pass produced both failure shapes (right subtitles with no log at all, and a missing log hiding a wrong entry). Logging is asserted unconditionally; its absence is the gap that had made Frieren S2 unverifiable. *Rejected:* relying on `test-season-resolution.js`'s hand-copied mirror of the selection block — a mirror can agree with itself while disagreeing with what ships, and can't see anything the real function does around the selection. The older file is kept for helper-level coverage, its mirror synced to the new tiers.

**2026-08-11 — Audit scoring fixed, not the resolver, where Jimaku has nothing.**
Several "proven EMPTY" findings were refusals, not faults — episodes or OVAs Jimaku simply doesn't hold in the entry resolved — and leaving them would put permanent false failures into every future pass, the same category error as Detective Conan's 580 refusals (2026-08-04). Three over-reporting shapes were fixed together, adding the flagged-not-proven categories `MISSING` and `SHARED-ENTRY` and suppressing MIXED across two numbering conventions for the same episode. *Rejected:* making the resolver find them — chasing a separate OVA entry per dubbed season is real scope, and the litmus test doesn't favour it.

### Process & roadmap

**2026-06-23 — CLAUDE.md and project-plan.md given separate jobs.**
Originally defined as a sync process: read the Decisions Log and Open Questions at session start, update both files, and scan for decision and open-question changes at session end. *Revised 2026-09-22:* both files had outgrown that. `CLAUDE.md` now holds only stable working rules (guardrail, working rules, engineering rules, repo map, commands, session protocol) and never project state; everything that changes as the project moves — status, product, scope, architecture, roadmap, decisions, open questions — lives here. Session start now reads Status plus the Architecture and Decisions of the components being touched, because reading the whole Decisions Log and Open Questions every session no longer scaled. The one intentional overlap is the condensed core goal and the litmus test (see How to use this doc). *Rejected (2026-09-22):* keeping the sync process — both files carrying overlapping status, and the whole Decisions Log and Open Questions read at every session start, which no longer scaled.

**2026-07-04 — Manual upload given its own Phase 4 slot, before Phase 5.**
Caught as a real scheduling gap: the manual subtitle upload fallback (.srt/.ass) was committed must-have scope that had never been assigned to a phase. Eric's call was that it should ship on its own, before Anki export work begins. No new parsing work was needed — `subtitle-parser.js` already handled `.srt`/`.ass` from Phase 2; this was the user-facing upload flow only. *Rejected:* bundling it into general Phase 4 work, or pushing it to Phase 6 — either would have had it compete with the bug-fix backlog or the later UX-polish phase.

**2026-07-05 — New Phase 4.5 for Jimaku integration.**
Created between Phase 4 and Phase 5, absorbing per-season offset/uploader memory and the title-resolution work out of Phase 6. Investigation showed these are one feature — reliable show/episode identification feeding a ranked subtitle-selection and memory system — not separable concerns that happen to touch Jimaku. Anki export (Phase 5) moved later to make room, an intentional trade-off: Jimaku search and selection is core-loop must-have scope, not differentiation polish, so it takes priority. *Rejected:* keeping the work split across Phase 4 (auto-detection) and Phase 6 (memory and title matching, as scheduled 2026-07-04).

**2026-07-06 — Live-testing show chosen for the tester's familiarity.**
The third manual live-testing show swapped from One Piece to Naruto: both are shounen with similar dialogue register and pacing, but Eric's personal familiarity with Naruto makes subtle wrong-definition or mistranslation bugs easier to catch, since the tester already knows what a line "should" mean. Scoped narrowly to the manual rotation (at the time, `content.js`'s hardcoded `SHOW_QUERY`/`EPISODE`); the 3-show automated corpus in `scripts/batch-test.js` (Bocchi/Frieren/One Piece, chosen for uploader and register diversity, not familiarity) is a different mechanism with a different purpose and stays unchanged. *Revised 2026-07-06:* swapped again, from the original Naruto series to Naruto: Shippuuden — same rationale, Eric's call. *Revised 2026-07-12:* swapped to Witch Hat Atelier, Eric's call; verified first that Jimaku has a real entry (id 11793, "Tongari Boushi no Atelier") with usable episode 1 files and no archive-first-file trap. *Rejected:* changing the automated batch-test corpus to match.

**2026-07-15 — Phase 4's live re-test closed by Eric's call, not by exhaustion.**
Three shows and several testing rounds surfaced and fixed a substantial number of real bugs; Eric judged that enough to move on, acknowledging more issues may surface later. Consistent with how the project treats untested edge cases elsewhere (e.g. forced-subtitle overlay behaviour, deprioritized until a real case appears): a live pass needs to reach the point of diminishing returns, not "provably no more bugs". Future bugs are logged and fixed as they come up. *Rejected:* continuing an open-ended testing loop.

**2026-07-17 — Phase 4.5 closed by Eric's call; project moves to Phase 5.**
An unusually long single-day live-testing session (Frieren + Witch Hat Atelier) surfaced and fixed eight real bugs beyond the original build-order scope — two Jimaku search/selection bugs, a missing forced re-render on two paths, an SPA-navigation detection race, a show-to-show `<video>`-rebinding gap, a hardcoded file hint silently overriding the uploader preference, activation on non-watch pages, and a stray empty subtitle box — every one confirmed fixed by live re-testing before closure. Same pattern as Phase 4: closed at diminishing returns, not on a claim that no bugs remain. *Rejected:* keeping the phase open until no more bugs could be found.

**2026-07-23 — English display pulled into Phase 5; settings panel pushed to Phase 6.**
Eric's call. English subtitle display, plus the deferred Anki sentence-translation field it unblocks, moved forward from Phase 6 into Phase 5, ahead of "edit last card". The settings panel (a consolidation of the offset, upload and switcher controls, added as a Phase 5 candidate on 2026-07-22) went the other way, into Phase 6: it is UI polish on working controls, not core-loop completion, and fits beside Phase 6's other polish (dictionary-popup redesign, appearance controls). "Edit last card" was kept as the last Phase 5 item. *Rejected:* leaving English display in Phase 6 and the settings panel in Phase 5.

---

## Open questions

Dates written `~YYYY-MM-DD` are inferred: the question was logged without an opening date, so the earliest date this document ties to it is used.

### Needs testing or real-world feedback

**Forced-subtitle overlay behaviour** (opened ~2026-06-23 · Subtitle overlay, sync & offset) — How the overlay coexists with Crunchyroll's own forced-subtitle layer is completely untested hands-on. No research or testing has turned up a single Crunchyroll title with an untoggleable forced layer (One Piece ep 894 is clean), so it's treated as speculative — don't invest more design thought, or go hunting for one, until a real case appears. Related, not a bug: Bocchi ep 1 QA found hardcoded in-scene translated text (e.g. book pages) with no fixed overlay zone, varying per scene and per fansub.

**lindera-wasm vs Vibrato-wasm** (opened ~2026-06-23 · Tokenizing & grouping) — Which would be the better fallback if kuromoji.js segmentation proves insufficient on colloquial, slang-heavy dialogue. Deferred until real testing shows a quality gap; the 2026-09-18 UniDic comparison found disagreements in both directions, not a gap.

**Dual-view "noise"** (opened ~2026-07-01 · Tokenizing & grouping) — Dual-view sometimes attaches a real but not strictly relevant coincidental match. A confirmed corpus instance (2026-07-03): もの…になる attaches the idiom "to prove successful" where もの is used compositionally — a wrong gloss shown at equal weight, but still non-destructive. Accepted as a trade-off unless live use shows it's genuinely distracting.

**あ's interjection ordering** (opened ~2026-07-05 · Dictionary data & lookup) — Bocchi ep 1 QA flagged あっ's "ah/oh/hey" sense as buried below rarer prefix/noun senses. It was grouped with 私/あたし to be settled by the duplicate-gloss and multi-reading work, which fixed 私/あたし, but あ itself was never re-checked. Needs a live or corpus spot-check before assuming it's resolved.

**Sentence-only capture** (opened 2026-07-02 · Anki export: cards & capture) — Capturing a sentence with no target word is a real use case (reconsidered 2026-07-04), but needs its own capture paradigm — a line-level trigger, and a decision about what fills word/reading/gloss — not a toggle on the existing flow. Kept deferred rather than cut: Eric expects it to come up less often than word capture, and real post-Phase-5 usage of the word-click flow will show whether it's needed better than guessing now.

**Orphaned-entry corpus override is thin** (opened 2026-07-19 · Dictionary data & lookup) — The frequency tiers' corpus override has only 3 episodes of real dialogue to rank against, so most of the 7,737 JMdict-common-but-TUBELEX-unscored entries fall back to newspaper-derived nf priority. Not blocking — nf priority plus the formal-register demotion produce reasonable tiers, confirmed live. If `batch-test.js`'s corpus ever grows, re-running `build-orphaned-tier-overrides.js` improves coverage for free.

**Pricing specifics** (opened 2026-06-30 · Product & scope) — The model is decided (subscription + lifetime); the monthly and lifetime price points aren't. Reviewed 2026-07-04 and kept deferred, since it blocks no coding work; revisit after soft-launch feedback.

**20–30 minute continuous-capture stability** (opened 2026-07-22 · Anki export: audio capture) — The only checklist item no live pass has reached. In-page navigation during capture was tested on 2026-07-31 and 2026-08-15: it no longer produces the wrong episode's audio, and since 2026-08-15 it produces the right episode's audio rather than none. Pause-mid-line dead air, accepted as expected on 2026-07-31, turned out wrong (a 44-second silent clip) and its cause is fixed.

**JLPT attribution not shown anywhere** (opened 2026-07-22 · Dictionary data & lookup) — The CC BY-SA 4.0 JLPT source data requires attribution, and nothing in the extension shows it yet. Not blocking Phase 5; it needs a credits/about surface, realistically a Phase 7 packaging item — flagged so it isn't lost before public launch.

**English captions depend on Crunchyroll's undocumented API** (opened ~2026-07-23 · English subtitles) — The four 2026-07-26 bugs (desync, vertical-order swapping, audio loudness, Translation on the front) are fixed, and the 2026-07-31 pass exercised English display heavily without seeing them again (the bug it found was in the capture chip). What keeps this open is the dependency, not a defect: if the `play` response's shape changes, English silently stops appearing — worth remembering if it breaks after an unrelated Crunchyroll update. `window.__jpImmersionSnifferStats()` is there for diagnosis.

**Dual-language stripping needs a live pass** (opened 2026-07-26 · Subtitle parsing & display filters) — Built after the second live round and verified only offline, against 27 real Jimaku files (zero Japanese lines lost; the tested `_ja-en.ass` cleaned exactly), and extended 2026-09-18 to rescue Japanese lines in dropped styles. Needs confirming on a real episode that a dual-language provider now shows a clean Japanese line, a clean Anki `Sentence`, and exactly one English line — ours. (The JP/EN timing half of this question was confirmed live on 2026-07-31; see Resolved.)

**Re-verify JP/EN pairing after the sign-cue change** (opened 2026-09-22 · English subtitles) — Pairing was confirmed live on 2026-07-31, but the sign-cue rule will change what `pairEnglishCues` returns. After it's built, re-run checklist E4, D9 and the English half of E6, and check that the 1:1, one-to-two and two-to-one shapes still pair as before — on every show in the sign-cue recapture, on both audio versions where they exist, as well as KonoSuba 3 ep 6.

**Split-sentence audio** (opened 2026-07-26 · Anki export: audio capture) — The card text was confirmed live on 2026-07-27 (a first-half capture produced the whole Japanese sentence and the matching English); the audio failed two live passes and was re-fixed twice (2026-07-27, 2026-07-31). On 2026-07-31 it was unreliable rather than uniformly wrong — sometimes both halves, sometimes only the first (an expiring wait, since made non-blocking and longer), and one second-half capture ran on into the following line, which has two possible causes, only one of them fixed (display windows wider than the sentence); the other would be the merge span over-extending when consecutive lines share an English translation. Checklist B6 (on a `[NanakoRaws]` file) and B7 then passed live on 2026-08-15 — **passed live pre-rework; re-verify on current code**, since that day's audio rework changed the capture path. B6 stays in the group B re-test. If the bleed recurs, record the line and provider — that tells the causes apart.

**Loudness normalization at real volumes** (opened 2026-07-26 · Anki export: audio capture) — Unit-tested only on synthetic tones (identical output RMS from player volume 1.0 down to 0.01, no clipping), and synthetic tones aren't dialogue over a music and effects bed; the 2026-07-31 pass didn't reach it. Needs Eric to capture the same line twice at very different player volumes and confirm both cards play at comparable loudness, and that `AUDIO_TARGET_RMS` (~-21 dBFS) sounds right beside existing Anki cards.

**Subtitle-text filtering, live** (opened 2026-07-31 · Subtitle parsing & display filters) — The 2026-07-31 pass found four annotation types still rendering as dialogue: parenthetical stage directions that aren't the whole line, speaker-attribution notes, doubled `((`/`))` around ordinary dialogue, and full-width punctuation. Punctuation was closed on 2026-08-15 (。 and `{\anN}` tags), and the other three share one root cause — the tests only saw a whole-line parenthetical — fixed offline on 2026-09-18 by running the filters per line (leftover parentheticals across 386 real files fell from 8,411 to 614 lines, the rest real content). Needs checklist F5.

**The edit panel's live failures, re-tested** (opened 2026-07-31 · Anki export: capture chip & edit panel) — The first pass failed the panel in four places, all fixed 2026-07-31 and none re-tested; its audio half was rebuilt on 2026-08-15 and has never been in a browser. Still never exercised on any pass: extending a clip past what was captured, and Anki being closed mid-edit (an error, with pending edits kept). **Unexplained:** a report that changing the word left the original note and created a second one — the panel's only Anki write is `updateNoteFields` on the existing note id, so either something else created it or they were separate captures. Checklist D1–D2 re-checks by counting notes after one capture and one word change.

**The chip's expand-time curve** (opened 2026-07-30 · Anki export: capture chip & edit panel) — Retuned once from live feedback (2026-07-31: a long merged sentence didn't stay readable long enough, with no complaint about single words lingering), so the per-character term and cap rose — 2400ms + 110ms per character, capped at 12s, a few seconds under the chip's 15s lifetime so the collapsed state still appears — while the base barely moved. Needs a second look at both ends; the untried alternative is discrete short/medium/long tiers. One named group of constants in `content.js`.

**Edit-buffer padding length** (opened 2026-07-30 · Anki export: capture chip & edit panel) — `AUDIO_EDIT_PAD_SECONDS` stays at 3 seconds each side. It was reported short twice, both times from a bug rather than the number: on 2026-07-31 the padding wasn't reachable (a fixed top-up timer, replaced by an on-demand re-cut, after which 3s was confirmed to stand), and on 2026-08-15 the right-hand pad never filled because the editor read the buffer once (it now keeps re-reading). Adjust only if extending a clip turns out to hit the 3s limit often in practice.

**The 2026-08-15 audio rework, live** (opened 2026-08-15 · Anki export: audio capture) — Five interacting changes — the sample clock, the paused-video write skip, asymmetric padding with a tail wait, flushing an interrupted capture, and the discontinuity clamp — are verified only against harnesses that drive the clock by hand, not through a real `ScriptProcessorNode`. What a harness can't check is whether a paused media element really stops producing input; if it doesn't, clips will come out short rather than long — safer, but still a failure. Checklist group B.

**Do Crunchyroll's caption files mark background lines with `\an8`?** (opened 2026-08-15 · English subtitles) — The fix that drops an unrelated background English line from `Translation` keys off ASS alignment overrides, the only signal in the file that tells the two apart, but no real Crunchyroll caption file had been inspected for one. The first real file (2026-09-22, the caption fixture) puts KonoSuba 3 ep 6's 26 top-of-screen dialogue lines in a `Konosuba_Main_Top` style with no override tag, so the override-only check sees none of them, and its title card carries `\an3` (bottom); the style's own alignment value wasn't captured. The sign-cue recapture saves `[V4+ Styles]`, which shows a style's own alignment (e.g. `Konosuba_Main_Top`'s). The sign-cue work picks this up; if a stray line survives, dump the caption file (URL from `window.__jpImmersionSnifferStats()`) and compare the two cues.

**The extension-reload banner is a stopgap** (opened 2026-08-15 · Platform & cross-cutting) — A content script could be re-injected into an open tab and everything but audio capture would recover; the blocker is that `createMediaElementSource` is once per `<video>` per page and the dead script still holds it. If a way is ever found to hand that graph over — or to detect an already-tapped video and degrade explicitly rather than silently — the reload prompt could become a genuine hot-swap. Not worth building speculatively; noted so the constraint isn't rediscovered.

**Old-form kanji and Chinese lines in lyric files** (opened 2026-09-18 · Subtitle parsing & display filters) — Kyujitai like 會/當/橫/來 get no dictionary entry (JMdict indexes modern forms), and some Kamigami files carry Chinese translation lines inside a Japanese style. Both are honest misses — nothing wrong is shown — and mostly in song lyrics; worth a variant table or a line-language check only if live use shows them in dialogue (Phase 6).

### Needs a decision

**ひとりも absorbing a character's name** (opened ~2026-07-05 · Tokenizing & grouping) — In ひとりもついに, telling "ひとり the idiom/quantifier" from "ひとり the character's name" needs show-specific knowledge the general phrase-matcher can't safely apply — structurally the same as the accepted kanji-heteronym limitation. A candidate for accepting as permanent, but not formally decided; revisit if more instances turn up.

**Independent JP/EN subtitle visibility** (opened 2026-07-29 · English subtitles) — Many immersion resources recommend watching without English, so letting EN be toggled independently is a plausible Phase 6 addition: JP-only and JP+EN are both worth supporting, and EN-only is explicitly rejected (it would suppress the Japanese overlay and popup, the extension's core purpose). Needs a decision on the mechanism (hold-to-peek vs persistent) and the persistence scope (global default vs per-session) before design starts.

**What Phase 7 settings work is needed** (opened 2026-09-22 · Storage & settings) — "Settings persistence" was a Phase 7 roadmap item, but settings already persist in `chrome.storage.local`: the API key, the four metadata toggles, offsets, and per-season uploader/entry memory. It was removed from the roadmap; what, if anything, Phase 7 needs beyond that (e.g. the settings panel, import/export, a reset) is Eric's call.

**Should the release-offset key move to the season's name?** (opened 2026-09-23 · Subtitle overlay, sync & offset) — Current behaviour: an offset adjustment writes `offset:<pathname>` for the episode and `offsetProvider:<series>:<season>:<releaseTag>` for the release, where `<season>` is Crunchyroll's season number — a list position that different works can share. On 2026-09-18 the remembered entry, the uploader preference and the sibling-title cache moved to the name-based `seasonMemoryKey`; the release-offset key did not. Should it follow them? That change set the precedent of not migrating old keys (Switcher, per-season memory & manual upload), which would apply to saved release offsets too.

**Which sign signal and which tags** (opened 2026-09-23 · English subtitles) — Which candidate defines a sign cue, alone or combined: typesetting override tags, the Actor/Name field, a rare style, or a style whose font differs from the track's most-used style — and, if override tags, which tags count. *Already settled:* `\an`, `\i`, `\b` and `\u` never count as typesetting on their own, and neither does a style's own alignment (the style-level form of `\an`); this moves into the decision entry once the signal is chosen. Open because each candidate has been checked on one episode only; resolved when Eric chooses from the measurement report after labelling (Roadmap → the sign-cue item).

### Resolved

- **JP/EN timing pairing confirmed live** (2026-07-31) → Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-26; the dual-language half stays open above.
- **The URL staleness check's two unmeasured premises** (2026-09-22) — both hold (a dub page's block names its own `/watch/` id; no block in 76 live navigations lacked a URL); F8 passed → Architecture → Show & episode detection.
- **Whether Crunchyroll's JSON-LD carries the episode's own URL or id** (2026-09-20) — yes, `url` and `@id`, and the block is replaced in place → Decisions → Show & episode detection, 2026-09-20.
- **Entry resolution as a whole** (2026-08-13) — closed: group A passed live and offline-clean over 2,526 episodes; it also settled the four undiagnosed 2026-08-04 audit findings, the 2026-07-27 question of whether films/OVAs/specials resolve (and the picker rescues the rest), and whether `resolveTextFiles` needed Crunchyroll's season episode range (it didn't) → Architecture → Jimaku entry resolution; Decisions 2026-08-11/12/13.
- **Films the CMS reports with no episode number** (2026-08-01) — not a real gap; JSON-LD carries `episodeNumber: 0` → Architecture → Show & episode detection.
- **Whether Crunchyroll numbers long franchises absolutely or per season** (2026-08-01) — both, depending on the show, measured across 30 series → Architecture → Show & episode detection.
- **Whether Crunchyroll publishes `partOfSeason.name`** (2026-07-31) — yes, confirmed live; the manual per-show season override isn't needed → Decisions → Jimaku entry resolution, 2026-07-27.
- **`JP_GAP_BRIDGE_SECONDS` at 1.0s** (2026-07-31) — confirmed by eye on real footage; never derivable from the gap distribution → Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-29.
- **English displaying only while Japanese displays** (2026-07-31) — working as designed and not distracting → Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-26.
- **Wrong-season Jimaku entry selection** (2026-07-26) — all four naming conventions, validated on 17 real show/season combinations → Decisions → Jimaku entry resolution, 2026-07-26.
- **How to handle dual-language Jimaku files** (2026-07-26) — Eric's call: strip the embedded track, keep the provider → Decisions → Subtitle parsing & display filters, 2026-07-26.
- **JP/EN timing stagger at offset 0** (2026-07-26) — English paired to the Japanese cue window → Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-26.
- **Crunchyroll's subtitle CDN hostname** (2026-07-26) — `vod-fy-mod.crunchyrollcdn.com`, already allowed → Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-26.
- **Whether `caption-url-sniffer.js` can see the `play` response at all** (2026-07-26) — yes, confirmed live; the Worker/service-worker theory ruled out → Architecture → English subtitles.
- **Anki Translation field appearing on the card front** (2026-07-26) — a dual-language `[SubsPlease]` file putting English into `Sentence`; the template was always back-only → Architecture → Anki export: cards & capture.
- **Whether the audio field should be empty when Crunchyroll is muted** (2026-07-26) — yes, intentional (no `[sound:…]`, no play button) → Decisions → Anki export: audio capture, 2026-07-26.
- **Show/episode toggle live-tested** (2026-07-26) — working, with the other three "Extra info" toggles → Architecture → Anki export: cards & capture.
- **JP/EN subtitle desync** (2026-07-26) — the English update sat behind the Japanese half's early return, not the offset → Architecture → English subtitles.
- **JP/EN vertical ordering** (2026-07-26) — one flex stack; Japanese-on-top is fixed intent, not configurable → Decisions → Subtitle overlay, sync & offset, 2026-07-26.
- **Whether a fully silent capture should be exported** (2026-07-26) — no → Decisions → Anki export: audio capture, 2026-07-26.
- **English subtitle data source** (2026-07-23) — Crunchyroll's own captions (option c), not Jimaku → Decisions → English subtitles, 2026-07-23.
- **How Crunchyroll exposes captions in the page** (2026-07-23) — not in the DOM at all; a signed `.ass` URL in the `play` response → Architecture → English subtitles.
- **Settings panel scheduling** (2026-07-23) — Phase 6 (Eric's call), priority within it undecided → Decisions → Process & roadmap, 2026-07-23.
- **Phase 5/6 build order** (2026-07-23) → Decisions → Process & roadmap, 2026-07-23.
- **JLPT tag live-tested** (2026-07-22) — badges, toggle and Anki field correct on a real page → Architecture → Dictionary data & lookup.
- **JLPT data source** (2026-07-22) → Decisions → Dictionary data & lookup, 2026-07-22.

## How to use this doc

### The split

This document holds **all project state** — what the project is, where it stands, how each part works, what was decided and what's still open — and it changes every session. `CLAUDE.md` holds **working rules only** (how to work in this repo: the guardrail, working style, engineering rules, repo map, commands, session protocol) and must never receive project state such as phase, priorities, feature status or recaps. The one intentional overlap is the condensed core goal and the litmus test in `CLAUDE.md`; this document holds the canonical versions (Product → Core goal; Scope → Litmus test). **If the core goal or the litmus test changes here, update `CLAUDE.md` in the same edit.** Step-by-step browser checks live in `docs/live-test-checklist.md`.

### Where new information goes

| If you… | it goes in… | in this shape |
|---|---|---|
| finished a task | Roadmap → current phase → **Done** (remove it from Remaining); if it changed how something works, also the component's *How it works now* | one line: what shipped + date |
| found a new task | Roadmap → current phase → **Remaining** (priority order), or a future phase | one sentence saying what to do; say if it's gated or deprioritized |
| built something not yet tested in a browser | Roadmap → **Built, not verified live**; the steps in `docs/live-test-checklist.md`; Status if it matters now | one line per item, with its checklist id |
| changed how something works | Architecture → the component's **How it works now** | rewrite to the new current state — never append a second description |
| discovered a constraint, limitation, or fact with no alternative | Architecture → the component's **Known limitations & gotchas** | `- YYYY-MM-DD — finding` |
| found a rule a component must never break | Architecture → that component's **Invariants** | one line stating the rule |
| chose between real alternatives | Decisions → the component's group (or Product & scope / Process & roadmap), in date order | the Decision template |
| revised an earlier decision | the original Decisions entry | retitle to the final state; add `*Revised YYYY-MM-DD:* what changed and why` — keep every revision's reason |
| opened a question | Open questions → **Needs testing or real-world feedback** or **Needs a decision** (test below) | the Open question template |
| resolved a question | Open questions → **Resolved** | one line: name, date, pointer to the Decision or Architecture entry |
| changed scope | Scope (lists, checkboxes, reason + date) and Decisions → Product & scope; if the litmus test or core goal changed, `CLAUDE.md` too | one line per Scope item; a Decision entry |
| learned something about a competitor | Competitive landscape → the item's facts; our status in its *Where we stand* line | facts and status kept apart |
| changed the top priority | Status → **Next up** and Roadmap → Remaining order | 2–4 items, one sentence each |
| closed a phase | Roadmap (collapse the phase to 1–3 lines) after moving every finding, number and gotcha from its Done list into the components; Status → Phase; Decisions → Process & roadmap if it was a judgment call | closed date, what it delivered, pointer to the components |
| needed a new component | a new heading in **both** Architecture and Decisions, plus the Contents list | the Architecture component template |

### Entry templates

Architecture component:

```
### <Component>
**What it does:** one sentence.
**Key files:** `file.js`, …
**Invariants:** rules this component must never break. Omit if none.
**How it works now:**
- bullets, current state only
**Known limitations & gotchas:**
- YYYY-MM-DD — finding
**See also:** other components holding part of the same feature (only if needed).
**Decisions:** see Decisions → <Component>
```

Decision:

```
**YYYY-MM-DD — Short title, ≤12 words.**
What was chosen and why, in 1–4 sentences. *Rejected:* each real alternative and why.
*Revised YYYY-MM-DD:* what changed and why (one per revision, if any).
```

(This replaces the old one-line format, `**Date — Short bolded title.** Reasoning/rejected-alternative in plain text after.`, retired 2026-09-22.)

Open question:

```
**Short name** (opened YYYY-MM-DD · <Component>) — The question. Why it's open. What would resolve it. (2–4 sentences)
```

Resolved open question:

```
- **Short name** (YYYY-MM-DD) — optional few-word outcome → pointer to the Decision or Architecture entry.
```

### Formatting rules

- **Decision titles are short labels (under ~12 words),** scannable in a list of 50 — never a recap. Bad: `**2026-07-03 — After investigating whether kuromoji's reading field could be used to disambiguate 一行 in context, we determined it could not because of a segmentation quirk and inconsistent behavior on other heteronyms, so we logged it as an accepted limitation.**` Good: `**2026-07-03 — Kanji-heteronym sense-context disambiguation (一行) rejected as an anchor-to-kuromoji-reading fix.**` Detail goes in the body.
- **Only genuine decisions go in Decisions.** The test: "what did you choose this over?" If the honest answer is "there was nothing else to do" — confirming an API contract, discovering a platform limitation, verifying a fact — it's a finding: put it in the component's gotchas, dated.
- **Revise, don't duplicate.** When a decision changes, update the original entry to show the final state, keeping a short dated reason for each revision. Never let one decision appear twice under two titles, and never collapse a chain down to only its end state.
- **Architecture is organized by component,** not by build phase. Put a new fact in the component it describes; where a feature spans components, each part gets a *See also* line.
- **Architecture describes current state only.** Replace an outdated description; never append a second one alongside it. History and reasoning live in Decisions.
- **Relocate, don't delete.** When you shorten anything — a Done line, a collapsed phase, a Resolved entry — the detail must already exist somewhere else in this doc.
- **Roadmap Done bullets are one line each:** what shipped and when. The explanation belongs in Architecture (what changed) and/or Decisions (what was chosen).
- **Keep Status short.** Fixed fields, 2–4 Next-up items. If it's growing, the detail belongs in Roadmap.
- **Open questions are 2–4 sentences:** the question, why it's open, what would resolve it. A concluded investigation is a Decision; a standing limitation is an Architecture gotcha.
- **Testing vs decision:** would a test, corpus result or usage data settle it on its own (→ *Needs testing or real-world feedback*), or does a person have to weigh options and choose (→ *Needs a decision*)? If neither — the reasoning is done and accepted — it isn't open at all.
- **Resolved entries are pointers.** Before adding one, check it isn't already fully covered by a Decision or Architecture entry; restate nothing beyond a few words.
- **Never duplicate a fact across sections.** Current state goes in Architecture, reasoning and rejected alternatives in Decisions; each references the other only if needed.
- **Reference items by name, never by number** — lists renumber when items resolve, and a numbered reference goes stale silently.
- An inferred date is written `~YYYY-MM-DD`.
- **Update `_Last updated:_`** at the top whenever you edit this file.

### End-of-session checklist

1. **Roadmap → current phase:** move finished items from Remaining to Done as one-line bullets; add newly found remaining items in priority order; update *Built, not verified live*, and add or refresh the steps in `docs/live-test-checklist.md`.
2. **Decisions:** scan the session for genuine decisions (the "what did you choose this over?" test). Add each with a short bolded title, dated, under the right group, in date order. If one revises an earlier entry, update that entry with a dated *Revised* note instead of adding a duplicate.
3. **Open questions:** move anything resolved to Resolved (one line, pointing to the entry that resolved it — don't restate its reasoning); add new ones to the right subsection (testing vs decision), 2–4 sentences, with an opened date and component.
4. **Architecture:** if something now works differently, rewrite that component's *How it works now* to the new current state (don't leave the old description beside it); add new findings to gotchas and new rules to Invariants.
5. **Scope and Competitive landscape:** check for drift — a feature moving between core loop and differentiation, a checkbox that no longer matches what's built, new competitor facts, stale *Where we stand* lines — and fix them in place.
6. **Status:** refresh Phase, Next up, Built but not yet verified, and Blocked. Keep it short.
7. Update `_Last updated:_`.
8. **`CLAUDE.md`:** edit only if a working rule, command or key file changed, or if the core goal or litmus test changed here (then update both). Never add project state to it.
9. **Tell Eric** in the end-of-session summary: what changed, what was validated live vs offline only, what's still unverified, and any ambiguities you flagged or had to decide. **If nothing decision-worthy happened, say so explicitly in the chat summary rather than skipping the check silently** — this doc then needs no Decisions edit.
